# Interp × energy: novelty and physics checks, verbatim

Companion to `interp-weight-edit-energy.md` (same directory). Each of the 18 seat ideas was attacked once by a Fable 5.1 checker and once by an Opus 5.5 checker, blind to each other, on 2026-11-07. Ideas 1-9 come from seat 1 (Fable), ideas 10-18 from seat 2 (Opus); their full text is in Appendix A of the design study. Every field below is the checker's return value, unedited. Sources a checker marks OPENED were opened by that checker; the lead re-opened only those marked [L] in the design study. Some checkers ran out of web-search budget and report sources as not found that others opened; where checkers disagree with each other or with a source the lead opened (for example the Astra reference and the UniGuard citation), section 5 of the design study records the resolution.

## 1. LOCKIN-SWAP: lock-in weight-swap bound on the switching channel of surgical edits (seat 1 (Fable))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- OPENED (primary): Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming', 2026-11-02, https://rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/ — Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights' reference exists. GPT-6 Astra edited 131,072 of Qwen3.5-0.8B's weights (0.016%): subtracting one direction from 64 output columns of the MLPs in layers 15 and 19. MATH-500 7.9% -> 48.1% at 50 passes; 'hit token limit' fell 93.0% -> 33.1%. Gemma 3 270M (rarely loops) did not improve. NO energy, power or inference-cost measurement anywhere in the post. The gain is behavioural (loop removal -> fewer tokens), i.e. exactly the channel LOCKIN-SWAP excludes by teacher-forcing.
- OPENED: Taneja, Kim, Xu, van Schaik, Genkin, Yarom, 'Hot Pixels' (USENIX Sec 2023, arXiv 2305.12784, read via ar5iv) — closest prior art for the physics. On Apple M1 MacBook Air/Pro/Mini iGPU, software sensors (IOReport via SocPowerBuddy, ~10 ms) show operand-Hamming-weight- and Hamming-distance-dependent GPU power and frequency (correlations about -0.96 for both). The only quoted absolute range is for an RX 6600: a full Hamming-weight sweep of all operands in a compute-bound loop moved power 97.5-98.1 W (about 0.6%). So the switching channel exists on Apple GPUs and is software-visible, but at the sub-percent level even for an all-operands, max-contrast sweep. It did NOT measure LLM weight edits or use a swap/lock-in design.
- OPENED: Kraken (arXiv 2603.02891, HTML v1) — GPU weight extraction via EM/CPA on Jetson Orin Nano (20 GS/s scope) and RTX 4090 far-field (2 MHz SDR); leakage model is Hamming distance of register overwrites by partial sums; ~1e5-2e6 traces per weight; Llama 3.2 1B partial W_q/W_k/W_v recovery. Confirms weight-value-dependent switching power is real but recoverable only with MHz-GHz probes and correlation over 1e5+ traces, not from a 10 Hz rail.
- OPENED (abstract only): BarraCUDA, arXiv 2312.07783 — correlation-EM weight recovery on Jetson Nano; no magnitude stated.
- OPENED (abstract only): Jeon, Karimian, Lehman, 'A New Foe in GPUs: Power Side-Channel Attacks on Neural Network', ISQED 2021, https://scholarworks.sjsu.edu/faculty_rsca/2766 — power profiling on shared GPU recovers 'a few core parameters'; no weight-value power magnitude stated.
- OPENED: hertzbleed.com (Wang et al., USENIX Sec 2022; follow-up IEEE S&P 2023) — data-dependent power becomes data-dependent DVFS frequency; follow-up notes GPU computations shift frequency (cites Hot Pixels). Relevance: a tiny effect could surface as a frequency rather than power change; the proposal's per-block frequency log is the right guard.
- OPENED (abstract + project page): ROME, Meng et al., arXiv 2202.05262 / rome.baulab.info — rank-one change to one mid-layer MLP key->value matrix. No energy/inference-cost discussion. OPENED (abstract): MEMIT, arXiv 2210.07229 — many memories across several layers; no energy discussion. OPENED (abstract): Arditi et al., 'Refusal ... Single Direction', arXiv 2406.11717 — directional ablation; weight-orthogonalisation detail is in the full paper, not on the abstract page; no energy discussion.
- Not found: any paper measuring rail/wall energy of ROME-, MEMIT- or abliteration-edited models, or any software-sensor lock-in/weight-swap design. Caveat: only the first of my five web searches ran (the session's shared WebSearch budget was exhausted); the targeted searches for 'energy of knowledge editing', 'data-dependent GPU matmul power', 'zero operands dense kernel watts' and 'lock-in software energy measurement' did not execute. UNVERIFIED (not opened): Maia et al. 2021 magnetic side channel on GPU power cable (arXiv 2109.07395, from a search snippet); Eyeriss-style zero-gating accelerator results (from memory, no URL opened).
- In-repo: /Users/edr/code/CapstoneProject/jw-wt/handoff/docs/process_traces/2026-10-31-magistrate-5cbfbcfc/KB-SLOT-FILL.md — GPU-rail δΔ = 1.047e-4 J/GB at 106.5 GB/s gives 11.15 mW; one R call = 12.65 min, two per arm = 25.3 min. The idea's floor derivation checks out arithmetically. Note that δΔ there was earned with ~12.6-min contiguous calls in R,C,C,R order at ~100% GPU active residency, GPU frequency matched to 0.08 MHz, thermal level 0 throughout; it is a floor for that regime, not for 2-4 s chopping. No existing JouleWise doc mentions ROME/MEMIT/abliteration or weight edits (grep of docs/research_question_bank.md and docs/research_question_registry.md returned nothing). The paper's rectangular-pulse fiducial fit on gpu_power (docs/paper/draft-v1.md:47, :425-448) is the in-house precedent for commanded-square-wave demodulation of the GPU rail.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1) The null is essentially guaranteed from first principles, so a 10 mW bound is 2-4 orders of magnitude looser than the expected effect and tells us little about mechanism. Scaling (share of streamed bytes touched) x (data-dependent fraction of GPU energy; Hot Pixels: ~0.6% for a full Hamming-weight sweep of ALL operands, allow up to 5% for DRAM/fabric bus toggling) x (~10 W GPU rail) gives the MAXIMUM effect if every touched bit flipped: Astra-style 0.016% edit 0.01-0.08 mW; ROME single down_proj (0.70% of bytes in a 7B) 0.4-3.5 mW; abliteration (o_proj+down_proj, 30.6% of bytes) 19-155 mW. But none of these edits flips all bits: ROME/MEMIT/abliteration/Astra perturb values by small amounts, leaving mantissa bit statistics and mean Hamming weight/distance unchanged, so the realistic expectation is another 1-2 orders below those maxima (sub-0.1 mW even for abliteration). The '<1 mW' estimate is right; the 10 mW bound will be met trivially. 2) The positive control as specified ('a 50% zeroed matrix', expected 0.1-1 W) is itself likely sub-floor. Zeroing 50% of ONE down_proj changes neither bytes streamed nor FLOPs on an Apple GPU (no zero-skipping in dense MLX matmul), only toggle statistics of 0.7% of the traffic: expected roughly 1-10 mW, i.e. at or below the 11 mW floor. 0.1-1 W is only plausible if ~all matrices are zeroed (and even then Hot Pixels' ~0.6% suggests ~60 mW at 10 W unless DRAM toggling dominates). Fix: make the positive control graded and whole-model (random vs 50%-zero vs all-zero weights, same token stream), which also yields a genuinely new number: the operand-switching share of decode energy on Apple silicon, measured with powermetrics. That coefficient is arguably the real deliverable; the edit bound then follows by multiplying it by the edit's byte share. 3) The 11 mW floor transfers imperfectly: it was earned with 12.6-min contiguous calls; 2-4 s blocks add a swap transient at every edge (array rebind, possible mx.compile retrace if weights are captured as constants, Metal buffer re-encode, first-touch cache/TLB misses on the other copy). Transients are symmetric across phases so they cancel in square-wave demodulation and the A-A' arm bounds them, but they cost edge samples (one 100 ms sample per edge ~5% at 2 s blocks) and add variance; a stage-0 pilot must re-measure the floor at this chop rate rather than inherit it. Also verify that the swap actually changes the kernel's weight pointer and does not trigger a per-block graph retrace; log per-block CPU rail and token latency to catch this. 4) powermetrics gpu_power at 100 ms: the field has sub-mW float precision (draft-v1.md:438 example 8.98937 mW) while gpu_energy is integer mJ; averaging over 25 min dithers any 10 mW/100 ms quantization, and the KB result already resolved 0.0001 J/GB on this rail, so quantization is not the limiter, but the underlying SMC counter resolution is unverified; Hot Pixels' IOReport path at 10 ms is a possible finer-grained alternative. 5) DVFS: Hot Pixels shows the M1 GPU converts data-dependent power into frequency changes, mainly in thermally/power-limited steady states. Keep thermal level 0 and GPU frequency matched (as in KB-SLOT-FILL, 0.08 MHz) and treat any per-block frequency difference as a confound, not a signal. 6) Memory: both copies resident is cheap for ROME (90 MB) but for abliteration on a 7B it is +4 GB (two copies of 31% of weights); fine on the MBP, constrains stage-0 model size on the Air. 7) Framing: because teacher-forcing removes the behavioural channel, the experiment measures only the switching channel, which Astra's own numbers (token-limit hits 93% -> 33%) show is dwarfed by the behavioural channel (orders of magnitude in J/task). The bound is useful only as the 'switching term is negligible' anchor of a decomposition; pair it with a free-decode arm (edited vs unedited, J/task and tokens/task) or it answers a question nobody is asking.

**Residual novelty**

Not refuted: no prior work measures the energy of ROME/MEMIT/abliteration-edited (or Astra-edited) models at any level, and no one has used an in-process weight-reference swap with a same-weights placement control and square-wave demodulation of a software power rail. Hot Pixels establishes that the operand-switching channel exists and is software-visible on Apple GPUs; Kraken/BarraCUDA show weight-value leakage needs GHz probes and 1e5+ traces. What remains new and worth doing: (a) the operand-switching coefficient of LLM decode on Apple silicon (whole-model graded zero/random control) with powermetrics, a number nobody has published; (b) the resulting edit-class bound, stated as a fraction of decode energy (<0.1% expected) and multiplied by each edit's byte footprint (0.016% Astra, 0.7% ROME, ~3% MEMIT, ~31% abliteration); (c) the decomposition claim that any energy effect of surgical edits is behavioural (token count, loop removal), using the Astra result as the motivating case. Rank: bank it behind the behavioural-channel experiment; the switching bound alone is a near-certain null whose value is as a one-paragraph anchor, not a headline. Suggested home: docs/research_question_bank.md in the handoff clone, as a candidate row with the floor derivation cited to KB-SLOT-FILL.md:22,28 and the positive-control correction above.

### Opus 5.5 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming: LLMs can natively read & write neural network weights', blog, 2026-11-02, https://rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/ (OPENED). This is the source behind Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights'. Astra edited 131,072 weights (0.016%) of Qwen3.5-0.8B: it subtracted one direction from 64 output columns of the MLP in layers 15 and 19. MATH-500 rose from 7.9% to 48.1% at 50 forward passes. The page attributes the gain to removing reasoning loops: the share of answers hitting the 32,768-token cap fell from 93% to 33%. It never mentions power or energy. Implication: this edit's energy effect would run almost entirely through output length, and the switching channel LOCKIN-SWAP targets would be tiny.
- Coverage of the same work: letsdatascience.com on Simon Willison's laptop replication (OPENED), https://letsdatascience.com/news/willison-replicates-mit-weight-reading-test-on-laptop-with-q-3488f9da. Search snippets only, not opened (UNVERIFIED): buildmvpfast.com (critique that it was a loop fix), zeli.app (failed bash-variant reproduction), yourstory, fourweekmba. None measure power.
- Gregersen, Patel, Choukse, 'Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs', arXiv:2409.18324, 2024 (OPENED: abstract plus ar5iv full text). Holding GEMM shape fixed and changing only the values changes A100/H100/V100 power by up to about 38-40%. Sampling was DCGM at 100 ms. Power depended on distribution-level properties: sparsity, bit similarity, LSB/MSB randomization, sorting/placement, and the mean (standard deviation did not matter). Kernels were compute-bound CUTLASS GEMMs on NVIDIA, and memory-bound workloads were not studied. This is the physics prior that value changes can move power. It also predicts that edits which keep value and bit statistics (ROME, MEMIT, abliteration, Astra column edits) should move power negligibly.
- Taneja et al., 'Hot Pixels: Frequency, Power, and Temperature Attacks on GPUs and Arm SoCs', USENIX Security 2023, arXiv:2305.12784 (OPENED via ar5iv, first ~100k characters). On the M1 Air iGPU, steady-state GPU power and frequency track the operands' Hamming weight (r of about -0.96), read through unprivileged IOReport. No mW magnitude is given. This shows data-dependent power is visible on Apple-silicon telemetry.
- te Lintelo, Koffas, Picek, 'SkipSponge/SpongeNet: Sponge Weight Poisoning of Deep Neural Networks', arXiv:2402.06357 (OPENED). Small weight and bias edits raise energy by up to about 13%. That figure comes from a simulated zero-skipping ASIC (counted operations and DRAM accesses), not measured power, so it does not carry over to dense Apple GPUs.
- Cina et al., 'Energy-Latency Attacks via Sponge Poisoning', arXiv:2203.08147 (search snippets only, UNVERIFIED). Training-time weight poisoning for sparsity-aware accelerators, with simulated energy.
- MobiEdit, arXiv:2506.13772 (search snippet only, UNVERIFIED). It reports the energy cost of performing a ROME edit on device, not the inference power of the edited model.
- Repo check: /usr/bin/grep of the handoff docs for lock-in, abliteration, MEMIT or weight swap found no earlier JouleWise proposal. The noise anchor is docs/process_traces/2026-10-31-magistrate-5cbfbcfc/KB-SLOT-FILL.md:21 (GPU rail 0.00879 +/- 0.00010 J/GB) and :28 (32 MiB rung).

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1) The positive control is probably overestimated by 1-2 orders of magnitude. Decode is a memory-bound GEMV, so zeroing half of one matrix leaves bytes moved unchanged and alters only bit toggles in that matrix's share of traffic. For a 0.8B bf16 model, one matrix is roughly 0.3-1% of bytes per token. At an estimated ~50 tok/s x 1.6 GB/token (~80 GB/s) and the measured GPU-rail traffic cost of 0.0088 J/GB (KB-SLOT-FILL.md:21), that matrix's whole GPU-rail traffic energy is about 2-7 mW [estimate]. Only a fraction of that depends on the data, and ALU toggling is small in a memory-bound regime. The expected control signal is about 1-5 mW, not 0.1-1 W. Fix: run a graded dose series that zeros a fraction f in {0.5, 0.1, 0.01} of all MLP weights in every layer (about two-thirds of the bytes). That gives a sensitivity in mW per (byte fraction x bit-change fraction), and the edit's bound can then be scaled from it. Note that most traffic energy lands off the GPU rail: the DC contrast is 0.039 J/GB against 0.0088 J/GB on the GPU rail (lines 21-22), roughly 4x more off-rail than on it. Bit-toggle savings in the DRAM interface will largely miss the rail being demodulated.

2) The bound as stated does not follow from the floor. A ~11 mW standard error per arm (my check: 0.000105 J/GB x ~80,300 GB per call / ~890 s gives ~9.5 mW, consistent) yields a 95% upper bound of about 20-25 mW, not 10 mW. Either restate the target or show in stage 0 that the lock-in beats the anchor. It plausibly can. The 11 mW comes from a 4-call R-C-C-R design dominated by drift, and demodulating at 0.1-0.25 Hz rejects thermal and DVFS drift. With 100 ms energy-counter samples, if white noise is tens of mW per sample, 25 min gives a sub-mW to few-mW standard error [estimate]. Decode, however, is much burstier than the streaming reduce in the anchor (per-token CPU dispatch, GPU idle gaps, power-state transitions), so measure the noise spectrum at the lock-in frequency on the A-A' arm first, on the MBP rather than the fanless Air, whose thermal throttling makes it fit for software only.

3) The null is nearly certain before any measurement, which caps the information gained. Gregersen et al. find power tracks distribution-level statistics (sparsity, bit similarity, sorting, mean). ROME/MEMIT rank-one updates, abliteration projections and Astra's 64-column subtraction (0.016% of weights) all leave those statistics essentially unchanged and perturb mostly low mantissa bits. Expected switching effect: well under 1 mW, consistent with the proposal's own estimate. The result is a calibration statement (edits change J/token by less than about 0.1-0.3% at fixed tokens, assuming a GPU-rail decode power of roughly 5-15 W), not a discovery. Abliteration touches about half the model's bytes, so it is the best case for any nonzero effect and should be the primary arm.

4) Use a pseudo-random swap sequence (an m-sequence, or randomized ABBA block lengths), not a fixed square wave. macOS periodic daemons, powermetrics' own sampling and GPU-governor loops at seconds timescales can produce lines near 0.1-0.25 Hz. A randomized reference also guards against aliasing from 100 ms sample-boundary jitter. Drop the samples that straddle each swap boundary.

5) Swap artifacts in MLX. The edited copy must match the original exactly in dtype, shape, strides, contiguity and quantization group layout, so the same Metal kernels run. A 4-bit MLX model needs requantization, which changes scales and biases. Avoid mx.compile caching, or verify that no recompilation happens on swap. Force eval sync at block boundaries. A-A' controls placement but not a first-touch transient after each swap; log per-token latency per block and treat a latency difference as a confound.

6) The DC meter cannot carry this result. Its contrast resolution (±0.0072 J/GB) is about 70x worse than the GPU rail's (±0.0001 J/GB). It is fine as a timing cross-check (the swap log is better), and a DC arm is only valid with the battery full or charging inhibited.

7) Frequency compensation. If the GPU governor lowers frequency under slightly lower load, power and time per token shift together. Report energy per token and the frequency distribution per arm, not mean rail power alone.

8) The Astra reference is real but secondary to measurement: blog-only (2026-11-02), with one public critique and a reportedly failed variant reproduction (UNVERIFIED snippets). Its performance gain was a loop fix, so pairing LOCKIN-SWAP with a teacher-free length-channel run (token counts x the existing J/token) is where the power-and-performance intersection Ed asked about actually shows up.

Feasibility overall: stage 0 software on the Air is fine. Stage 1 (20-40 min of MBP time per model) is plausible for a ~10-25 mW bound, but only if the stage-0 noise spectrum and the graded positive control show the instrument can see a few mW. As specified, the 0.1-1 W control would mislead the sensitivity calibration.

**Residual novelty**

Novel enough, not refuted. No source opened or found measures inference power or energy of a surgically edited LLM (ROME, MEMIT, abliteration or Astra-style) against its unedited twin at a fixed token stream, and none uses an in-process weight-swap lock-in on Apple-silicon rails. The nearest work, Gregersen et al. 2024, shows value-dependent GEMM power on NVIDIA under distribution-level manipulations. It never touches model edits, decode, memory-bound regimes or Apple silicon. Two things remain new. (a) A clean decomposition: an edit's energy effect = (change in token count) x J/token plus a switching term, with a published upper bound on the switching term. This is the measurable part of Ed's interest in how surgical edits change power and performance. (b) The method, a randomized in-process A/B weight swap demodulated against the rail trace, as a general instrument for same-work, different-values power contrasts. It addresses Ed's worry that power is hard to separate from background machine noise. The scientific payoff is a near-certain null, though. The more interesting result is the length channel: Astra's loop fix cut the share of answers hitting the 32k token cap from 93% to 33%, which is a large energy effect measurable from token counts plus the existing J/token calibration. LOCKIN-SWAP is best framed as the supporting control that licenses 'edit energy = length-channel energy', not as a stand-alone finding.

---

## 2. ABLATION-ARTIFACT: the physical energy cost of zero- vs mean- vs resample-ablation (seat 1 (Fable))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- OPENED. Bhalachandra, Austin, Williams, Wright, 'Understanding the Impact of Input Entropy on FPU, CPU, and GPU Power', arXiv:2212.08805 (Dec 2022; ar5iv full text). FP64 DGEMM on A100: constant-valued inputs ~239 W vs random ~398 W (+67%); 64-core AMD Milan CPU 158 W vs 188 W (+19%); power rises with how often adjacent rows/columns differ; sparse patterns reach TDP once >~6% of the matrix is random; attributed to cycle-to-cycle bit flips in FMA datapaths (~8 pJ/FLOP upper bound on the data-dependent part); random inputs also ran 4% slower on the A100. All-zero matrices NOT measured. This is the mechanism the idea relies on, established on NVIDIA/AMD, not Apple. It shows CONSTANT (not only zero) operands collapse datapath power, which undercuts the idea's premise that mean-ablation is energy-neutral.
- OPENED. Gregersen, Patel, Choukse, 'Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs', arXiv:2409.18324 (Sep 2024; ar5iv full text). A100 (plus H100, V100, RTX 6000 spot checks), 2048x2048 GEMM, FP32/FP16/FP16-tensor-core/INT8, DCGM at 100 ms: input patterns move GEMM power by up to ~38%. Few unique values, high bit similarity, sorting, and higher sparsity all lower power; FP16 tensor-core path is the most data-sensitive. Bit-flip hypothesis stated but untested; no all-zero measurement; no mixed random-x-sparse operand case; no transformer/LLM run; no Apple silicon.
- OPENED (secondary coverage). The Register, 'Forget retraining - AI can now tweak another AI's weights', 3 Nov 2026 (theregister.com/a/5307105), and letsdatascience.com Willison-replication article (3 Nov 2026). Ed's reference IS findable: Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming' blog post, 2 Nov 2026 (primary URL given as rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/, NOT opened - UNVERIFIED). GPT-6 Astra edited ~0.016% (~131k) of Qwen3.5-0.8B's weights with no gradient descent; MATH-500 7.9% -> 48.1%, ~3x IFEval; authors and critics (Honnibal, per secondary coverage; buildmvpfast.com rebuttal page returned 403, UNVERIFIED) attribute most of the gain to removing a reasoning-loop direction, i.e. the edit changes generation length/behavior, not per-token compute. No energy or power measurement anywhere in this work.
- UNVERIFIED (search snippets only, not opened). Pochinkov et al., 'Investigating Neuron Ablation in Attention Heads: The Case for Peak Activation Centering', arXiv:2408.17322 (2024): compares zero, mean, resample and peak ablation on model performance only; no energy. Michel, Levy, Neubig 2019 'Are Sixteen Heads Really Better than One?' (zero-ablation of BERT heads; accuracy/speed, no energy). Forum thread on 'Transformer circuit faithfulness metrics are not robust' discusses mean vs resample ablation cost as equal computation; no measurement.
- UNVERIFIED (search snippets only). SpAtten (HPCA 2021, arXiv:2012.09852) and Energon (arXiv:2110.09310): head/token pruning energy savings on custom accelerators via skipped DRAM fetches, i.e. structural removal, not in-place masking on a GPU. Not the same question.
- UNVERIFIED (search snippets only). Apple Developer Forums thread 819927 (M4 Max): powermetrics GPU power rose 65 W while system DC rose 179 W under heavy GPU load, suggesting the gpu_power figure is a partial model-based estimate. Consistent with JouleWise's own BYTES result (docs/process_traces/2026-10-31-magistrate-5cbfbcfc/KB-SLOT-FILL.md: GPU rail 0.0088 J/GB vs DC 0.039 J/GB). Relevant because a counter-based estimator may not see bit-toggle power at all.
- Power side-channel literature (search snippets, UNVERIFIED): ReLU branch leakage via RAPL, MACPruning side channels, 'Open DNN Box' (arXiv:1907.10406). Shows per-neuron activity is power-observable in principle on CPUs/FPGAs, but nobody uses energy as an interpretability importance score, and nobody has compared ablation styles by energy. No prior work substantially answers the claim; refuted=false.
- Repo check: /Users/edr/code/CapstoneProject/jw-wt/handoff has no LOCKIN-SWAP, ABLATION, interp, or data-dependent-power material (grep of docs/, CLAIMS_STATUS.md, research_question_bank.md, research_question_registry.md); LOCKIN-SWAP is a sibling proposal in this batch, not existing infrastructure. Note: WebSearch budget for the session was exhausted after 6 queries; Eyeriss zero-gating, MIT blog primary, and Apple GPU data-dependent-power follow-ups were not searched.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1. MECHANISM IS REAL BUT THE PREDICTED ORDERING IS WRONG. Bhalachandra 2022 shows constant-valued operands cut A100 DGEMM power by ~40% of the random-input value, nearly as much as sparsity does. Mean-ablation feeds an identical d_head vector at every token position, so along the token (M) dimension of the W_O GEMM it is a 'fixed input' in Bhalachandra's sense; zero-ablation additionally removes toggles along the reduction (k) dimension and may trigger any operand-zero gating Apple's FMA units have (undocumented). Resample ablation preserves natural statistics. Honest prediction: dJ(zero) <= dJ(mean) < dJ(resample) ~ 0, with the mean-vs-zero gap set by whether the kernel's cycle-to-cycle operand stream runs over tokens or over k (MLX steel GEMM tiling decides this). Rewrite the claim as a three-level ordering, not 'mean and resample do not'. 2. MAGNITUDE OF THE DIRECT TERM IS ~10x SMALLER THAN STATED. Zero-ablating one head's output changes operand values in a (d_head x d_model) slice of W_O only, i.e. ~1/(12 n_heads) of one layer's matmul work, ~1/(12 n_heads L) of the model's: ~8e-5 for a 32x32 model. Taking the NVIDIA ~40% data-dependent datapath share as an upper bound, the direct effect is ~3e-5 of GPU matmul power: ~0.5 mW at a 15 W prefill draw, not 1-10 mW. Decode is weight-read bound on Apple silicon (the BYTES J/GB result) and weights are unchanged by activation ablation, so decode shows even less; use teacher-forced prefill-style members only. Per-layer zeroing (all heads) is ~15-50 mW, not 0.1-1 W. QK^T, softmax and V matmuls of the ablated head still run unless the graph is changed, so keep the op graph byte-identical across styles (out = out*(1-m) + m*v with v = 0, mean tensor, or resampled tensor; null quad uses v = own output). 3. THE 'INDEPENDENT OF FUNCTIONAL IMPORTANCE' CLAIM IS CONFOUNDED BY THE DOWNSTREAM TERM. Any ablation changes the residual stream in all later layers, so bit-toggle statistics change in (L-l)/L of the network, a term far larger in scope than the direct slice and correlated with functional importance (important heads move downstream activations more). Its sign is unknown. To isolate the direct term, add a 'patched-downstream' arm: run clean, cache the layer-(l+1) residual, then run the ablated pass with the residual patched back to clean (patch op present in every arm; null arm patches its own value). Direct = ablated+patched minus null; downstream = ablated minus ablated+patched. Without this the correlation-with-importance result is uninterpretable. 4. THE GPU RAIL MAY BE BLIND TO THE EFFECT BY CONSTRUCTION. powermetrics gpu_power is an estimate; if it is driven by activity counters times a V/f model it cannot see Hamming-distance-dependent switching, and the 0.0001 J/GB GPU-rail resolution is irrelevant. DC input (physical current) must be primary; the GPU rail becomes a secondary result ('does Apple's estimator include data-dependent power?'), which is itself a publishable side finding. DVFS: a real power drop may change GPU active frequency; log and compare per-arm frequency as BYTES does (1379.6 MHz both arms). 5. NOISE BUDGET vs COST. Anchors: replays repeat to ~2 J on 2-4 kJ (~5e-4 to 1e-3 relative); BYTES DC half-width 0.0072 J/GB x 32 GiB ~ 0.23 J over ~1 h ~ 65 uW-equivalent lock-in resolution. A 0.5 mW direct per-head effect over 10 s members is ~5 mJ, far under the ~25-100 mJ repeat noise of a 50 J member, so single-quad per-head detection is out; ~1 h of ABBA/lock-in per head x style gives ~8 sigma on DC, i.e. 16 heads x 3 styles ~ 48 Mac-hours (~5 Mac-nights), not 0.3. Per-layer zeroing (~15-50 mW) is resolvable in ~5 min per cell on DC: 32 layers x 3 styles ~ 8 h ~ 1 Mac-night. The idea's cost estimate is off by 3-15x. Prefill members also run hotter and less steady than the BYTES synthetic streams, so expect worse floors than the anchors. 6. GATE-0 PREREQUISITE. No published measurement of data-dependent GEMM power on Apple silicon exists (one search, snippets only). Run a 1-hour microbench first: MLX fp16/bf16 GEMM 2048^2 with random vs constant vs zero vs 50%-sparse operands, GPU rail + DC. If constant-vs-random is <5% of GEMM power on M-series, the per-head experiment is hopeless and only the per-layer grid survives; if 30-60% as on NVIDIA, proceed. 7. RELEVANCE TO ED'S INTEREST. Astra-style edits touch ~0.016% of weights and change behavior (loop removal); at fixed tokens their datapath-energy footprint is sub-mW and invisible on this rig. The dominant energy effect of surgical weight edits is via generation length and token distribution, which JouleWise's existing fixed-vs-free-length machinery already measures well. Frame the artifact study as a calibration result needed before any 'energy of an edit' claim, not as an interp discovery.

**Residual novelty**

Not refuted. Nobody has measured (a) data-dependent GEMM/transformer power on Apple silicon, (b) the energy cost of in-place activation ablation of any style, (c) zero vs mean vs resample ablation by energy, or (d) whether powermetrics' GPU-rail estimator captures bit-toggle power at all. Residual novelty after the attacks: a three-level ordering test (zero <= mean < resample) with a patched-downstream arm separating the direct datapath term from the importance-correlated downstream term, run DC-primary. Recommended reshaping: Gate 0 = 1 Mac-hour GEMM operand-pattern microbench on M-series (replicating Bhalachandra/Gregersen; publishable alone, feeds LOCKIN-SWAP); Gate 1 = per-layer x style grid, ~1 Mac-night, DC primary, GPU rail as estimator-blindness check; Gate 2 (bank) = per-head grid only if Gate 0 shows >=30% constant-vs-random GEMM contrast, ~5 Mac-nights with hour-long lock-in per cell. Position it as a calibration prerequisite for any 'energy of a surgical weight edit' claim, stating plainly that at fixed tokens such edits' per-token energy effect is below the Mac rig's floor and their real energy effect is via behavior/token count.

### Opus 5.5 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Gregersen, Patel, Choukse, 'Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs', arXiv:2409.18324 (2024; SC'24 workshop PDF link 404'd, so I read the arXiv abstract and HTML). It is the closest physical precedent. On an A100 running CUTLASS GEMMs (2048x2048; FP32, FP16, FP16 tensor-core, INT8) with shapes held fixed, changing only the input data shifted power by up to about 38-40%. 'Matrix sparsity decreases GEMM power' (T12). Highly similar bit patterns lower power (T4). A larger mean lowers floating-point power (T2), while standard deviation has no significant effect (T1). Sparsity applied to sorted matrices can raise power, peaking around 30-40% sparsity (T13). The authors attribute the effect to bit flips. They did not test memory-bound kernels or Apple GPUs, and the text gives no per-sparsity magnitudes (those are only in the figures).
- Petri, Zhang, Chen, Schlichtmann, Li, 'PowerPruning: Selecting Weights and Activations for Power-Efficient Neural Network Acceleration', DAC 2023, arXiv:2303.13997 (opened). It picks weight and activation values by the power and timing they cost in the MAC units, then retrains, and reports up to 78.3% power reduction. This is circuit-level work on digital accelerators, not GPU measurement, but it shows that value-dependent MAC power is an established mechanism.
- Shumailov et al., 'Sponge Examples: Energy-Latency Attacks on Neural Networks', arXiv:2006.03463, EuroS&P 2021 (abstract opened). Crafted inputs raise energy 10-200x across CPUs, GPUs and accelerators. That shows input-dependent energy exists, but most of the GPU effect is in workload size and latency, not bit toggles at a fixed shape.
- Cina et al., 'Energy-Latency Attacks via Sponge Poisoning', arXiv:2203.08147 (seen only as a search result, UNVERIFIED). Poisoning during training reduces activation sparsity on sparsity-exploiting accelerators.
- Zhang and Nanda, 'Towards Best Practices of Activation Patching in Language Models: Metrics and Methods', arXiv:2309.16042, ICLR 2024 (opened; the last 17k characters were not read). Different corruption methods (Gaussian noising versus symmetric token replacement) lead to different interpretability conclusions. Zero, mean and resample ablation do not appear in the text I read, and energy is not discussed.
- Ed's reference was found and the primary source opened: Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming: LLMs can natively read & write neural network weights', blog post of 2026-11-02 at rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming. GPT-6 Astra subtracted one MLP output direction in layers 15 and 19 of Qwen3.5-0.8B: 131,072 weights, or 0.016% of the model. With 50 forward passes, MATH-500 went from 7.9% to 48.1%, and answers hitting the 32,768-token cap fell from 93% to 33.1%. A Gemma 3 270M control showed no meaningful gain. The post reports no power or energy measurements. Secondary coverage includes Matthew Honnibal's criticism that the gain was mostly a loop fix (buildmvpfast.com and yourstory.com both returned 403; zeli.app was opened).
- I found no paper that measures the physical energy of zero-, mean- or resample-ablation, or that proposes an energy-based importance score. The web-search budget ran out before I could run an Apple-silicon-specific search for data-dependent GPU power, so Apple-specific prior work is unchecked.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1) The per-head effect is about 10-100x smaller than the idea's 1-10 mW, so per-head contrasts are infeasible. Zero-ablating one head zeroes only the d_head slice of that layer's O-projection input. Q, K, V, the attention scores and every other matmul run unchanged. The affected share of per-token MACs is about (d_model^2/H)/(12 d_model^2 L) = 1/(12HL), roughly 8e-5 for L=H=32. Even if a zero operand made those MACs free, the ceiling is about 8e-5 of matmul dynamic power, about 1 mW at a 10-20 W GPU rail. Gregersen's whole-matrix changes move GEMM power by at most about 40%, which makes a realistic figure 0.1-0.5 mW in compute-bound prefill. In decode (bandwidth-bound matrix-vector products), energy is dominated by streaming weights from DRAM. Activation zeros do not change that traffic, so the effect is smaller still. Against the anchors: replay repeatability of about 2 J on 2-4 kJ is 0.05-0.1%. For a 50 J member (about 3 s at about 15 W) that is roughly 25-50 mJ, while a 0.3 mW effect over 3 s is about 1 mJ. A 2-sigma detection would need on the order of 10^3-10^4 members per arm. Per token in decode, the GPU-rail resolution of 0.0001 J/GB on a model of about 4 GB is about 0.4 mJ/token. The per-head direct effect is on the order of microjoules per token, about 100x below that.
2) Per-layer zeroing is plausible but smaller than claimed: tens of mW, not 0.1-1 W. Zeroing all MLP neurons of one layer zeroes the down-projection input, about 1/3 of that layer's MACs, or about 1% of model MACs at L=32. The ceiling is about 1% of matmul dynamic power (about 0.1-0.2 W). A realistic figure is 20-80 mW in prefill. That is about 0.07-0.25 J on a 50 J member, which ABBA with about 10-20 quads can resolve. In decode it sits near the 0.4 mJ/token GPU-rail threshold. Zeroing a whole attention block's output gives about 1/(12L) of MACs, roughly 3x smaller. Zeroing a layer's residual contribution zeroes no matmul operand directly; its whole effect runs through downstream activations.
3) The claim's own mechanism conflicts with its predictions. (a) Mean-ablation writes the same vector into every token row. By Gregersen's T4 (high bit similarity lowers power) and T2 (a larger mean lowers floating-point power), mean-ablation should also lower GEMM power in prefill. 'Mean- and resample-ablation do not' is therefore physically doubtful; only resample ablation keeps natural statistics. (b) 'Independently of functional importance' does not hold. Any ablation changes the residual stream and therefore every downstream activation. That indirect path scales with how much the component matters, can change activation norms and outliers across all later GEMMs, and is spread over far more MACs than the direct zeroed slice. For a single head it could easily dominate the direct effect. The design must separate direct from downstream effects. One way is to apply the replacement but stop it from propagating, by feeding downstream layers the clean residual. Another is to compare against a sham arm.
4) Implementation artifacts can exceed the physics. Ablation hooks add kernels. Resample ablation needs stored source activations, or a second pass, which costs extra memory traffic. A zero ablation written as slicing or skipping saves real work. In MLX, multiplying by a constant 0 might be simplified under mx.compile (unverified). Every style must run through one identical fused op, out = where(mask, repl, out), with repl a runtime tensor of matching dtype and shape. A sham arm where repl equals out is required as the null.
5) Possible fatal precondition: does the Apple GPU rail see data-dependent toggling at all? I could not verify whether powermetrics' per-rail GPU energy is measured current or a model driven by activity counters and DVFS state. If it is a model, data-dependent switching may be invisible on that rail, and only the noisier whole-system DC meter would show it. Gregersen also saw weaker effects on GDDR6 than on HBM parts, and nothing is known about Apple GPUs or LPDDR. A cheap positive control must come first: a fixed-shape MLX GEMM and GEMV with random, all-zero, constant-row and sorted inputs, read on the GPU rail and the DC meter together. If whole-matrix zeros do not move the GPU rail by more than about 1% in compute-bound GEMM, drop the idea.
6) The motivation is weak. Nobody uses energy-based importance scores, so 'an energy-based importance score would be biased' attacks a method that does not exist. On Ed's question about weight edits: in the Astra case, energy changes almost entirely through behavior. Generation length changes, with cap-hitting answers falling from 93% to 33%, so the energy shift is large and easy to measure. Per-token physics of a 0.016% weight edit (changed weight bit patterns crossing the DRAM bus) is on the order of 1e-4 relative and below the floor. The experiment sits at the noisiest end of the power-signal problem Ed raised.
Feasible version: (i) the Apple positive-control microbenchmark, about 1-2 hours; (ii) only if (i) passes, per-layer MLP down-projection zero vs mean vs resample vs sham, in teacher-forced prefill (compute-bound), on 4-8 layers spanning functional importance. Use GPU rail plus DC, ABBA with null quads, and the direct-only versus with-propagation split. Drop the per-head grid unless (i) shows a much larger effect than predicted here. With the sham and split arms, the cost of about 0.3 Mac-night is plausible.

**Residual novelty**

Moderate-to-low. What is new is narrow. (1) As far as I could find, nobody has measured data-dependent (bit-toggle) GPU power on Apple silicon. A Gregersen-style microbenchmark (zero, constant-row, sorted and random inputs at fixed shapes), read on the powermetrics GPU rail and a DC meter together, would be a small but real addition. It would also show whether the GPU rail can see data-dependent switching at all, which matters for every JouleWise rail claim. (2) No one has measured the physical energy of ablation styles or split it into a direct operand-zeroing term and a downstream activation-shift term that depends on the component's importance. As stated, the claim is probably wrong in two places. Mean-ablation's constant rows should also lower power, and the downstream term ties energy to functional importance, so the effect is not independent of it. The per-head version is roughly 100x below the noise floor. The per-layer prefill version may be detectable at about 20-80 mW. The more useful direction for Ed's weight-editing interest is behavioral energy: how surgical edits, such as the Astra loop-direction removal (verified in the primary blog post), change tokens per task and therefore joules per task. That signal is large and robust, while per-token toggle physics is marginal.

---

## 3. EDIT-FRONTIER: joules per correct answer before and after an Astra-class loop-fix edit (seat 1 (Fable))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming' / 'LLMs can understand and write neural network weights', blog 2026-11-02, rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming (OPENED): Qwen3.5-0.8B, thinking on, 32,768-token cap, temp 0.6/top-p 0.95/top-k 20/presence 1.5; base 7.9% MATH-500 with 93.0% hitting the cap; 50-pass edit 48.1% with 33.1%; edit = direction subtracted from 64 output columns of MLPs in layers 15 and 19 (131,072 weights, 0.016%); Gemma 3 270M control moved <1 point. No energy, latency, hardware, tokens-per-answer, thinking-off or reduced-budget comparison. No arXiv listing found.
- github.com/RikiyaT/lm_understand-logs (OPENED): full Astra logs, exp8_math holds exact weight diffs + apply script (CC BY 4.0 / MIT); hardware only 'a rented GPU node'; eval token caps/thinking mode not stated in README; no energy data.
- huggingface.co/JonathanColetti/Qwen3.5-0.8B-Astra-WeightEdit (OPENED; third-party application of the authors' diff, hash-checked against their checkpoint): independent vLLM bf16 rerun: MATH-500 base 7.4 -> edited 50.2 (thinking on); base thinking-off 45.8; cap-hit rates base-on 93%, edited-on 32%, base-off 32%, edited-off 31%; edit costs 1.5-2 points with thinking off. Card: 'This removes a failure mode; it doesn't add capability.' No energy or latency. ONNX/GGUF conversions are third-party.
- Alan West, dev.to 'GPT-6 Astra Edited Qwen3.5-0.8B's Weights. The MATH-500 Jump Was a Loop Fix' (OPENED): loop-fix framing; only a 48-token CPU smoke test; no energy/latency/token averages. Points to huggingface.co/RikiyaT/Qwen3.5-0.8B-weight-space-programming (UNVERIFIED, not opened) and NotCherry GGUF (UNVERIFIED).
- Hacker News thread 50351111 'The Astra can read and write weights to improve Qwen post is misleading' (OPENED, first 100k chars): mech-interp commenters say the Qwen result is loop reduction via directional ablation, 'mere directional ablation, a well-known technique', citing Arditi et al. 2024 arXiv 2406.11717 (UNVERIFIED, not opened) and Honnibal's blog honnibal.dev/blog/astra-read-write-misleading (UNVERIFIED, not opened). No energy, power or token-count discussion.
- Sean Goedecke, 'LLMs are not superhuman at reading and writing neural network weights' (OPENED): calls the procedure 'basically the Heretic abliteration playbook'; tasks under 32k were already solved by the base model; no energy/compute mention.
- arXiv 2610.14389 'Measuring the Inference Energy Efficiency of Open-Weight LLMs across Benchmarks, Reasoning Modes, and Hardware' (OPENED): 86 checkpoints, 0.8B-400B, six NVIDIA platforms (no Apple silicon); defines energy per correct answer (benchmark energy / correct count); 44 thinking-on/off pairs at fixed weights: thinking raises energy ~3.7x for median +4.5 points, truncation at cap 3% -> 17%; generated tokens explain 60-85% of log-energy variance; energy per generated token varies 3-10x across configs and rises 2.0-4.9x from first 8K output tokens to the last (KV pressure); no weight edits or abliteration. Substantially answers the generic 'thinking-mode toggles change J/correct by >2x and mostly via tokens' question.
- arXiv 2505.14733 'The Energy Cost of Reasoning: Analyzing Energy Usage in LLMs with Test-time Compute' (UNVERIFIED, search snippet only): test-time reasoning on a 1.5B model gives similar accuracy gain at +57.4% energy per query.
- dev.to 'How to Rank Local LLMs by Cost per Correct Answer (Measured GPU Energy, 8 Ollama Models)' (UNVERIFIED, snippet only): practitioner J-per-correct metric; Qwen3 reasoning models 555-672 tokens/task vs 68-125 for Gemma without accuracy gain.
- JouleWise repo (docs/research_question_bank.md ~L1605-1617, PC-4/PC-5/PC-6): decode bandwidth-bound at near-flat 23-28 W so decode energy differences are decode time; chunked KV-growth slope known per model; token-shape sufficiency (content collapses to token counts at fixed shape). Planning anchor 512-token decode ~47 J at 1.5B (~0.09 J/token). The idea's '0.18-0.67 J/token ALIGN decode coefficients' were not found in the handoff docs I could read (UNVERIFIED; at 0.8B expect lower than the 1.5B anchor). Repo already runs the qwen3.5 family (configs/examples/mac_mlx_qwen35_122b.json, Qwen3.5-122B-A10B-4bit via mlx-community), so mlx-lm qwen3_5 support exists for the MoE variant; dense 0.8B support is likely but unchecked.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1. Headline is a foregone conclusion, not a measurement. With PC-4 flat decode power, J/correct = (W/tok-rate) * tokens/correct; published numbers already give ~12x (390k vs 31k tokens per correct answer). Energy measurement adds nothing to the >2x claim; the only thing it can add is the J/token non-constancy (KV slope) and the fixed-token residual. 2. The statistical noise is in the denominator, not the joules. At n=100 MATH-500 prompts the base arm yields ~8 +/- 2.7 correct answers (binomial), so J-per-correct carries ~35% relative uncertainty from correctness counts alone; the edited arm ~48 +/- 5 (~10%). The 2 J replay floor on 2-4 kJ episodes (0.05-0.1%) is irrelevant to the headline ratio; any claim finer than 'order of magnitude' needs the full 500 problems (and the blog used 4 samples per problem). 3. Wall-clock does not fit 'half a Mac-night'. Base arm: 100 prompts x ~30.8k tokens ~ 3M decode tokens. Qwen3.5-0.8B bf16 is ~1.6 GB of weights; bandwidth-bound decode on an M4 Max (546 GB/s) gives maybe 150-200 tok/s realistic, on an M4 Air (120 GB/s) ~50-60 tok/s. That is 4-6 h (MBP) or 14+ h (Air) per base pass; ABBA doubles it; edited arm (~14.8k tokens/prompt) adds ~2 h per pass; teacher-forced replays of all ~9M tokens double again. Realistic: 20-30 Mac-hours at n=100 on the MBP, or n~25 / cap 8k to fit one night. Energy per base pass ~300-500 kJ at ~0.1-0.17 J/token; fine for the DC meter but long. 4. J/token is not constant across a 32k generation. Qwen3.5-0.8B is hybrid: 6 of 24 layers are full GQA attention (2 KV heads, head dim 256) and 18 are Gated DeltaNet (fixed-size state). KV per token ~ 6 x 2 x 2 x 256 x 2 B ~ 12 KB, so a 32k context carries ~400 MB of KV, ~25% of the weight bytes; late tokens cost ~25% more than early ones (single-stream, memory-bound estimate; 2610.14389 saw 2-4.9x on batched GPUs). The base arm spends most tokens at long context and the edited arm does not, so 'whole change explained by one decode coefficient' will fail by tens of percent unless the repo's KV-slope term is included; that is RQ-KV-GROWTH, not a weight-edit effect. 5. Fixed-token residual physics. Identical architecture, byte counts, FLOPs and kernel schedule; the only mechanism for a difference under teacher forcing is data-dependent switching power (different activation bit patterns downstream of layer 15, plus 131,072 different weight values). Between two realistic activation distributions this is plausibly 0-1% of dynamic power (general knowledge; I could not open a citation before the search budget ran out, UNVERIFIED). At 0.1-1% of a 3 kJ episode that is 3-30 J, i.e. the residual is not guaranteed to be inside the 2 J floor; it is a bound worth measuring but the repo's content-sentinel null at fixed shape (a far larger activation perturbation than this edit) predicts it will be small. 6. Quantization can destroy the edit. The edit is a direction subtracted from 64 down_proj columns; 4-bit MLX quantization (the repo's default) may round it away or alias it. Must run bf16 (or verify cap-hit rate survives quant before any energy run). bf16 at 0.8B is fine for memory but slower. mlx-lm qwen3_5 dense support must be verified (repo only has the 122B MoE config); the ONNX/GGUF variants are third-party. 7. Teacher-forced token-by-token decode in MLX needs a custom loop that feeds fixed tokens; RQ-MLX-KV-REPLAY covers prompt-cache replay, not forced decode. Buildable, but it is new harness code, not 'rerun the same outputs'. 8. Blinding the free-running arms is cosmetic: arms differ by hours of runtime and visible looping text. Blinding only matters for the fixed-token residual arms, where the effect is near floor; design ABBA and blinding there. 9. Ed's noise worry is misplaced for this experiment: on free-running arms the signal is kJ vs a 2 J floor; background-process noise does not matter. It matters only for the residual, which is exactly the part that the design should treat as a bound, not a detection. 10. Missing comparators: base/thinking-off (45.8%, 32% cap-hits) and base with a hard budget cap are the controls that separate 'weight edit' from 'any loop stopper'; without them the experiment attributes to interp-style surgery what thinking-off already delivers at near-identical token cost. 11. Baseline fragility: HN commenters question whether 7.9% is a setup artifact; the HF rerun got 7.4, consistent, but the 32,768 cap is the whole effect (at an 8k cap the base arm is ~4x cheaper and the ratio collapses), so any J/correct number must be reported as cap-conditional. 12. Model download is required and needs Ed's approval; the diff + apply script (CC BY 4.0 / MIT) is small, the base Qwen3.5-0.8B (Apache-2.0) is ~1.6 GB bf16.

**Residual novelty**

Narrow. Ed's reference exists: it is the MIT CSAIL blog/technical report "Sparks of Weight-Space Programming" (Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab, 2026-11-02; GPT-6 Astra edits two mlp.down_proj tensors, layers 15 and 19, 64 output columns each, 131,072 weights = 0.016%), with public logs at github.com/RikiyaT/lm_understand-logs (opened; diffs + apply script, no energy, latency or hardware details) and edited weights at huggingface.co/RikiyaT/Qwen3.5-0.8B-weight-space-programming (not opened; UNVERIFIED). No arXiv listing or peer review was found. Nobody has measured energy, power or latency for this edit, for abliteration, or for any directional-ablation weight edit as far as my searches reached (search budget ran out before targeted model-editing/energy and data-dependent-GPU-power queries; those two gaps are stated, not closed). So the experiment is not pre-empted. BUT the headline claim is not a hypothesis, it is arithmetic: from the published cap-hit and accuracy numbers (base 93% of answers hit the 32,768 cap at 7.9% correct; edited 33% at 48.1%), tokens per correct answer are roughly 30.8k/0.079 = 390k vs 14.8k/0.481 = 31k, about 12x, and the repo's own PC-4 finding (decode is bandwidth-bound at near-flat 23-28 W, so decode energy differences are decode time) makes J-per-correct track tokens by construction. ">2x" is guaranteed before any joule is measured. The "whole change explained by token counts at one decode coefficient" sub-claim is already known to be false in detail: J/token is not constant over a 32k generation (repo PC-5 KV-growth slope; arXiv 2610.14389 reports energy per generated token rising 2.0-4.9x from the first 8K tokens to the last in thinking mode on GPUs), so a free-running comparison needs E = fixed + a*p + b*d + KV-slope, not b*d alone. The only genuinely open number is the fixed-token (teacher-forced) residual between the two weight sets, and the repo's token-shape-sufficiency line (RQ-CONTENT-SENTINEL / C5-W.1 / PC-6: content collapses to token counts at fixed shape) already targets that null for perturbations far larger than a 0.016% weight edit; check its status in CLAIMS_STATUS.md before treating it as answered. What would be new and worth banking: (1) the first measured fixed-token energy residual of a published interp-derived weight edit on Apple silicon, as a bound (expected null, resolvable to ~0.1% of episode energy with the 2 J replay floor); (2) a per-token power trace aligned to loop onset in a 32k degenerate generation, testing whether repetitive-loop text has any J/token signature at matched context position (a 32k-scale content sentinel); (3) a three-way J-per-correct comparison the blog omits: edited/thinking-on vs base/thinking-off (HF card rerun: 45.8% MATH-500, 32% cap-hits, i.e. the edit buys about +4.3 points at the same cap-hit rate) vs base with a hard budget cap (e.g. 8k), which isolates what the weight edit contributes beyond "any loop stopper". Recommend reframing EDIT-FRONTIER around (1)-(3), banking it behind ideas 1 and 2, and dropping the ">2x" headline as a claim.

### Opus 5.5 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- OPENED: Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming: LLMs can natively read & write neural network weights', https://rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/. This is the source of Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights'. Experiment 8 subtracts one 'loop direction' from 64 output columns of mlp.down_proj in layers 15 and 19 (131,072 weights, 0.016%). It ran 10, 25 or 50 forward passes in Python with no gradient step. At a 32,768-token cap, thinking on, T 0.6, top-p 0.95, top-k 20 and presence penalty 1.5, MATH-500 went from 7.9 to 48.1% and cap hits from 93.0 to 33.1%. It reports no energy, latency or mean token counts. The same method failed on Gemma 3 270M.
- OPENED: the GitHub logs at https://github.com/RikiyaT/lm_understand-logs exist. They hold exact Exp-8 weight diffs against the public base, an apply script, and per-turn wall time for the editing agent. Whether they hold per-problem Qwen MATH-500 outputs with token counts is UNVERIFIED (the page does not say).
- OPENED: the edited weights are public at https://huggingface.co/JonathanColetti/Qwen3.5-0.8B-Astra-WeightEdit (bf16 safetensors, GGUF, ONNX; edit CC BY 4.0). The uploader applied the authors' diff and matched their checkpoint hash. This independent vLLM run gives MATH-500 base 7.4 (93% cap hits) vs edited 50.2 (32%) with thinking on, and base 45.8 vs edited 43.6 with thinking off. Against base thinking-off the edit gains only +4.3 points [1.9, 6.8]. The card says the edit 'removes a failure mode; it doesn't add capability'. The blog also names the authors' own HF repo RikiyaT/Qwen3.5-0.8B-weight-space-programming (repo not opened, UNVERIFIED). So the idea's 'UNVERIFIED' on the logs and weights can be cleared: both are public.
- OPENED: honnibal.dev/blog/astra-read-write-misleading. A critique: Exp 8 is Python-driven weight writing with model execution, not raw weight reading, and the method did not transfer to Gemma. It has no energy or token data.
- OPENED: dev.to/alanwest 'GPT-6 Astra Edited Qwen3.5-0.8B's Weights. The MATH-500 Jump Was a Loop Fix'. It gives truncation rates only. Uncontrolled Hacker News claims put base thinking-off at 52-55%.
- OPENED: Qwen/Qwen3.5-0.8B model card. 24 layers in a 3:1 Gated DeltaNet to gated-attention hybrid (6 full-attention layers), hidden size 1024, FFN 3584. The card warns this model is more prone to thinking loops than other Qwen3.5 models, and it is non-thinking by default.
- OPENED: Luo, Li, Wang, Chen, 'Where Does the Energy Go? Profiling LLM Agent Inference on Blackwell GPUs', arXiv:2609.29707 (Aug 2026; NVML, RAPL and IPMI). Per-token energy differs by less than 1% between thinking and non-thinking modes. The extra per-problem energy is 'driven by output volume rather than a change in per-token efficiency'. This is the GPU analogue of the claim's 'whole change explained by token counts', for a mode switch rather than a weight edit.
- OPENED: Jin, Wei, Brooks, 'The Energy Cost of Reasoning: Analyzing Energy Usage in LLMs with Test-time Compute', arXiv:2505.14733 (A100, NVML). It reports accuracy per Wh for reasoning vs base, and that incorrect reasoning answers run longer 'due to loops or divergent reasoning'. It does not claim energy is linear in tokens.
- OPENED: Gregersen, Patel, Choukse, 'Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs', arXiv:2409.18324. On NVIDIA, GEMM power changes by up to about 40% with the input values alone, with shapes held fixed (bit flips). This is the only known route by which a value-only weight edit could change power when the bytes moved stay the same.
- Search hits NOT opened (UNVERIFIED): arXiv 2608.28044 (request and token energy characterization), 2605.00300 'Token Arena' (defines J per correct answer as j·T/A), 2505.07961 (efficient small reasoners), 2505.17250 ConciseRL.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1. Wrong coefficients. The 0.18-0.67 J/token ALIGN numbers come from Qwen3-30B-A3B 4-bit MoE at about 12k context (RECORDs 2026-11-03 c9ac22ae, 2026-11-04 7e06863b, 2026-11-05 f775edaf). They do not transfer to a 0.8B bf16 DeltaNet hybrid. Repo planning figures put 0.5B at about 0.047 and 1.5B at about 0.100 J/token for 512-token decode (03-fable-seat.md, ladder consult). Expect about 0.05-0.1 J/token here, and fit fresh coefficients, including the attention-length term, on 6 attention layers plus 18 DeltaNet layers. 'Explained at the ALIGN coefficients' would fail for a trivial reason.

2. Detectability is fine. A cap-hit problem is 32,768 tokens x about 0.06-0.1 J, roughly 2-3 kJ, the same scale as the 2 J replay anchor. The arm difference is about 15k tokens x about 0.07 J, or about 1 kJ per problem, some 500x the floor.

3. Cost is badly underestimated. The base arm is about 30.5k tokens per problem, so 100 problems are about 3.1M tokens. The edited arm adds about 1.4M. One free-running pass of both arms is about 4.5M decode tokens. Single-stream MLX throughput for this model is UNMEASURED. At a guessed 100-250 tok/s that is 5-12 h for one pass. ABBA doubles it, and teacher-forced re-runs of both arms' outputs in both weight sets double it again: 2-4 Mac-nights, not half a night. Batching would change the measured regime. Lowering the cap to 8k changes the loop and cap-hit behaviour, which is unpublished at that cap. Fix: 50 problems, one free-running pass per arm (the replay floor already covers repeatability), and teacher-forcing on a small subsample only (for example 8-10 problems, both arms' sequences, both weight sets, ABBA).

4. The headline ratio is limited by accuracy counts, not by energy. At 7.9% on 50-100 problems the base arm gets 4-8 correct, so log J per correct has a sampling SE of about 0.35-0.5. The meter adds nothing to that precision; the repo's J-per-correct council made the same point (one answer moves log J/correct by 0.18). Base J per correct is also mostly set by the cap (93% of problems hit it), so the '>2x' is a property of the eval protocol.

5. The fixed-token residual is predicted far below the floor. At batch-1 decode the GEMV is memory-bound, and the edit reads identical bytes, so the 0.0001 J/GB traffic contrast is exactly zero. The only route is data-dependent switching (Gregersen et al., up to about 40% on compute-bound NVIDIA GEMM). Scaled by the 1.6e-4 fraction of weights changed, that is at most about 6e-5 relative, about 0.13 J per 2 kJ problem. Downstream activation and DeltaNet-state value changes are also tiny next to weight traffic. So the residual can only ever be bounded at about 0.1%, never detected. The design should say so before running and report a bound, not a confirmation.

6. Teacher-forcing must replay outputs as one-token decode steps on the cache, not as one prefill. Prefill is compute-bound and has a different per-token cost.

7. Comparators. Without base thinking-off (45.8% per the HF run) and base thinking-on with a different presence penalty, the frontier is misleading: the edit 'fixes' a configuration nobody should deploy.

8. Practicalities. mlx-lm support for Qwen3.5's Gated DeltaNet hybrid is UNVERIFIED. The edited weights and the diff are public (HF and GitHub), so no reproduction is needed, but both downloads need Ed's approval. Sampling is stochastic: pair seeds per problem across arms, and blind the arm labels in the energy analysis, since the transcripts themselves reveal the arm (loops).

9. On Ed's worry that power is hard to pull from the noise: this design does not need fine power resolution, because the effect is all tokens. That is why it says little about weight-edit physics. Experiments where power could move at fixed behaviour need a compute-path change, such as router or expert edits on an MoE (expert locality, cache hits) or steering hooks that add FLOPs.

Web search budget ran out before I could check mlx-lm Qwen3.5 support or Qwen3.5 decode throughput on Apple silicon; both stay UNVERIFIED.

**Residual novelty**

No one has measured the energy of this edit or of any loop-removing weight edit. On that literal question the idea is new. Its headline is still decided before any measurement, though.

(a) The '>2x J per correct' follows from published numbers alone. Base thinking-on averages at least 0.93 x 32,768, about 30.5k tokens per problem. The edit averages at least 0.33 x 32,768 plus 0.67 x L, about 13-15k for any plausible L. Accuracy rises about 6.1x, so J per correct falls by roughly 13-15x, with or without a power meter.

(b) 'Explained by token counts' is the expected result. Luo et al. 2026 already show per-token energy is invariant to within 1% across reasoning modes on GPUs. Here no bytes moved change at all.

(c) The 'fixed-token residual within the replay floor' is a near-certain null. A bound on it is the only measured quantity that cannot be predicted.

What is actually new:
1. The first Apple-silicon J-per-correct frontier for a published interpretability-style edit. It should include the comparator that matters: base thinking-off, which per the HF reproduction is within 4.3 points of the edit and probably far cheaper per correct answer.
2. A measured upper bound on the per-token energy effect of a value-only surgical edit, roughly 0.1%. That gives a citable null for Ed's 'surgical edit changes power' intuition.
3. A clean decomposition: J per correct = per-token cost x tokens / accuracy, with the context term fitted for a 3:1 DeltaNet hybrid.

The intersection Ed actually wants is power changing at fixed behaviour. It lives in edits that change the compute path: MoE router edits that change expert choice and locality, edits that produce exploitable sparsity, early-exit, or inference-time steering that adds compute. A value-only down_proj edit cannot produce it.

---

## 4. JOULE-MAP: per-component energy attribution of a transformer on Apple GPU (seat 1 (Fable))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- OPENED: Dissecting Transformers: A 'CLEAR' Perspective towards Green AI (arXiv 2510.02810, v2 HTML). Component-Level Energy Assessment via Repetitions: forward hooks cache each component's inputs, then attention / MLP / norms / embedding / LM head are replayed in isolation N=1,000-10,000 times and NVML energy is divided by N (T=20 trials). RTX 5000/6000 Ada, 15 models (BERT family, Llama-3.2-3B, Gemma-3-4B, Qwen2.5-3B, Phi-4-mini, BART, FLAN-T5, GPT-OSS-20B). Findings: MLP dominates absolute energy (GPT-OSS-20B @8 tok: attention 53 mJ vs MLP 685 mJ), attention has the highest E/FLOP, E/FLOP falls with sequence length (fixed launch/setup overhead), with KV cache attention energy is ~flat 1-512 tok; attention/(attn+MLP) share rises only ~7.8% -> ~9.6% from 8 to 128 tokens. Precision: component stddev <9.5% (about 1% near 1 J); sum-of-components captures 92-101% of whole-model energy. Not Apple silicon, not unified memory, no per-layer-index attribution, no in-situ skip ablation, no DRAM/traffic rail, contexts <=512.
- OPENED: Energy-Efficient LLM Serving via Disaggregated Attention-FFN and Flexible Frequency Scaling / AFlex (arXiv 2608.01891, abstract). A800, Qwen3-32B and Mixtral-8x7B in SGLang; operator-level attention vs FFN energy with per-operator DVFS; attention and FFN have different energy-optimal frequencies; up to 49% energy/token saving. Component split is attention vs FFN only, NVIDIA, no per-layer attribution, no memory-traffic rail.
- OPENED: GreenBench (arXiv 2608.28667, abstract). Apple M4 Pro 48 GB, powermetrics, 3-9B models via Ollama, energy/token at package and system level, no per-component or per-layer attribution, no repeatability figures. Establishes whole-model Apple-silicon energy/token baselines only.
- OPENED: From Tokens to Watt-hours (arXiv 2607.26571, abstract). Analytical H100 model splitting energy into compute, parameter-access, KV-cache write and attention-read terms with calibrated coefficients; no measurement of components, not Apple. Relevant as the J/FLOP + J/byte decomposition JOULE-MAP would fit.
- OPENED: Determining Energy Efficiency Sweet Spots in Production LLM Inference / SweetSpot (arXiv 2602.05695, abstract). H100 + TensorRT-LLM, 1-9B models, input/output 64-4096; energy/token vs lengths with 1.79% MAPE model; phase/length level, no component attribution.
- OPENED (Ed's reference): 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming: LLMs can natively read & write neural network weights', Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/, 2026-11-02; and The Register coverage 2026-11-03 (theregister.com/a/5307105). GPT-6 Astra edited 131,072 weights (0.016%) of Qwen3.5-0.8B: subtracted one direction from 64 output columns of the MLPs in layers 15 and 19 to remove a looping failure. MATH-500 7.9% -> 48.1%, GSM8K 13.8% -> 67.1%, IFEval 21.0% -> 62.3%; answers hitting the 32,768-token limit 93.0% -> 33.1% (50 forward passes). No compute/latency/energy of the edited model reported. Secondary coverage (yourstory, fourweekmba, buildmvpfast, emergent.sh, zeli, letsdatascience) UNVERIFIED (not opened).
- In-repo: BYTES/block6 design already records KV-cache reads as a second variable (KV reads add 2-27% of bytes/token at 2k context; MoE decode slower per byte) — /Users/edr/code/CapstoneProject/jw-wt/handoff/docs/design/block6_collection_draft.md:575,596. KB J/GB result (GPU 0.00879 +/- 0.00010, rest 0.0296 +/- 0.0084, DC 0.0389 +/- 0.0072 J/GB) — docs/process_traces/2026-10-31-magistrate-5cbfbcfc/KB-SLOT-FILL.md. Decode-floor calibration: 50 J members, stddev 0.21 J, floor_abs 3.59 J, comparative floor 7.38 J — /Users/edr/code/CapstoneProject/jw-wt/handoff/df-ph-decode-floor-mint1.json. Attribution-scale convention ~1 J, 6 J planning bar — docs/research_question_bank.md:1205-1225. No existing layer-skip / module-skip / teacher-forced arm anywhere in docs (grep).
- NOT SEARCHED (web-search budget exhausted mid-task): layer-pruning energy literature (ShortGPT-style) and Metal/MLX per-kernel power tooling. From memory, not verified: Apple's Metal System Trace / Xcode GPU counters expose timing and bandwidth counters but no energy, so powermetrics rails remain the only energy source; treat as UNVERIFIED.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1) The IDEA's own detectability anchor is wrong. The repo's 50 J decode members (df-ph-decode-floor-mint1.json) have floor_abs 3.59 J and comparative floor 7.38 J; a 1/36 whole-layer skip on a 50 J member is ~1.4 J, BELOW the floor. It is only resolvable on the 2-4 kJ-episode regime (replays repeat to ~2 J): 1/36 of 2-4 kJ is ~55-110 J, 25-50x the repeat spread. Attention-module skip in a dense GQA layer is ~20% of the layer's bytes (attn projections ~2.5 d^2 vs MLP ~10.5 d^2) -> ~0.5% of the episode = 11-22 J, still >5x the 2 J spread. Per-layer norm/router/embedding shares are <0.1% per layer (~2-4 J) and sit AT the floor: not resolvable per layer; only resolvable as all-layers-at-once skips or via KB-style hour-long interleaved contrasts. 'A few percent' precision therefore holds for MLP/attention/lm_head/KV, not for the small modules. Comparing 'hundreds of mW' to a '50 J floor' also mixes power and energy; state the contrast in J at fixed tokens with the episode size.
2) Time confound. At fixed tokens, skipping a layer shortens the episode by ~1/36 because decode is bandwidth-bound; the raw whole-episode delta then contains P_baseline x dT (3-5 W idle incl. display x ~3 s on a 100 s episode = ~10-15 J), i.e. 20-25% of the 55 J signal. Must use energy-above-idle-baseline with duration accounting (the KB harvest already does this) or hold wall time fixed by idle padding. Same issue for the DC/rest traffic rail, where idle 'rest' is a larger fraction.
3) Additivity. CLEAR on NVIDIA found sum-of-components = 92-101% of whole-model energy; expect similar non-additivity on Metal from command-buffer scheduling, kernel fusion, and GPU DVFS. The 'few percent shares' claim needs a prereg'd additivity budget: sum of 36 single-layer skips vs an all-layers skip vs whole-model-minus-embedding. Record powermetrics GPU active frequency per arm and gate on equality (the KB run showed 1379.6 MHz in both arms; a module-skip that changes kernel mix may shift the governor).
4) Identifiability of J/FLOP vs J/GB is impossible at batch 1. For weight-streaming modules FLOPs and bytes are both proportional to parameter count; for attention-over-KV both are linear in context. The regression is collinear and J/FLOP per module is not separately estimable from the proposed arms. Add a batch-size axis (B=1,4,16: FLOPs scale with B, weight bytes do not) or a prefill-vs-decode contrast; otherwise report J/GB only and derive J/FLOP.
5) Teacher forcing keeps tokens (and bytes/FLOPs) fixed, but the skipped layer's downstream activations go off-distribution. Energy is weakly data-dependent (operand switching power; magnitude on Apple GPUs UNVERIFIED, likely a few % of dynamic power), so single-layer skips of middle layers (small perturbation) are preferable to all-layer skips for calibrating per-layer shares, and any denormal/NaN/saturation paths must be checked. In MoE models a skipped router must be replaced by replayed recorded expert indices, otherwise the bytes change and the arm measures routing, not the router.
6) 'Stable across prompt sets' is nearly tautological under teacher forcing at fixed tokens in a bandwidth-bound regime (identical bytes regardless of prompt); it is a useful null check for the data-dependence term, not a result. Likewise 'MLP weight streaming dominates' is already shown by CLEAR and follows from byte counts; frame it as a calibration, not a finding.
7) 'Attention share grows with context' is model-specific: Qwen3-Next-style models (mostly linear attention, little growing KV, per block6 notes) should show no growth; if Flash-Next/qwen4_exp is of that type the headline prediction fails for the primary model. Prereg the prediction per architecture; at 2k context the repo already expects KV reads at 2-27% of bytes/token, so a three-context design (e.g. 512/4k/16k) gives a tens-of-J, easily resolvable contrast for KV-heavy models.
8) Cost. 36 layer arms x 3 contexts x >=5 interleaved bundles x 1-2 min episodes is 9-18 Mac-hours before module arms, not 0.5 Mac-night. Feasible only with a sampled design (skip every k-th layer, grouped-layer skips, all-attention / all-MLP skips) and ~2 kJ episodes.
9) The weight-edit intersection Ed is interested in: value-only edits like Astra's (131k weights, no change in bytes or FLOPs) have a per-token energy effect only through data-dependent switching, plausibly <0.1% of decode power -> ~1-2 J on a 2-4 kJ episode, i.e. at the repeat floor; resolvable only with the KB-style hour-long interleaved design (which reached ~1% relative on the GPU rail). The dominant, trivially measurable energy effect of that edit is behavioral: MATH-500 answers hitting the 32,768-token limit fell 93% -> 33%, so energy per correct answer changes by a large factor through token count, not watts. The honest design is two-tier: fixed-token teacher-forced edited-vs-unedited (per-token watts, expect null at floor) plus free-running energy-per-solved-problem (large). On Ed's noise worry: the KB run's contention monitor attributed only 4.5e-6 J/GB (0.2% of the half-width) to background daemons under interleaving; the uncertainty was dominated by idle baseline and clock anchoring, so background activity is manageable with the existing interleaving and contention gating.

**Residual novelty**

Not refuted, but the headline is partly pre-empted: CLEAR (arXiv 2510.02810) already attributes transformer inference energy to attention/MLP/norm/embedding/LM-head on NVIDIA GPUs across 15 models, shows MLP dominates absolute energy and attention has the highest E/FLOP, and reports a weak rise of attention share with sequence length; AFlex does attention-vs-FFN operator energy on A800. What remains novel: (a) in-situ attribution on Apple unified memory via layer-skip/module-skip identity-bypass arms with teacher forcing (CLEAR uses isolated kernel replay, which does not capture scheduling, fusion, DVFS or shared-bus effects); (b) per-layer-index energy maps (CLEAR aggregates by component type); (c) separating compute (GPU rail) from memory traffic (DC minus rails / 'rest') per module, giving J/GB per module on a unified-memory SoC, which no opened paper does; (d) long-context decode (>=4k-16k) attention/KV share, where CLEAR stops at 512 tokens; (e) an additivity audit (sum of skips vs whole) at the repo's 2 J / 2-4 kJ repeatability, about an order of magnitude tighter than CLEAR's 92-101% capture. Reframe the claim: drop 'MLP dominates' and 'stable across prompt sets' as findings (both are expected and near-tautological under teacher forcing), keep 'per-layer J/GB map with additivity budget', 'context-dependent KV traffic share per architecture', and position JOULE-MAP as the calibration layer for the weight-edit energy question (fixed-token edited-vs-unedited contrast at the KB-style floor, plus free-running energy-per-solved-problem). Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra edits weights' reference exists and was opened: MIT CSAIL blog 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming' (Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab, 2026-11-02), with The Register coverage 2026-11-03; it reports no energy or latency numbers for the edited model. Web-search budget ran out before layer-pruning energy literature and Metal per-kernel power tooling could be searched; those remain UNVERIFIED gaps.

### Opus 5.5 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- VERIFIED (opened): Shokoufa, Sadeghi, Kamal, Pedram, 'Multi-Level Modeling of LLM Inference Latency and Energy via Hybrid Analytical-ML Predictors' (HYMELL), arXiv 2608.06723. This is the closest prior work. On an H100 NVL (and an A6000 in an ablation) it reads the NVML energy counter around RMSNorm, Softmax, GEMM, the attention block and the FFN block, each run in isolation for at least 10 s. Decode attention MAPE is 0.599%. It reports no attention/MLP energy shares, nothing per layer, no lm_head, no in-situ ablation and no context-share curve. It uses NVIDIA only, with no unified memory and no wall/DC meter. https://arxiv.org/html/2608.06723
- VERIFIED (opened): Vartziotis et al., 'From Tokens to Watt-hours: Analytical Energy Estimation for LLM Inference on Modern GPUs', arXiv 2607.26571. This is analytical only ('does not execute neural networks'). It splits energy into compute, parameter access, KV writes and attention reads, and it argues that attention-read energy grows super-linearly with output length. It has no attention/MLP compute split. Validation against borrowed numbers is off by 5-27%. It supports the 'attention share grows with context' prediction as theory and does not measure it. https://arxiv.org/html/2607.26571
- VERIFIED (opened): Chung, Wu, Ma, Chowdhury, 'Where Do the Joules Go? Diagnosing Inference Energy Consumption', arXiv 2601.22076. It measures whole-model GPU energy per token or response on H100/B200 across 46 models. It does not attribute energy to attention, MLP, layers or the KV cache. https://arxiv.org/html/2601.22076v2
- VERIFIED (abstract only): Vellaisamy, Lam, Blanton, Shen, 'Characterization of Request and Token Energy Costs for LLM Inference Workloads on GPU Platforms', arXiv 2608.28044. It splits H100/H200 energy into prefill/generation phases and fixed/per-token costs, with no component attribution. https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28044
- VERIFIED (abstract only): Wang et al., 'A Systematic Characterization of LLM Inference on GPUs', arXiv 2512.01644. Search snippets say FFN dominates at 1K-8K context, attention dominates above about 16K, and both are bandwidth-bound on Jetson Orin. Those are latency and utilization shares, not energy. I did not read the full text, so whether it also has per-operator energy is UNVERIFIED. https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.01644
- UNVERIFIED (metadata only; PDF body unreadable): Benazir and Lin, 'Efficient Mixture-of-Experts LLM Inference with Apple Silicon NPUs', arXiv 2604.18788. The search snippet says it breaks M2 Ultra MoE energy into CPU, ANE and communication. It is the most likely Apple-silicon overlap. Someone should read it before this is registered. https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.18788
- UNVERIFIED (search snippet only): GreenBench, arXiv 2608.28667. Whole-model powermetrics energy of open LLMs on Apple silicon. Per the snippet it notes that powermetrics package power excludes DRAM and the memory controller, which matches this attack's point about rail boundaries. https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.28667
- UNVERIFIED (search snippet only): Hybrid heterogeneous clusters paper, arXiv 2407.00010. Polls powermetrics for Apple GPU energy at whole-model granularity. https://arxiv.org/pdf/2407.00010
- UNVERIFIED (search snippets only): layer-skipping papers (GateSkip, arXiv 2510.13876; D^3, arXiv 2503.08524; SkipGPT). They report FLOP and latency savings, not measured joules. GateSkip lists real latency and throughput as open. https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.13876v1
- VERIFIED (opened), Ed's reference: Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming: LLMs can natively read & write neural network weights', blog post, 2 Nov 2026. GPT-6 Astra subtracted one direction from 64 output columns of the layer-15 and layer-19 MLPs of Qwen3.5-0.8B (131,072 weights, 0.016%). MATH-500 went from 7.9% to 48.1%, and answers hitting the 32,768-token cap fell from 93.0% to 33.1%. The post reports no energy, compute or latency measurement. It is a blog post, not peer reviewed, and critics such as Honnibal call the framing misleading. https://rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/ (secondary summary: https://themenonlab.blog/blog/weight-space-programming-llms-read-write-neural-network-weights/)
