It is not GPU data and not this regime. Hot Pixels gives Apple GPU data dependence only as correlations on the Air.

3. Even if frequency moved, tok/s would barely follow. Decode on Apple silicon is memory-bandwidth-bound: for a 70B 4-bit model, tok/s is roughly 300-400 GB/s divided by about 40 GB per token. tok/s sensitivity to GPU core clock is well below 1, so a 1-3% clock change may give a tok/s change smaller than run-to-run noise. The likely coupling paths are fabric or DRAM DVFS and possible hardware compression (GPU.zip). As far as I could see, neither appears among the GPU or cluster frequency fields the repo parses (only GPU and cluster fields are parsed). Logging GPU frequency alone can therefore miss the mechanism that matters.

4. The instrument is not the limiting noise; drift is. Wall-clock tok/s is precise. A 10 Hz DVFS residency average over 10-20 min (about 6-12k samples) resolves residency-weighted frequency to well under 1%. Rail energy replays to about 2 J on 2-4 kJ (about 0.05-0.1%). The real noise is heat soak, ambient temperature and fan-state history over 10-20 min fills. Single long fills per condition confound entropy with run order and thermal history. Use interleaved ABAB blocks with a fixed thermal preconditioning or headroom hold, and run-to-run tok/s noise needs to be measured first (unmeasured here).

5. Near the thermal boundary the coupling is a threshold effect. A few watts from entropy mostly shifts when throttling or OS thermal pressure starts, not the steady-state mean frequency. The right outcome there is time to throttle or pressure onset (Hot Pixels' method: 300-3000 s on M1 CPU), compared across matched preconditioning. These runs fall in the regime the registered thermal gate excludes, so they are characterization only and cannot share cells with gated data.

6. Fan power is the most plausible leak, and it is invisible to rails. On an actively cooled part, extra GPU or DRAM power should raise SoC temperature and fan RPM. The DC-input meter sees fan power; the powermetrics rails do not. Log SMC fan RPM and die temperature, and treat DC-input minus rails as an outcome. 'Switching power leaks into the non-rail budget' is the more likely positive finding than 'leaks into performance'.

7. Relevance to Ed's interp angle (surgical weight edits): low-entropy operand matrices are not model edits. 4-bit quantized weights of real models are near maximum entropy, and a ROME- or MEMIT-style rank-one edit to a few MLP matrices changes a tiny fraction of the bytes streamed per token. The data-dependent power change from a surgical edit is therefore probably below every floor listed (unmeasured). Only edits that change a large share of streamed weights (pruning, sparsification, re-quantization, value clustering) could plausibly matter, and Gregersen's sparsity and few-unique-values results are the right prior there. DVFS-COUPLE answers whether ENTROPY-DIAL energy differences can be read as pure power differences. It does not answer whether surgical edits change energy.

8. Cost is fine (+20 min per fill, riding on ENTROPY-DIAL). Treat it as a mandatory covariate log for ENTROPY-DIAL (tok/s, residency-weighted GPU frequency, GPU active residency, fan RPM, die temperature, DC-input minus rails), not as a standalone experiment.

**Residual novelty**

Not refuted, but the headline null is mostly predicted. Hot Pixels shows that actively cooled M1 Macs have no data-dependent frequency, and Gregersen et al. show input-dependent GPU power with runtime constant to the microsecond on a non-throttling A100.

What remains open:
1. A first check on an M3 Max (higher power density than M1) under sustained, memory-bound LLM decode, rather than ALU microbenchmarks or compute-bound GEMM.
2. Whether entropy-driven power moves the time to OS thermal-pressure onset near the rig's documented 70B thermal boundary. This is a threshold effect, not a mean-frequency effect.
3. Whether the extra power appears as fan power in DC-input minus rails. This is the most plausible leak on an actively cooled part, and only the dual meter can see it.
4. Ruling out non-DVFS paths such as hardware compression changing bytes moved per token.

Its main value is as a cheap control that lets ENTROPY-DIAL energy deltas be read as pure power differences, with tok/s equal to within about 0.1%. For Ed's surgical-weight-edit interest, it says little directly: rank-one edits touch too few streamed bytes to plausibly move power or frequency (unmeasured), so the experiment is relevant only to bulk edits such as pruning and re-quantization.

I could not check Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra' reference because the session web-search budget ran out.

---

## 9. MODEL-ID: can a power trace tell which weights are running? (banked) (seat 1 (Fable))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Arefin & Serwadda, 'Deep Neural Exposure: You Can Run, But Not Hide Your Neural Network Architecture!', ACM IH&MMSec'21 (opened: PDF from author site, text extracted locally). GPU-Z power log at 100 samples/s on a workstation GPU; TSFresh features + classifiers; 10 CNN architectures identified at >80% accuracy; transfer to unseen derivative architectures only 41%. Architecture-level only; no same-architecture/different-weights, quantization or fine-tune arm.
- Patwari et al., 'DNN Model Architecture Fingerprinting Attack on CPU-GPU Edge Devices', IEEE EuroS&P 2022 (opened: NSF PAR PDF, text extracted locally). User-space RAM/CPU/GPU usage stats on Jetson; Random Forest; 99% accuracy into architecture family, 87.2% for unknown models; states weights/parameters are not needed and not recovered. Family-level only.
- Gao et al., 'DeepTheft: Stealing DNN Model Architectures through Power Side Channel', IEEE S&P 2024, arXiv:2309.11894 (opened: arXiv abstract). RAPL power at ~1 kHz on CPUs/MLaaS; recovers network structure (99.75% Levenshtein accuracy) and layer hyperparameters (99.60% F1). No weights, no fine-tuned variants.
- Horvath, Chmielewski, Weissbart, Batina, Yarom, 'CNN architecture extraction on edge GPU', arXiv:2401.13575 (opened: abstract). EM emissions from Jetson Nano; 15 CNN architectures 'easily distinguishable'. Architecture only.
- Batina, Bhasin, Jap, Picek, 'CSI Neural Network' (USENIX Security 2019), arXiv:1810.09076 (opened: abstract). EM/power on ARM Cortex-M3 recovers activation functions, layer/neuron counts AND weights. This is the one line of work that recovers weight values, but on a microcontroller with per-multiplication leakage; nothing comparable exists for GPU/SoC rails at 100 ms sampling.
- Mohammadi, Hashemi, Mohammadi, 'Securing Deep Learning Hardware: A Survey of Side-Channel Vulnerabilities and Countermeasures', arXiv:2607.04055 (2026) (opened: abstract only). Survey says SCAs extract 'architectures, parameters, and even user inputs'; abstract has no LLM, GPU/Apple-silicon, or weight-edit-detectability content. Full text UNVERIFIED.
- Kimpson, Baker, Graham, 'Can Power Draw Constrain Covert Compute? Limits of Analogue Verification for AI Governance', arXiv:2610.07476 (2026-10-05) (opened: arXiv API abstract). Closed form for beta, the hidden compute a power trace cannot exclude; A100 worst case beta=1.16, matched-energy adversary hides >=0.41; with verifier re-execution at the observed operating point beta drops to 0.059. Conclusion: analogue power alone constrains compute weakly. This is the 're-execution regime' the idea cites; it is about compute QUANTITY, not model identity, so it motivates but does not answer the claim.
- Kimpson, Baker, Graham, 'Detecting AI Training in Datacentre Power', arXiv:2610.30182 (2026-10-19) (opened: arXiv API abstract). Viterbi tracking of iteration cadence in facility power; >=96% of training runs detected at 1% FAR with a >=2 Hz meter. Workload-class detection, not model identity.
- Karvonen, Reuter, Rinberg, Marks, Garriga-Alonso, Warr, 'DiFR: Inference Verification Despite Nondeterminism', arXiv:2511.20621 (opened: abstract). Token-DiFR detects 4-bit quantization with AUC>0.999 within 300 output tokens; Activation-DiFR in 2 tokens. Pure output/activation verification, no hardware signal. This is the baseline the power-trace classifier must be compared against: when outputs are available, tokens beat power by orders of magnitude for the precision arm.
- Rinberg et al., 'Verifying LLM Inference to Detect Model Weight Exfiltration', arXiv:2511.02620 (NOT opened; UNVERIFIED beyond search snippet). Re-execution/activation-matching verifier.
- 'UniGuard': the three arXiv papers with this title (Pham et al. 2026 multi-view guardrails; Oh et al. 2024 multimodal jailbreak guardrails; CP-uniGuard 2025 malicious-agent detection in collaborative perception) are all safety-guardrail works, none about model integrity, tamper detection or side channels (opened: arXiv API title search). The idea's 'links to UniGuard' looks like a misattribution and should be dropped or re-sourced.
- Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights' reference: FOUND. The Register, 'Forget retraining - AI can now tweak another AI's weights', 2026-11-03 (opened), reporting MIT CSAIL (Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab) post 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming' (2026-11-02): GPT-6 Astra edited 0.016% of Qwen3.5-0.8B's weights without gradient descent, MATH-500 up to ~6x, IFEval ~3x, reduced reasoning loops. The MIT post itself and the secondary claims (131,072 weights, one direction in layers 15/19 MLPs, Honnibal 'loop fix' criticism, Gemma 3 270M null) were NOT opened and are UNVERIFIED.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1. The positive arm is nearly tautological, not a classifier result. The repo's own PC-4 fact (decode bandwidth-bound at ~23-28 W, near-flat power from 1.5B to 122B-A10B) plus BYTES-LAW mean precision (bytes/weight) and sparsity (active bytes/token) change decode TIME, hence energy, by tens of percent: hundreds of joules on a 2-4 kJ episode against ~2 J replay repeatability. A single scalar (episode duration or total energy) separates the variants; no trace-shape classifier is needed and wall-clock alone leaks the same bit. Reporting 'high accuracy' here adds nothing beyond the BYTES/PRECISION results it rides on. Keep it only as a positive control.

2. The null arm is physically guaranteed under the stated design, so it needs reframing as a bound, with the instrument's role made explicit. A merged MEMIT or abliteration edit preserves tensor shapes, dtype, quant layout, kernel sequence and bytes moved per token, so time and bandwidth-driven energy are identical by construction. The only remaining physical channel is operand-dependent switching power (bit-toggle statistics of weights/activations). Abliteration rewrites whole matrices but preserves the value distribution; MEMIT alters a few MLP matrices by low-rank deltas; the Astra edit touched 0.016% of weights. Any switching-power shift is orders of magnitude below the 0.05% (2 J / 4 kJ) replay floor. Worse: powermetrics gpu_power/gpu_energy are SoC-reported mW / integer-mJ fields at 100 ms (draft-v2-skeleton.md line 987); if these are derived from activity counters and DVFS state rather than current sensing, operand-dependent switching power may not register at all. The result would then be a limit of powermetrics, not of 'power traces'. The USB-C DC meter is a true measurement but whole-machine and noisier. The write-up must say which limit it measured; otherwise 'measured limit of power-trace model identification on consumer hardware' overclaims.

3. False-positive confounds that would make edits look detectable: (a) edited checkpoint re-saved with different dtype/quant/group size or an unmerged LoRA/hook path (extra kernels) - trivially detectable and an artifact, not weight identification; the protocol must re-quantize base and variants through one identical pipeline and hash the layouts; (b) sequential unblinded replays pick up thermal/ordering drift, which a classifier will learn as 'variant' - interleaved, blinded ABBA ordering is mandatory, and the test should be a permutation test / AUC with CI, not raw accuracy; (c) 'fixed tokens' is ambiguous: with teacher-forcing, token identity still differs but bytes do not (negligible); with a max_tokens cap and EOS allowed, lengths differ and the behavioral channel dominates (Astra's edit removed loops, abliteration removes refusals, both change output length). That behavioral channel is the only place the power trace can 'see' an edit, and it is a length/looping effect, not a weight effect.

4. Statistics for the null: predeclare the minimum detectable effect. With sigma ~2 J per replay and n per arm, MDE ~ 2.8*sigma*sqrt(2/n) ~ 1.8 J at n=20, i.e. ~0.05% of a 4 kJ episode; report 'edits indistinguishable to within X J / Y%' with the GPU-rail per-GB anchor (~0.0001 J/GB) as the resolution floor.

5. Cost is not zero. The Mac replays are cheap (k variants x n ~20 x 1-2 min), but producing the variants is engineering: a MEMIT port for Qwen in MLX, abliteration-direction extraction, identical requantization, plus the blinding harness. All of that is off-Mac work and should be sized before unbanking.

**Residual novelty**

Modest but real, if reframed. No opened source measures whether merged weight edits (MEMIT, abliteration, Astra-style surgical edits) are distinguishable from the base model in a device power trace, on any hardware; prior power/EM/system-stat work stops at architecture or family identification, weight recovery exists only on microcontrollers (CSI NN), and the governance literature (Kimpson 2610.07476) bounds compute quantity, not model identity. The defensible contribution is a published bound: 'on Apple silicon with powermetrics at 100 ms and a USB-C DC meter, merged surgical edits are invisible to within X J per episode, while precision/sparsity variants separate by Y J via bytes-per-token alone'. The interesting framing for Ed's interp interest is 'energy sees behavior, not weights': a three-arm design (precision/sparsity positive control; merged edits teacher-forced = expected null with bound; merged edits free-running = detectable only through output length/looping/refusal changes, which must be normalized per token to be more than trivial). Drop the UniGuard link (misattributed), keep Kimpson re-execution and DiFR as the comparison regimes, and state explicitly whether the measured limit is a powermetrics-instrument limit or a physical one. Recommend: stays banked; cheap to run once PRECISION-TAMPER/LOCKIN-SWAP artifacts exist, but the variant-production engineering, not Mac time, is the real cost.

### Opus 5.5 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Xiang et al., 'Open DNN Box by Power Side-Channel Attack', arXiv:1907.10406 (2019, preprint). OPENED. Uses power traces on embedded devices to classify DNN architecture and estimate internal parameters, about 96.5% average accuracy. This is prior art for the 'architecture/variant ID from power' half. The abstract does not mention pruning or sparsity.
- Maia, Xiao, Li, Grinspun, Zheng, 'Can one hear the shape of a neural network? Snooping the GPU via magnetic side channel', USENIX Security 2022, arXiv:2109.07395. OPENED. Recovers layer topology and hyperparameters from the GPU power cable's magnetic field in a single query. It does not claim weight recovery, which matches the expected null for same-shape weight edits.
- Lipp, Kogler, Oswald, Schwarz, Easdon, Canella, Gruss, 'PLATYPUS', IEEE S&P 2021 (platypusattack.com). OPENED. Software-read RAPL told operand Hamming weights apart. Intel's fix switched the energy reports to a model-based estimate, so the same instructions on different data could no longer be told apart. This is the key precedent: a power counter built from a model is blind to the data being processed.
- Wang, Paccagnella, He, Shacham, Fletcher, Kohlbrenner, 'Hertzbleed', USENIX Security 2022; follow-up 'DVFS Frequently Leaks Secrets...', IEEE S&P 2023 (hertzbleed.com). OPENED. Data-dependent power changes DVFS frequency and therefore timing on x86. Arm 'may be affected'. Apple was not confirmed.
- H. He, 'Strangely, Matrix Multiplications on GPUs Run Faster When Given Predictable Data' (thonking.ai blog, 2024). OPENED. On an A100 power-capped matmul, all-zero inputs ran at 295 TFLOPS and randn inputs at 257, because switching power depends on data values. This sets a rough ceiling on value-only power effects. It is the upper bound for unstructured-sparsity contrasts, not for small edits.
- ml-energy/zeus-apple-silicon README (github.com/ml-energy/zeus-apple-silicon). OPENED. It says IOReport energy values are 'believed to be model-based estimates' derived from utilization, frequency and voltage. It cites the powermetrics man page: values are 'estimated and may be inaccurate'. The repo already records this at docs/phase_4/related_work_draft.md:258 and docs/research_question_bank.md:1876.
- Kimpson et al. (power-trace re-execution regime). UNVERIFIED. The web search budget was exhausted. The paper is absent from the repo (grep for 'kimpson' over /Users/edr/code/CapstoneProject/jw-wt/handoff finds nothing). Not opened; do not cite until verified.
- UniGuard. UNVERIFIED. The only UniGuard I know of is a multimodal-LLM jailbreak guardrail (2024), which is unrelated to power traces. It could not be opened or confirmed to be the intended reference.
- Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights' research. NOT FOUND. The search budget was exhausted before any query ran. The repo knows 'gpt-6-astra' only as a council/Codex model seat (docs/decision_log.md D-184, D-186). Nothing in the repo describes a weight-editing capability. Treat the claim as unsourced.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1) THE RAILS ARE A MODEL, NOT A SENSOR. powermetrics/IOReport GPU-rail energy is a model-based estimate built from utilization, frequency and voltage (Zeus README, powermetrics man page). Two variants that run the same kernels on same-shaped tensors at the same DVFS state will therefore give matching rail energy in expectation. This holds by construction, whatever the weight values are. So 'chance level for surgical edits' on the GPU rail is not a measured limit of power-trace identification. It is a property of Apple's estimator, exactly the PLATYPUS lesson. Only the USB-C DC-input meter sees real switching physics. A rail result must be reported as an estimator limit, not a physics limit, or reviewers will call it circular.

2) THE 'HIGH ACCURACY' HALF IS TRIVIAL. Precision variants (bf16, 8-bit, 4-bit) change kernels, bytes moved and wall time by tens of percent. The anchors resolve rail contrasts to about 0.0001 J/GB, and a stopwatch or token-latency trace alone separates them with no power data. Structured pruning changes shapes and is just as trivial. A classifier at about 100% adds nothing beyond Xiang 2019 and Maia 2022 unless it is restricted to a feature set with timing removed. The only non-trivial sparsity case is unstructured sparsity run through dense MLX kernels: same kernels, same bytes, only the values differ. There the rails should see nothing (model-based). The DC meter might see a few percent at high sparsity; the A100 zero-vs-randn result suggests about 10-15% at the extreme.

3) THE SIZE OF A SURGICAL-EDIT EFFECT IS FAR BELOW THE FLOOR. Under teacher-forced fixed tokens, MEMIT or abliteration leave graph, shapes, bytes and kernels unchanged. Only the bit statistics of the edited matrices move. Abliteration removes about one direction in d (about 4096), roughly 2e-4 of the weight energy. MEMIT touches about 5 of 30+ layers with a small-norm rank-one update. After requantization the 4-bit code distribution is essentially unchanged. A generous estimate for the DC-meter effect: data-dependent switching share of about 0.1-0.2 × edited-parameter share of about 0.05-0.33 × toggle-statistic change of about 1e-3 or less. That gives about 1e-5 to 5e-5 relative, or about 0.03-0.15 J on a 3 kJ episode. Replays repeat to about 2 J, so the effect is 10-60× below single-replay noise. Resolving it would need roughly 10^3-10^4 paired replays, so a null is guaranteed and uninformative unless it is framed as an equivalence upper bound with a stated minimum detectable effect.

4) CONFOUNDS THAT WOULD FAKE A POSITIVE. (a) Without strict teacher forcing, an edit changes the tokens: abliteration flips refusals, so lengths and stop tokens differ. Energy then 'identifies' the edit through behavior, not power. (b) In MoE models (Flash-Next/qwen MoE), an edit that moves the residual stream changes router choices even at fixed tokens. That changes which experts are read and their cache locality, which is a real bytes/time signal. It is a legitimate, interesting channel, but it is not 'weights seen via power' and it must be separated. (c) Requantizing an edited model can change group scales or layout and alter the kernel path; byte-identical layout must be verified. (d) DVFS or thermal state, background daemons and battery charge flows at the DC meter (battery must sit at the charge limit) are larger than the effect. Blinding and an ABBA order are required.

5) THE THREAT MODEL IS WEAK. Anyone who can read powermetrics on the host can hash the weights, which is exact and free. Power-based ID matters only for a verifier who does not trust the host, and that verifier cannot trust host-reported powermetrics either; it would need its own physical meter. The 'consumer hardware model-ID limit' claim needs a threat model, or it reads as a solution looking for a problem.

6) FEASIBILITY OF THE BANKED PLAN IS FINE. Running it as desk analysis over PRECISION-TAMPER/LOCKIN-SWAP replays costs no extra Mac time. Neither experiment exists in the repo yet (grep finds no PRECISION-TAMPER or LOCKIN-SWAP), so 'follows from' is contingent.

**Residual novelty**

Not refuted, because no opened source tests LLM weight-edit or precision identification from Apple-silicon power. As stated, though, the claim's two halves are respectively trivial (precision and structured variants separate on timing alone) and true by construction (the GPU rails are an activity-based model, so same-kernel edits cannot appear on them). The defensible residual is a reframed and much sharper result: a rails-vs-wall value-sensitivity test.

Hold kernels, shapes, bytes and tokens fixed (teacher-forced) and vary only weight values along a graded ladder: random → unstructured 50/90% zeros through dense kernels → abliteration/MEMIT → a bit-identical control. Then ask: (i) does the physical DC-input meter resolve value-dependent energy on Apple-silicon GPUs while the powermetrics rails stay flat? That would be a PLATYPUS-style demonstration that Apple's rails are data-blind, and a direct validity statement about the instrument the whole field uses on Macs. (ii) What equivalence upper bound, in J per episode and as a relative share, can be put on any surgical edit's energy footprint?

The ladder gives a dose-response from the large-effect end (zeros) down to the null end (edits), so the edit null comes with a demonstrated sensitivity rather than being uninformative. An optional MoE arm tests whether edits are visible through router-induced expert/byte shifts rather than through power physics. This links Ed's interp interest to measurement validity: surgical edits change capability without changing compute, and the instrument limit lies in Apple's estimator, not in the physics. A cheap synthetic matmul microbenchmark of zeros vs random values at fixed shape could run first to establish whether the DC meter has any value sensitivity above about 2 J before any model-edit work. Cited references to Kimpson et al., UniGuard and the 'GPT-6 Astra edits weights' claim remain unverified or unfound (the web search budget was exhausted) and should not appear in the bank until checked.

---

## 10. S1: Apple-GPU data-dependent energy ladder (M4 Air desk microbenchmark) (seat 2 (Opus))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Taneja et al., 'Hot Pixels: Frequency, Power, and Temperature Attacks on GPUs and ARM SoCs', USENIX Security 2023, arXiv 2305.12784 (OPENED, ar5iv full text). Closest prior work. On M1 Air / M2 Air iGPUs an OpenCL bitwise-AND kernel swept Hamming weight 0-32: power r=-0.963 and frequency r=-0.961 with HW on the M1 Air; a shift kernel swept Hamming distance: power r=-0.971, freq r=-0.951. Sign is NEGATIVE on the passively cooled Air (higher HW -> more heat -> throttling -> lower freq and lower power), positive on fan-cooled Pro/Mini. Steady-state M1 Air iGPU ran at 0.89-0.96 GHz / 5.1-5.4 W at 92 C vs 1.28 GHz / 10.5-12.1 W on Pro/Mini. Read via SocPowerBuddy (IOReport), no sudo. No GEMM, matvec or any ML kernel on any device; no percentages, only correlations. So 'Apple GPU power depends on operand statistics' is already established qualitatively; the GEMM/quantized-matvec ladder magnitudes and the statistics-preserving nulls are not.
- Chawla et al., 'Uncovering Software-Based Power Side-Channel Attacks on Apple M1/M2 Systems', arXiv 2306.16391 (2023, rev. 2024) (OPENED, abstract only). Unprivileged SMC power keys ('on-chip power meters'), AES key recovery, plus a throttling side channel. No GPU matmul. Relevant because SMC keys may be a current-sensed readout rather than the IOReport activity model.
- Wang et al., 'GPU.zip: On the Side-Channel Implications of Hardware-Based Graphical Data Compression', IEEE S&P 2024, hertzbleed.com/gpu.zip (OPENED). Software-transparent, data-dependent lossless compression found on integrated GPUs from AMD, Apple, Arm, Intel, Qualcomm. A confound for the all-zero / constant arms if it reaches MTLBuffer traffic (the paper's targets are render targets; unverified for compute buffers).
- Wang et al., 'Hertzbleed', USENIX Security 2022 and 'DVFS Frequently Leaks Secrets', IEEE S&P 2023, hertzbleed.com (OPENED). Data-dependent power -> DVFS frequency on x86; the 2023 follow-up shows CPU frequency leaking GPU computation. Establishes the power->frequency mediation the Air will exhibit.
- Batina, Bhasin, Jap, Picek, 'CSI NN', USENIX Security 2019 (OPENED, abstract page). Network weights recovered from EM/timing of a Cortex-M3 running inference: the direct ancestor of 'weight values are visible in the power trace'. Microcontroller, not GPU.
- ml-energy/zeus-apple-silicon README (OPENED). IOReport 'Energy Model' channels: CPU, GPU, GPU SRAM, ANE, DRAM (newer chips), 1 mJ, no sudo; values 'believed to be model-based estimates derived from utilization, frequency, and voltage'; sub-10 ms windows quantization-dominated. Tested on M4.
- JouleWise repo: docs/process_traces/2026-10-31-magistrate-5cbfbcfc/KB-SLOT-FILL.md (OPENED) gives the anchors the idea cites (GPU rail delta 0.00879 +/- 0.00010 J/GB; GPU-rail contrast 85% stall time, only 0.144 W power rise; DC-input half-width 0.0072 J/GB, ~70x coarser than the GPU rail). docs/research_question_bank.md:1876-1883 already rules an IOReport-vs-powermetrics cross-check arm into the Phase 0 desk day with the caveat 'both paths are Apple's model'. docs/handoff/2026-11-06-v4-and-port/SATURDAY-OPERATOR-CARD.md:83 names the DC meter (KM003C) on the measurement MBP; nothing in the repo places a KM003C on the M4 Air.
- UNVERIFIED: the idea's 'A100 literature reports 38-67% for GEMM' operand-dependence figure. Five arXiv site-search queries (data-dependent GPU power; 'Hamming weight' GPU power; data-dependent energy GPU; data-dependent power tensor core; GPU power random data zeros matmul) returned no matching paper, and the shared WebSearch budget was exhausted before this task's first query. The practitioner observation (random vs zero operands on NVIDIA GEMM draws very different power; gpu-burn and MLPerf Power use real/random data for that reason) is real but I could not open a citable source for the 38-67% number; treat it as unanchored until someone supplies the reference.
- NOT FOUND: Ed's reference 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights'. Web search was unavailable, a direct fetch of openai.com/index/gpt-6/ returned 403, and I have no record of such a release or paper. I cannot confirm it exists; the design should not cite it until located.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1) The readout may not see the physics. IOReport GPU energy and powermetrics rails are, per Zeus's README and Apple's man page, model-based estimates from utilization, frequency and voltage. A zero-operand GEMM and a randn GEMM at the same frequency and occupancy could report IDENTICAL IOReport energy while the real toggle power differs, or the model could show the effect only after it leaks into the DVFS/throttle state. Hot Pixels' Apple results do not settle this: its HW correlation on the Air is mediated by throttling. Consequence: the GPU-rail/IOReport positive control cannot be the GO criterion on its own. The DC-input meter is the physics arbiter, and the repo places the KM003C on the MBP, not the Air; the design must either move the meter to the Air for the run or run the positive-control pair on the MBP first. Conversely this makes a second, cheap finding available: 'does Apple's energy model respond to operand statistics at fixed frequency?' (fits the existing IOReport cross-check arm at research_question_bank.md:1876). 2) Thermal regime inverts the sign and confounds E/op. Hot Pixels measured the M1 Air iGPU steady state at 5.1-5.4 W / 0.9 GHz at 92 C. A 4096^2 bf16 GEMM burst at 15-20 W will put the M4 Air in a thermal cap within minutes even at 30% duty; in that regime data-dependence appears as throughput, and E/op mixes the toggle effect with the V^2 benefit of the lower DVFS point, so a high-HW arm can come out CHEAPER per op (the Hot Pixels sign). Mitigation: PRBS blocks short enough (<= 1 s) that arms share thermal state, E/op reported conditional on GPU frequency bins (frequency needs sudo powermetrics; IOReport has none), a <= 10% duty or reduced-occupancy variant that stays at max frequency, and an actively cooled MBP replication of at least the two extreme arms. The GO rule should include 'frequency residency equal across arms within the swap'. 3) bf16 resolution makes the smallest surgical arm a null by construction. bf16 has 7 mantissa bits (relative ULP 2^-8 = 0.0039). A rank-one perturbation with ||dW||/||W|| = 1e-3 leaves most elements bit-identical after rounding, so that arm is an A/A', not a weight edit; 1e-2 flips LSBs of a minority of elements; only 1e-1 is a real multi-bit edit. Say so, or run the surgical arms in fp32/fp16 as well. Also 'int4 code permutation with the same histogram' must mean a position permutation (preserves Hamming weight) and not a code relabeling (which does not unless popcount-preserving). 4) The memory-bound arm is on the wrong boundary. DRAM I/O energy (bus toggling, already DBI-limited on LPDDR5X) sits on the DRAM/rest side, not the GPU rail; the stream test found 85% of the GPU-rail contrast was stall time and only 0.144 W power rise, and the DC half-width (0.0072 J/GB) is ~70x the GPU-rail one. 'Less than GEMM' will be a bound, not a measurement, in 6-10 h. Read the IOReport DRAM channel (available on M4 per Zeus) and the DC meter, and make the weight ring >= 1 GB of distinct matrices: a single 4096x14336 int4 matrix (~29 MB) repeatedly re-read is partly SLC-resident and latency-bound, as the 32 MiB dispatches in KB-SLOT-FILL were. 5) All-zero/constant arms have non-toggle shortcuts: GPU.zip-style compression on Apple iGPUs (unverified for compute buffers), cache/zero-line effects, and possible hardware operand isolation; any arm that also runs faster must be flagged as 'not a pure power effect'. The sorted-rows arm is the useful same-histogram control (Hamming distance vs weight) and should be kept. 6) Noise-floor arithmetic is plausible but transferred. The 0.1%/h GPU-rail floor comes from the M3 Max with sudo powermetrics under custody conditions; the Air with IOReport only, passive cooling, battery top-off cycles (log B0AC/charging state, as the stream test did) and no DC meter has no measured floor. The A/A' GO test is the right first step; expect the floor to be set by thermal drift, not by the 1 mJ counter. 7) Framing for Ed's interp interest: the surgical-arm effect is predicted at <= 1e-5 and can only be bounded at ~1e-3, whereas a weight edit that changes output by one token changes task energy by ~0.1-1%. Energy effects of weight edits are mediated by behavior (tokens, routing, stop reason), not by bit toggles; S1 is the clean negative that licenses ignoring the toggle channel. 8) Cost is understated if the DC meter and MBP replication are required: add one MBP desk session; still 0 Mac-nights.

**Residual novelty**

Not refuted. Hot Pixels establishes only that an Apple iGPU's power/frequency depends on Hamming weight for a trivial AND kernel, with the sign set by throttling; no source characterizes operand-statistics energy for bf16 GEMM or MLX 4-bit matvec on any Apple GPU, and the statistics-preserving null ladder (rank-one, sign flips, position permutations, outliers) has no precedent I could find. What remains novel and worth running: (a) the magnitude ladder for compute-bound bf16 GEMM on an Apple GPU at matched frequency, with the DC meter as arbiter; (b) the bound on statistics-preserving weight edits (the result Ed's interp question needs, framed as 'weight-edit energy effects are behavioral, not electrical'); (c) a by-product the project can publish within its instrument-validation line: whether IOReport/powermetrics energy models respond to operand toggling at fixed frequency at all, which decides whether Apple's software meters can ever see this class of effect. Redesign before building: short PRBS swaps, frequency-binned E/op, <= 10% duty or an MBP replication, DC meter on the Air, fp32 companions for the surgical arms, >= 1 GB weight ring for the matvec, and drop the unanchored 38-67% A100 citation until a source is found.

### Opus 5.5 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Gregersen, Patel, Choukse, 'Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs', arXiv:2409.18324 (2024). Opened (abs + HTML). A100 PCIe, with FP16 generalization to H100, V100 and RTX 6000. 2048x2048 CUTLASS GEMM in FP32, FP16, FP16-tensor-core and INT8; DCGM power at 100 ms. Power changes by up to 38% across value distribution, bit similarity, sorting/placement, sparsity and LSB/MSB zeroing. Sparsity applied to sorted matrices does not compound; for FP types, power peaks at 30-40% sparsity. The proposed mechanism is bit flips, and the paper presents it as a hypothesis. This substantially answers the NVIDIA question, and S1's arm list (sorted rows, unstructured zeros, Hamming weight, outliers) largely repeats its taxonomy. No Apple or integrated GPU is tested.
- thonking.ai, 'Strangely, Matrix Multiplications on GPUs Run Faster When Given Predictable Data' (blog). Opened. On a power-capped A100, zeros reach 295 TFLOPS against 257 for randn (about 15%). The gap grows as the power limit drops, inverts at 100 W, and vanishes at low fixed clocks. The effect therefore appears through the power cap and DVFS: it shows up in time, not only in power.
- Taneja et al., 'Hot Pixels: Frequency, Power, and Temperature Attacks on GPUs and ARM SoCs', arXiv:2305.12784 (USENIX Sec 2023). Opened (HTML). On the Apple M1 Air GPU, an AND kernel's steady-state power and frequency both fall as Hamming weight rises (r about -0.96); a shift kernel's Hamming distance correlates with power at about -0.97; the M2 Air shows similar trends. Telemetry came from IOReport via a modified SocPowerBuddy at 10 ms, with no sudo. On the passively cooled Air these effects appear under thermal limiting (about 93 C). On the fan-cooled MBP, a CPU +0 vs +1 test shows only 12.83 vs 12.86 W at unchanged frequency. The paper gives no magnitudes in percent, and the Appendix A CPU Hamming-weight model found no correlation. Matmul is not covered.
- PLATYPUS (platypusattack.com). Opened. Intel RAPL readings showed operand Hamming-weight dependence while they were measurement-based. Intel's fix switched RAPL to a model-based mode in which identical instructions with different data cannot be told apart. This is the key precedent: a vendor energy counter sees operand data only if it is backed by a measurement.
- ml-energy/zeus-apple-silicon README (GitHub). Opened. 'IOReport energy values are believed to be model-based estimates', and it cites powermetrics' own 'estimated and may be inaccurate'. The repo's docs/council_log.md:317 likewise calls powermetrics 'vendor-MODELED rail telemetry'.
- In-repo evidence: KB-SLOT-FILL.md:21,76 (M3 Max known-bytes test). 85% of the resolved GPU-rail contrast is the same ~10 W drawn for longer (stall time), and the only sizeable power rise (1.63 W) shows up in 'rest' (DC minus rails). The GPU rail behaves like activity x time, and the traffic-dependent energy sits off-rail.
- UNVERIFIED (not opened): 'Chawla et al.' (Apple CPU AES via SMC keys), cited in the idea. The '38-67%' A100 range: I could confirm only 38% (Gregersen), and the source of 67% is unknown. Hertzbleed and Collide+Power (data-dependent DVFS and power on x86) are relevant but not opened.
- Ed's reference 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights': I could not find it. The web-search budget ran out before the query ran. In this repo 'Astra' is the project's own GPT-6 delegated-seat name (docs/process/automation_history_2026-09-16.md:182; research_plan_2026-09-16.md), not a published result. I found no paper and make no claim about it.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1. The rail may be blind by construction, and this is the main attack. IOReport and powermetrics GPU energy is widely believed to be modeled from activity and DVFS state: Apple says 'estimated', Zeus says 'model-based', and the repo's council log says 'vendor-MODELED'. If the estimator is counters x V/f coefficients, operand statistics at a fixed DVFS state are invisible on the GPU rail by construction. In PLATYPUS, Intel's model mode erased exactly this signal. The data dependence Hot Pixels saw on Apple came through thermal DVFS on the Air (data -> heat -> frequency -> modeled power), not from switching energy at a fixed operating point. The M3 Max KB test points the same way: the GPU rail resolved stall time, and the power rise was in 'rest'. Consequence: a null on the rail cannot tell 'Apple GPU has no data-dependent energy' from 'the rail cannot see it'. Fix: make the USB-C DC-input meter the arbiter for the positive control (all-zero vs randn). Pre-register three outcomes: rail and DC both move (measured or partly measured), DC moves and rail null (modeled rail; this is itself a publishable instrument finding), or both null. The GO rule as written ('positive control reaches 0.5% on the rail') would wrongly kill the study in case two.

2. The DC floor is roughly 70x worse than the GPU-rail floor (KB: DC ±0.0072 vs GPU ±0.0001 J/GB per hour). The '10x the 0.1%/h rail floor' arithmetic does not hold if DC is the only valid boundary. The GPU share of Air DC power under GEMM is perhaps 50-70%, so a 0.5-1% GPU effect is about 0.3-0.7% at DC. Against the 2 J / 2-4 kJ replay repeat (0.05-0.1%), that is a 3-10x margin for a large positive control, and only if the Air's DC is clean. It is not clean today. The Air is the 24/7 analysis and agent box (Sol, Claude, Spotlight, photoanalysisd), and the KB test already caught daemon bursts up to 5 W on a quiet Mac. Ed's own point applies: on a multitasking machine, DC power is the hardest signal to pull out. PRBS in-process interleaving cancels slow drift but not bursty, correlated CPU load. Run on a quiesced Air (agents stopped, Spotlight and photoanalysis suspended, display off or fixed), and put a CPU-rail contention gate on each PRBS slot.

3. The passively cooled Air turns energy into frequency. Once the Air nears its thermal limit, low-switching data produces higher frequency rather than lower power (Hot Pixels; thonking's power-cap inversion), and J/op then mixes switching energy with the V/f curve. A 30% duty cycle helps but does not guarantee a fixed state. Log GPU frequency and residency per slot, analyze only slots whose frequency matches the reference to 0.1%, and report time/op alongside J/op. Apple exposes no GPU clock lock (Hot Pixels). The 'energy per op' claim also needs the time path controlled: MLX may short-circuit or a different kernel path may handle all-zero or constant inputs.

4. The arms may not exercise the datapath the A100 intuition assumes. Apple GPUs have no tensor cores. Whether M4 has native bf16 ALUs or MLX up-converts bf16 to fp32 in registers is uncertain: verify the kernel MLX actually dispatches, and add an fp16 arm. The A100 effect lives largely in the tensor-core datapath, so a 0.5% prior for Apple is a guess, not a scaled estimate.

5. The 4-bit matvec arm is mostly DRAM I/O. Its data-dependent energy is DRAM bus toggling, which the KB test places in 'rest' (DC only), not on the GPU rail. LPDDR5X data-bus inversion further limits Hamming-weight sensitivity. The expected effect (a few mJ/GB out of about 0.04 J/GB of I/O energy) is at or below the DC floor of ±0.007 J/GB per hour. A likely null is fine, but the claim's 'by less' cannot be resolved as a positive number.

6. Artifacts. All-zero and constant buffers must be explicitly written and resident: zero-page or compressed-memory backing, or a zero fast path, would remove DRAM traffic and fake a large effect. Arms must use identical dispatch geometry and kernel selection, so check the kernel name and grid per arm. The rank-one, sign-flip and code-permutation 'surgical' arms are predicted at 1e-5 or below, more than 100x under any floor here, so they can only be bounded and cannot serve as nulls that test anything. The A/A' and content-swap nulls are the real controls.

7. Hard-coded anchors: '38-67%' is only half verified (38%), and the '0.1%/h GPU floor' is from the fan-cooled M3 Max (consistent with KB:21: about 8 J on about 7.6 kJ), not the Air. Measure the Air's own A/A' floor first.

8. The cost is plausible (about 1 Air-day plus 6-10 h runs), but it collides with the Air's role as the 24/7 analysis machine through about 11-09. Schedule it in a quiesced window, not alongside agent work.

**Residual novelty**

Nothing I found characterizes data-dependent energy for Apple GPU matmul or quantized matvec. Prior work covers NVIDIA GEMM thoroughly (Gregersen: up to 38%) and Apple GPU only for scalar AND and shift kernels under thermal throttling (Hot Pixels). So S1 is not refuted, but its arm taxonomy is mostly a port of Gregersen's. The most defensible new contribution is different from the stated claim. First, an instrument-validity result: does Apple's IOReport/powermetrics GPU rail see operand-dependent switching energy at all, at a matched DVFS state, judged against an independent DC-input meter? A 'DC moves, rail null' outcome would show that the rail is modeled, as in PLATYPUS, and would directly bound what any rail-based 'surgical weight edit changes power' interpretability study on a Mac can detect. Second, a positive-control magnitude for zero, sparse and sorted bf16/fp16 GEMM on M4 at DC, which is new but probably small. The surgical arms (rank-one, sign flips, code permutations) are predicted 100x or more below every floor, so they add only upper bounds. Recommend reframing S1 as 'Can a Mac see data-dependent compute energy? Rail vs DC on the M4 Air', with DC as the arbiter, a frequency-matched slot analysis and a quiesced Air.

---

## 11. PRBS lock-in by in-process weight swapping (instrument method) (seat 2 (Opus))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming: LLMs can natively read & write neural network weights', blog post 2026-11-02, https://rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/ (OPENED). This is Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights' reference: Astra edited 131,072 of Qwen3.5-0.8B's weights (0.016%, MLPs of layers 15 and 19, one direction), MATH-500 7.9% -> 48.1%, GSM8K 13.8% -> 67.1%, share of MATH-500 answers hitting the 32,768-token limit 93.0% -> 33.1%. No energy, power, latency or efficiency measurement of the edited model; no arXiv paper, only logs, an HF checkpoint and a demo.
- Goedecke, 'LLMs are not superhuman at reading and writing neural network weights', https://www.seangoedecke.com/llms-are-not-superhuman-at-reading-and-writing-neural-network-weights/ (OPENED): the Qwen fix resembles steering/abliteration; no efficiency or energy discussion. Secondary coverage (mindstudio.ai, emergent.sh, cellcog.ai, explainx.ai, techjacksolutions.com) UNVERIFIED, not opened.
- Gregersen, Patel, Choukse, 'Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs', arXiv:2409.18324, https://arxiv.org/html/2409.18324 (OPENED): same-shape GEMMs on an A100 (DCGM at 100 ms; also H100, V100, RTX 6000) change power by up to 38% with input value distribution, bit similarity, placement and sparsity; hypothesised mechanism is bit-flip count. This is the closest prior answer to 'do weight values change power': yes at kernel level, on NVIDIA, with 100% of values changed, no end-to-end LLM result, no surgical-edit fraction, no Apple silicon.
- Hertzbleed, https://www.hertzbleed.com/ (OPENED): data-dependent power feeds DVFS frequency on x86 (Intel 8th-11th gen, Zen 2/3; Arm 'may be affected'; Apple silicon not mentioned). Establishes that operand-dependent power exists and couples into frequency, i.e. a weight-value contrast can appear as a time term as well as a power term.
- Chuss, 'A Software-Based Lock-In Measurement for Student Laboratories', arXiv:1711.00997, https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.00997 (abstract OPENED; PDF not text-extractable): software lock-in on recorded reference and detector time streams, sensitivity improved by orders of magnitude over unmodulated measurement. Zurich Instruments application note on cross-correlation noise reduction, https://www.zhinst.com/sites/default/files/zi_appnote_mfli_cross_correlation.pdf (UNVERIFIED, not opened). PRBS / m-sequence perturbation for system identification is textbook (Godfrey, 'Perturbation Signals for System Identification', 1993 - UNVERIFIED, not opened). None of these applies the method to software energy measurement.
- Hahnel et al., 'RAPL in Action' (ACM TOMPECS 2018), https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3177754 (NOT OPENED, HTTP 403 - UNVERIFIED): search snippet reports RAPL package power correlates with temperature; relevant to the drift argument but unconfirmed here.
- In-repo: docs/research-audits/2026-11-07/methodology-vs-literature.md at commit 036c7402d (read via git show; the path is not checked out in the handoff clone). Line 28 and the idea-5 row (line 304) give the 0.08-0.25 %/degC leakage figure as a 'K20 fit', i.e. a discrete NVIDIA GPU, not Apple silicon. Lines 159, 880 and 965 already recommend randomising A/B/B/A orientation; A/A null quads already exist in ideas 1, 5, 6 and 9. docs/process_traces/2026-10-31-magistrate-5cbfbcfc/KB-SLOT-FILL.md confirms the anchors: GPU rail 0.00879 +/- 0.00010 J/GB, rails +/- 0.0021, DC +/- 0.0072, GPU frequency 1379.6 MHz in both arms, 85% of the GPU-rail contrast is a time term (the GPU stays powered while waiting on memory).
- Search budget note: the shared WebSearch budget for this session ran out after three queries; the specific queries 'PRBS/lock-in applied to RAPL or powermetrics' and 'energy of ROME/MEMIT-edited models' were not run. I found no prior work doing in-process PRBS weight swapping with lock-in demodulation of power, but that absence is not fully established.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1. THE ANCHOR IS ALREADY ABBA AT THE CLAIMED SENSITIVITY. The GPU-rail half-width of +/-1.05e-4 J/GB (+/-0.011 W on ~10 W) in the ~1 h R,C,C,R known-bytes session IS a four-block A/B/B/A result, i.e. the existing method already reaches ~0.1% (2 sigma) per hour on the GPU rail. The idea's own target, 'about 0.1% per sqrt(hour)', therefore shows no gain. A lock-in beats blocks only to the extent the block interval is drift-limited rather than white-noise-limited, and nobody has measured the noise power spectral density of the powermetrics GPU rail or the KM003C during steady decode. That PSD (where the 1/f knee sits relative to 0.25-1 Hz chip rates) decides whether the instrument helps at all. It can be measured for free from existing non-restricted traces or on the Air before any MBP time is spent; the proposal should be gated on it.

2. THE THERMAL-MISMATCH JUSTIFICATION IS NUMERICALLY NEGLIGIBLE AT THE TARGET. A 1% contrast on a 10 W GPU rail is 0.1 W. With a laptop die-to-ambient resistance of order 0.5-1 K/W that is a 0.05-0.1 K arm-to-arm temperature difference, and at 0.08-0.25 %/degC (a K20 number, not Apple silicon) the leakage term is 0.004-0.025% of power, i.e. a 0.4-2.5% relative bias on a 1% contrast and far below the 0.1% floor. ABBA already cancels linear drift exactly. So the headline mechanism for beating 750 s blocks is weak; the realistic win is against slow quadratic drift and bursty daemons, and only on boundaries where those dominate (rails +/-0.22 W and DC +/-0.77 W, not the GPU rail at +/-0.011 W).

3. DAEMON NOISE IS NOT BROADBAND. photoanalysisd/corespotlightd episodes last seconds to minutes, so their spectrum has power inside the 0.25-1 Hz PRBS band. A PRBS reference averages the noise PSD across its band; it does not reject in-band bursts. Expect the DC-boundary improvement to be a factor of a few at most, not the 10x implied by the 'uncorrelated variance' framing, and it has to be demonstrated with the measured PSD.

4. SAMPLING-GEOMETRY DUTY LOSS. powermetrics delivers ~130 ms records unaligned to swap timestamps. Dropping edge records costs ~50% of samples at 0.5 s chips and ~13% at 2 s chips; the KM003C at 50 Hz loses little but has the worse floor. Short chips, the ones best placed above the drift knee, pay the largest duty penalty; this trade must be computed, not asserted. IOReport energy counters may be the right witness here but their cadence and resolution on this macOS build are unverified (the methodology doc notes macmon's warning about counters stopping on macOS 27).

5. SWITCHING-TRANSIENT BIAS THE PROPOSED NULLS CANNOT SEE. If the arms differ in load, each swap can trigger a GPU DVFS/power-gating transition; a transient of tens of ms per edge is a bias proportional to 1/T_chip. The A/A' null has no load difference and the content-swap null is symmetric, so neither detects it. Add a chip-length sweep (0.5, 1, 2, 4 s) and require the demodulated contrast to be flat; a slope is the edge bias. Hertzbleed shows power-to-frequency coupling is real on x86; Apple silicon is unmeasured.

6. THE CONTENT CONFOUND IS THE BIGGEST SCIENCE PROBLEM. Swapping weights mid-stream in a free-running decode makes a hybrid model whose token stream and KV cache depend on which arm was active at each step; the 'contrast' then mixes weight-value power with content and sequence-length effects, and carryover through the KV cache is not present in ABBA. To measure the power of edited weights the design must teacher-force an identical token sequence (forward passes only), which isolates weight-value and kernel effects but says nothing about performance. Performance and power cannot come from one lock-in run; they are two experiments.

7. SURGICAL EDITS ARE BELOW ANY MAC FLOOR. The Astra edit changed 0.016% of weights. The only mechanism by which weight VALUES change per-token power at fixed work is data-dependent switching (bit flips): Gregersen et al. see up to 38% when 100% of GEMM values change between extreme distributions on an A100. Scaling by the changed fraction gives an expected relative GPU-power effect of order 1e-5 to 1e-4 (0.001-0.01%) for an Astra-scale edit, one to two orders of magnitude under the 0.1%/sqrt(h) floor; 3 sigma at 0.01% needs of order 1,000 h. The proposal's own 'at or below 0.01% infeasible' line concedes this. Worse, on Apple silicon decode is bandwidth-bound: 85% of the known-bytes GPU-rail contrast was a time term and the GPU mostly waits on memory, so ALU switching is a small share of the GPU rail, and weight-bit toggling on the LPDDR5 interface lands in 'rest'/DC, where the floor is 70x worse. Edits that ARE detectable (whole-layer re-quantisation, head/expert pruning, structured sparsity) change bytes moved or kernels by percent-level amounts, and ABBA already resolves those.

8. THE LARGE WEIGHT-EDIT ENERGY EFFECT IS BEHAVIOURAL, NOT PER-TOKEN. In the Astra result the share of answers running to the 32k cap fell from 93% to 33%; the per-task energy effect of an edit is dominated by generated length and looping (several-fold), measurable with ordinary per-request energy and no lock-in. That is where 'surgical edits change power AND performance' is actually testable on this Mac.

9. IMPLEMENTATION: feasible in MLX (parameter references swapped between mx.eval points, no mx.compile of the model, host-side swap timestamps lead GPU execution by milliseconds, resident duplicate tensors are small for surgical edits; for whole-layer edits a few hundred MB). SLC effects are negligible because decode streams all weights from DRAM every step. No blocker here; the concerns are 1-8.

**Residual novelty**

Narrow. (a) No prior work found that applies PRBS/lock-in demodulation to software energy measurement by in-process tensor swapping; the instrument is new as an instrument, but its claimed advantage over A/B/B/A is unsupported until the noise PSD of the powermetrics GPU rail, IOReport and the KM003C under steady decode is measured (free, from existing public traces or the Air; the first deliverable, and a go/no-go gate for any MBP time). (b) If the PSD is red at the DC/rest boundary, a lock-in comparator for DC and rest contrasts (where daemons and bursty load dominate) is a real contribution; on the GPU rail it is not. (c) The chip-length-sweep null for switching-transient bias is a methodological addition worth keeping whatever happens to the instrument. (d) For Ed's interp interest, the publishable experiment is not per-token power of surgical edits (expected 0.001-0.01%, undetectable) but a two-part study: per-task energy of weight-space-programmed models driven by behaviour change (length, looping, reasoning tokens), which nobody has measured and which ordinary per-request energy with A/A nulls resolves; plus teacher-forced per-token energy only for edits that change bytes moved or kernels (re-quantisation, pruning, expert removal), where ABBA already suffices. Bank the lock-in instrument behind the PSD measurement; file the weight-edit energy study as its own entry in the future-research docs.

### Opus 5.5 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Gregersen, Patel, Choukse, 'Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs', arXiv 2409.18324 (2024), opened (https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18324 and ar5iv HTML). Changing GEMM input values at fixed shapes moved power by up to about 40% on an A100, rechecked on H100, V100 and RTX 6000. Power was read through DCGM at 100 ms and averaged over 10 seeds with 10k-20k iterations. The study has no lock-in or interleaving, no Apple hardware, and does not test edits to a small fraction of entries. It establishes that weight content can change power; it does not cover the instrument.
- Taneja et al., 'Hot Pixels: Frequency, Power, and Temperature Attacks on GPUs and Arm SoCs', USENIX Security 2023, pp. 6275-6292, opened (USENIX page and ar5iv 2305.12784). IOReport power on an M1 MacBook Air/Pro, including the iGPU, correlates with operand Hamming weight (r about -0.96) and Hamming distance (r about -0.97). Averages are steady-state over about 20 s, reported as correlations without watt deltas. This is the key evidence that Apple's IOReport/powermetrics GPU channel responds to data content at all; it is not shown on the M3 Max.
- Cina et al., 'Energy-Latency Attacks via Sponge Poisoning', Information Sciences 702 (2025), arXiv 2203.08147, opened (abstract page only). Training-time weight changes that raise energy and latency by cutting activation sparsity on accelerators. This is the closest 'weight edit changes energy' work, but it is not surgical, not Apple, and has no lock-in.
- Wang et al., 'GPU.zip', IEEE S&P 2024, opened (hertzbleed.com/gpu.zip). Data-dependent hardware compression on Apple and other iGPUs, which is graphics data, not compute buffers. Relevant only as a warning that buffer content can change DRAM traffic.
- Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Weight-Space Programming' blog post, opened (https://rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/), plus The Register coverage of 3 Nov 2026, opened (https://www.theregister.com/a/5307105). This is Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights' reference, and it exists. Astra subtracted one direction from 64 output columns of the MLP output weights in layers 15 and 19 of Qwen3.5-0.8B: 131,072 weights, 0.016% of the model. MATH-500 went from 7.9% to 48.1%, by fixing reasoning loops. The post mentions no power, energy, latency or quantization.
- UNVERIFIED (from memory; the IEEE and ACM pages returned empty or 403): Callan, Zajic, Prvulovic, SAVAT (MICRO 2014, DOI 10.1109/MICRO.2014.39) and FASE (MICRO 2015, DOI 10.1145/2830772.2830798). These alternate two activities A/B in a loop at a chosen frequency and detect the difference at that frequency (EM side channel). If accurate, this is direct precedent for alternation/lock-in measurement of activity differences, with a periodic square wave rather than PRBS, and EM rather than power rails.
- Background, not a finding: correlation detection with maximum-length sequences (system identification, chopper and lock-in amplifiers) is textbook. No prior work was found that applies PRBS lock-in by in-process weight swapping to on-die power rails of Apple silicon or any other platform. My web search budget ran out after 2 searches, so the search was shallow: only directly fetched URLs were checked.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1. The effect size is below the method's own floor for the edits Ed cares about. The Astra edit touched 0.016% of weights in two down-projection matrices. Take the extreme upper bound: those weights swing the full Gregersen range (about 40%, which needs whole-distribution changes such as zeros versus random), and about half of GPU power is data-dependent. The per-step GPU-rail change is then about 0.016% x 0.4 x 0.5 ≈ 3e-5, i.e. 0.003%. A small-norm direction subtraction that keeps the value distribution should be one to three orders smaller again. The idea's own detectability section calls 0.01% infeasible, so no amount of lock-in hours reaches Astra-class edits. Only bulk edits are in range: whole-tensor ablations, pruning or sparsification, re-quantization, or LSB-randomization controls, which reach about 0.1-1%.

2. The method measures the wrong component. Swapping weights every 0.5-2 s during free generation mixes trajectories: KV cache and tokens produced under one arm are consumed by the other. A clean per-step contrast therefore needs teacher-forced, fixed token streams, and that removes by design the energy change that dominates. The Astra edit works by removing loops, which changes token counts by tens of percent. Episode-level energy already resolves that (replays repeat to about 2 J on 2-4 kJ, i.e. 0.05-0.1%) with no lock-in. For 'surgical edit changes power AND performance', the large, measurable term is tokens x J/token, not W.

3. PRBS does not fully leave the drift band. A non-return-to-zero m-sequence has a roughly flat spectrum down to 1/(N·Tc), so part of the reference overlaps 1/f and thermal drift. A square-wave chop or a Manchester-coded (bi-phase) m-sequence nulls DC and low frequencies. Use bi-phase chips, or high-pass and pre-whiten before correlating.

4. 'Beats ABBA' is unproven. The anchor itself (±0.011 W, about 0.11% in about 1 h of R,C,C,R with 760 s blocks) already equals the claimed 0.1%/√h. Lock-in wins only if that half-width was dominated by nonlinear drift or temperature mismatch rather than white decode-step jitter. That noise spectrum is not known. The cheap first step is to compute the GPU-rail PSD from an existing non-restricted steady-decode powermetrics capture (the KB raw plist sits under night-archive, so the lead must arrange a non-claim copy or a fresh 20-minute capture) and predict both designs' variance before building. The leakage argument is real (0.08-0.25 %/°C, so a 1 °C arm mismatch already exceeds 0.1%), but it is a systematic in ABBA only when the arms draw different power or drift nonlinearly.

5. Sampling. powermetrics delivers interval-average records of about 130 ms with jitter, and the KB clock-fit bound was 10-17 ms. At 0.5 s chips, dropping edge samples discards about 25-50% of the data, so use 1-2 s chips or read cumulative IOReport counters at the runtime's own swap stamps. The KM003C at 50 Hz is fine on timing, but its noise is about 70x the GPU rail's (±0.77 W versus ±0.011 W per hour), so the DC channel will not resolve anything below about 1%.

6. Channel validity. Hot Pixels shows that M1 IOReport GPU power responds to operand Hamming weight. That is not shown for the M3 Max, and IOReport GPU energy may come partly from an activity model. The macOS 27 Energy Model entitlement hazard (macmon) applies. A null on the rail would not prove a null in the silicon, so a physically metered cross-check is required, and that needs effects of about 1% or more.

7. Background load at 8-47 W is uncorrelated in expectation but adds variance. It also shifts the shared SoC power and thermal budget, so heavy agent load can cause DVFS events and throttling that coincide with chips by chance. Log GPU frequency per chip and include frequency as a regressor or exclusion.

8. Implementation (feasible). MLX is lazy, so each swap needs mx.eval and a sync before the timestamp. Avoid mx.compile on swapped modules. Check that per-step latency does not differ between arms, because the KB contrast was 85% time-driven. Both buffers must be distinct allocations of identical size, and the A/A' and content-swap nulls cover buffer identity. Memory overhead is trivial for the edited tensors only.

9. Background (not an objection): Ed's intuition is right that power is a hard signal to separate. For surgical edits, the tractable intersection is an energy-per-task change from behaviour (token counts, loops), measured at episode level. Per-token power is a bulk-edit question that needs whole-tensor-scale changes to clear about 0.1% in a few hours.

**Residual novelty**

Not refuted. No source found swaps model tensors inside one process on a pseudo-random schedule and demodulates powermetrics/IOReport rails and an inline DC meter to measure the power contrast of a weight edit. Nor does any source report a sensitivity-versus-time curve against ABBA blocks on Apple silicon. Each ingredient is known: lock-in or MLS correlation is textbook, A/B alternation is SAVAT/FASE-style (UNVERIFIED), and content-dependent GPU power is shown by Gregersen 2024 on NVIDIA and Hot Pixels 2023 on M1 IOReport. So the residual novelty is narrow. It is (a) a measured noise-spectrum and sensitivity comparison, PRBS lock-in versus ABBA, for the GPU rail and DC input of an M3 Max, and (b) the first content-only weight-edit power measurement on Apple unified memory, with A/A' and content-swap nulls. The methods contribution (a) is publishable whatever the effect size. Claim (b) is very likely to come back null for Astra-class surgical edits (see feasibility). Recommended reframe: an instrument characterization with graded positive controls. Use synthetic 'edits' of known power, for example LSB randomization or zeroing a whole tensor per Gregersen, at roughly 1%, 0.3% and 0.1% of GPU-rail power, and read the sensitivity curve off those. Then report a surgical edit as an upper bound. Bank it under item 3/4 (later), not 1/2.

---

## 12. Two-channel decomposition of edit energy: fixed-token replay vs free-running (MBP pilot) (seat 2 (Opus))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Sparks of Weight-Space Programming', blog post 2026-11-02, https://rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/ (OPENED). This is Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra edits weights' reference: it exists, but it is a non-peer-reviewed blog, not a product feature. Astra removed one 'looping' direction from 64 MLP output columns in layers 15 and 19 of Qwen3.5-0.8B (131,072 weights, 0.016%); MATH-500 7.9% -> 48.1%, GSM8K 13.8% -> 67.1%, IFEval 21.0% -> 62.3%; answers hitting the 32,768-token cap fell 93.0% -> 33.1%; Gemma 3 270M (rarely loops) did not improve. The post reports NO latency, token-count, compute or energy numbers, so the energy angle is untouched. Secondary coverage opened: https://emergent.sh/news/gpt-6-astra-edits-neural-weights-directly-no and https://smartscope.blog/en/blog/gpt-6-astra-research-directly-reading-writing-ai-2026/ (both cite only the blog).
- Arditi et al. 2024, 'Refusal in Language Models Is Mediated by a Single Direction', arXiv 2406.11717 (OPENED, HTML sec. 4.1): weight orthogonalization W_out' <- W_out - r r^T W_out on embedding, positional, attention-out and MLP-out matrices; stated to be equivalent to inference-time directional ablation (Appendix E). No inference cost, latency or energy reported. This is the proposal's edit primitive; its runtime-hook twin is the natural 'same intervention, different kernels' control.
- Liu et al. 2025, 'Quantization Hurts Reasoning? An Empirical Study on Quantized Reasoning Models', arXiv 2504.04823 (OPENED): 'Contrary to expectations, quantized models do not exhibit increased output lengths.' Closest published look at whether a weight-level change moves the token channel; it does not measure energy or non-quantization edits.
- Dauner & Socher 2025, 'Energy costs of communicating with AI', Frontiers in Communication (OPENED): reasoning models' higher emissions 'driven primarily by their elevated token production' (+543.5 thinking tokens per MCQ), single A100. Establishes the token channel as the dominant energy lever for reasoning, with no fixed-token replay and no weight edits.
- Jin, Wei, Brooks 2025, 'The Energy Cost of Reasoning', arXiv 2505.14733 (OPENED, abstract): 'critical interaction between TTC performance and output sequence length'; no weight edits, pruning or quantization, no per-token/length decomposition stated in the abstract.
- Kannan et al. 2026, 'GreenBench', arXiv 2608.28667 (OPENED, abstract): M4 Pro, powermetrics, per-token energy for five 3-9B models; no edit or pruning contrasts, no length/per-token separation. Shows the rails-only Apple-silicon baseline practice the KB result (f = 0.238) corrects.
- Yang, Chen, Sze 2017, 'Designing Energy-Efficient CNNs using Energy-Aware Pruning', arXiv 1611.05128 (OPENED): model size and operation counts 'do not necessarily result in lower energy consumption'; pruning that 'can greatly decrease the number of weights but the energy reduction is limited'. Prior art for the intuition that unstructured zeroing does not buy energy on dense hardware (CNNs on an accelerator model, not LLM decode on Apple GPUs, not measured at the wall).
- Wang et al. 2022, 'Hertzbleed', USENIX Security 2022, https://www.hertzbleed.com/ (OPENED): 'the dynamic frequency scaling of modern x86 processors depends on the data being processed'. Prior art that operand values change power and, via DVFS, clocks; i.e. a 'zeros' arm is not physics-neutral.
- Taneja et al. 2023, 'Hot Pixels: Frequency, Power, and Temperature Attacks on GPUs and Arm SoCs', USENIX Security 2023, arXiv 2305.12784 (OPENED, abstract only; the PDF could not be text-extracted here): 'the sensor data observed correlates with both instructions executed and data processed' on Arm SoCs and GPUs. Whether Apple M1/M2 GPUs are in the device list is UNVERIFIED in this session (I recall they are).
- MLX affine quantization docs, https://ml-explore.github.io/mlx/build/html/python/_autosummary/mlx.core.quantize.html (OPENED): code = round((w - beta)/s) with beta the group minimum, so a real zero weight is stored as code round(-beta/s), not as bit pattern 0, and dequantizes to a value within +/- s/2 of zero rather than exactly 0. Bears directly on the 4-bit zeroing arm (see concerns).
- mlx-lm LoRALinear source, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ml-explore/mlx-lm/main/mlx_lm/tuner/lora.py (OPENED): y = linear(x) + (scale * ((dropout(x) @ lora_a) @ lora_b)).astype(x.dtype); base stays quantized; A/B created without explicit dtype (default float32 per MLX convention, UNVERIFIED on the page). An unmerged rank-16 adapter therefore adds bytes (fp32 A,B) as well as kernels; it is not a pure kernel-count control.
- Batina et al. 2018, arXiv 1810.09076 and Maji et al. 2021 'Leaky Nets', arXiv 2103.14739 (OPENED, abstracts): power side channels recover NN parameters on microcontrollers; the abstracts do not state the Hamming-weight/zero-operand mechanism, so I cite them only as 'power depends on weights' at the microcontroller scale. The research-program synthesis already cites Batina.
- In-repo anchors (read): replay pair-difference SD 2.97 J on ~2.7 kJ (docs/research-audits/2026-11-07/research-program.md:114 on research/2026-11-07-research-program; RUN_STATE.md:999); attribution floor ~0.7-1.0 J boundary placement vs 0.29-0.49 J repeatability on ~50 J points (PROJECT_STATUS.md:100-105); KB coverage f = 0.238 +/- 0.098 with DC contrast 0.0389 +/- 0.0072 vs GPU 0.00879 +/- 0.00010 J/GB (docs/process_traces/2026-10-31-magistrate-5cbfbcfc/KB-SLOT-FILL.md:20-23,60); GPU-clock/residual correlation +0.79 to +0.92 (research-program.md M3); ALIGN member fixed cost 516.6 s (FINAL-DESIGN.md:44 as cited there); ALIGN Apple endpoint is pinned greedy with no forced-token path (joulewise/align/apple/endpoint.py:273-277 and apple/pilot.py:54 @ cda28a5ef), so 'token-identical replays' are deterministic re-runs, not teacher forcing; pinned small dense 4-bit models on the MBP: mlx-community/Qwen3-1.7B-4bit, Qwen3-4B-4bit, Qwen3-8B-4bit (docs/registrations/difficulty_pack_v2.md:35-38); MBP is an M3 Max (README.md:51). I found no in-repo pruning/ablation/steering energy measurement.
- Search limits: the session's WebSearch budget was exhausted after one query (the Astra one), so prior-art coverage came from targeted fetches of papers I already knew. I could not run Scholar/arXiv keyword sweeps for 'energy of model editing', 'pruning energy on Apple GPU', or 'LoRA inference energy'. Treat 'no prior work found' as weaker than a normal sweep; a 20-minute search when the budget refills is advised before registration. Not opened: ML.ENERGY leaderboard, S-LoRA/Punica, Gemma Scope (UNVERIFIED).

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

Verdict: not refuted; nobody I could find measures the energy of surgical weight edits or decomposes edit energy with a fixed-token replay. But the detectability and cost arithmetic in the proposal borrows the 30B/2.7 kJ numbers and does not survive transfer to a 4B 4-bit model unchanged, and three of the six arms are mis-specified as physics controls. Details:

1. Noise floor does not scale with episode size. The 0.11% (2.97 J on 2.7 kJ) figure is one point; the instrument's own floor is absolute: 0.29-0.49 J repeatability plus 0.7-1.0 J boundary placement on ~50 J points (PROJECT_STATUS.md:100-105), i.e. ~1-2% at 50 J. A 4B-4bit replay of 30 prompts x ~300 tokens is roughly 9k tokens; at a plausible 0.1-0.2 J/token (GPU rail; the only in-repo small-model numbers are a July exploratory 362.8 J suite, RUN_STATE.md:7301, and an unattributed 0.1057 J/token slope at :6757, so this is an estimate) that is 0.9-1.8 kJ per replay. With a pair SD of ~2-3 J that gives MDE(80%, n=16) about 0.13-0.2% and TOST at +/-0.1% needs roughly 30-60 pairs, not 11. Fix: either lengthen replays to >= 2.5 kJ (~15-25k forced tokens, ~5-7 min each at 50-70 tok/s) or size n from an A/A pilot of 8 pairs at the chosen length BEFORE committing arms. Also note 0.1% is three times below the 'clearable phase contrast ~5 J' D-078 clause 11 floor unless the replay boundaries are whole-episode, which they should be.

2. Cost is 2-4x understated. Seven arms x 16 ABBA pairs x 2 = 224 replays. At 3-6 min each that is 11-22 h of pure measurement before any guard, cooldown or baseline, i.e. 1-2 Mac-days by day, not 0.3-0.5. If the kit inherits the ALIGN member law (516.6 s fixed per member) the fixed cost alone is 32 h; the kit must be lean (60 s idle baselines, shared A arm across contrasts, arms interleaved in one session). The ~30-prompt free-running channel per arm is cheap by comparison (7 x 30 x ~1 min).

3. The 4-bit 'zeros' arm probably produces no zeros. MLX affine 4-bit stores w=0 as code round(-beta/s) and dequantizes it to a value within +/- s/2 of zero, so the multiplier sees small non-zero mantissas and the memory bus sees non-zero codes; neither the ALU-toggling nor the bus-toggling saving the arm is meant to exhibit exists. Either zero the codes to exactly dequantize to 0 by snapping beta to a multiple of s per group (changes the other weights slightly), use a quantization mode with an exact zero (mxfp4/nvfp4 if the pinned model's kernels support it), or drop the 4-bit zeros arm and keep bf16 zeros only. State this in the registration or the arm will read as a null and be misinterpreted.

4. Size of the zeros effect may sit at the TOST boundary. Zeroing 50% of ONE MLP block in a ~36-block model touches ~1.4% of per-token MACs; if zero operands save ~30-50% of multiplier dynamic power and ALU dynamic power is ~30% of the GPU rail, the expected effect is 0.1-0.3% of GPU-rail energy, right at the +/-0.1% band. The physics channel does not need behavioural fidelity, so make the zeros positive control large (50% of all MLP weights, or whole blocks) and keep a separate small 'scientific' zeros arm for the token channel. Pre-register sign, not size; no prior Apple-GPU number exists to predict size.

5. Bus-toggling lands off-rail. The KB result says ~3/4 of marginal memory-traffic energy is outside the powermetrics rails (f = 0.238; KB-SLOT-FILL.md:60,72-76). Any DRAM I/O effect of bf16 zeros therefore shows up mostly in 'rest', invisible to the GPU-rail primary readout, and the KM003C DC channel is ~10x noisier in relative terms (DC contrast 0.0389 +/- 0.0072 vs GPU 0.00879 +/- 0.00010 J/GB). So the +/-0.1% equivalence is a GPU-rail claim; say so, and expect the DC channel to resolve only percent-level effects (head removal, LoRA, SAE), not the zero-byte edits.

6. Requantization confound. Folding W' = W - r r^T W (or any edit) into a 4-bit model means dequant -> edit -> requant; the requant alone can move codes and hence tokens. Add a 'dequant-requant with r = 0' arm (A''); it is nearly free and separates edit from requantization in the token channel. Similarly bf16 and 4-bit zero arms need their own bf16 and 4-bit bases (two base models, not 'one small dense 4-bit model').

7. 'LoRA = kernel-count control' is wrong as stated. mlx-lm's unmerged path adds two matmuls, a scale, a cast and an add per projection, AND reads fp32 A/B: for d ~ 2560, rank 16 on every projection is ~10% extra bytes against a 4-bit base. Better pure kernel-count control: an unmerged LoRA with B = 0 (default init). Its outputs are bit-identical to the base, so the token channel is exactly zero and the physics channel is kernel overhead alone. Likewise, replace the SAE-hook arm with Arditi's inference-time directional ablation hook of the SAME direction that was folded: provably the same intervention, different implementation, no SAE required (no public SAE for Qwen3-4B was verified; an SAE encode/decode at one layer would also add ~0.5 GB/token of bf16 reads, dwarfing everything else). Note that fold vs hook outputs are equivalent only up to numerics; log the token-identity rate.

8. Teacher forcing is a build item, not an existing path. The ALIGN endpoint is pinned greedy; 'token-identical replays' were deterministic re-runs, not forced decoding (endpoint.py:273-277, pilot.py:54 @ cda28a5ef). The kit needs a one-token-per-step forced loop that still computes the logits/argmax and discards it, with the same prefill and cache behaviour as free running; a batched-prefill 'replay' would measure a different, compute-bound regime and invalidate the channel split.

9. Carry-over and DVFS. GPU clock explains most of the benign residual (+0.79 to +0.92). Arms with different duty cycles (LoRA/SAE hooks slow decode; head removal speeds it) change the thermal state of the following A measurement in an ABBA block. Use fixed cooldown gaps, record SMC/clock, keep the clock as a covariate, and alternate ABBA/BAAB. Head removal also changes matrix shapes (29 of 32 q-heads under GQA) and may change kernel tiling efficiency, so its per-byte energy could rise; pre-register sign of bytes, not size.

10. The headline claim is close to tautological. For a zero-byte, zero-kernel edit the physics channel can only be data-dependent toggling, which is sub-percent; and Astra's own loop edit moved cap-hits 93% -> 33% on a 32k cap, a token-channel effect of tens of percent. '>= 95% via token channel' will hold trivially whenever |dE_total| is large and is undefined when it is small (A/A'). Report the absolute physics-channel bound in J and % (with CI) as the primary result and the share only for arms with |dE_total| > 5%. The decomposition should be written as dE = [E(edit, own tokens) - E(edit, base tokens)] + [E(edit, base tokens) - E(base, base tokens)]; the design measures the second bracket directly, which is right.

11. Finding the direction is desk work with its own risk. A 'verbosity or looping direction' for Qwen3-4B needs a contrast corpus and validation that ablating it changes length by the 10-70% the proposal assumes; Astra's direction is for Qwen3.5-0.8B (already on the MBP per the R0 note), but the blog did not, as far as I could see, publish the direction or edited weights (UNVERIFIED). Budget a desk day for direction discovery or the token channel may be small and the whole experiment reduces to physics controls.

12. On Ed's 'power is hard to pull out of noise': this design is the right answer to that worry in the lab (fixed-token replay removes the 19-58 J token-model residual and reaches the 0.1% replay floor), but only on a quiet, single-tenant Mac with GPU-rail counters; it says nothing about recovering edit signatures on a busy machine, and should not be framed as if it did.

**Residual novelty**

High, after reframing. No published work measures the energy of surgical weight edits, and none uses a fixed-token (teacher-forced) replay to split an edit's energy effect into a physics channel and a token channel; the closest items are per-token benchmarks (GreenBench), reasoning-length energy papers (Dauner & Socher; Jin et al.), 'weight count is not energy' (Yang et al. 2017) and data-dependent power side channels (Hertzbleed, Hot Pixels). The timely hook is real: the MIT CSAIL 'Weight-Space Programming' post (Takehi et al., 2026-11-02) shows GPT-6 Astra removing a looping direction from MLP output columns of Qwen3.5-0.8B, exactly the proposal's edit class, and reports no compute or energy at all. The novel, defensible deliverables are: (a) a per-mille GPU-rail bound on the physics channel of zero-byte edits (orthogonalization, direction ablation) on Apple silicon, (b) the first measured data-dependent (zeros) energy effect for LLM decode on an Apple GPU, with the bf16/4-bit distinction, and (c) a clean fold-vs-hook comparison (same direction ablated in weights vs at runtime, and LoRA with B = 0) isolating kernel overhead from behaviour.
