Drop the '>= 95% share' headline (near-tautological), drop or repair the 4-bit zeros arm, replace the SAE arm with the inference-time ablation hook, size n from an A/A pilot at the real episode energy, and budget 1-2 Mac-days plus 1-2 desk days rather than 0.3-0.5 plus 1. Caveat: prior-art coverage was limited by an exhausted search budget; re-sweep before registration.

### Opus 5.5 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Gregersen, Patel, Choukse 2024, 'Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs', arXiv:2409.18324 (opened abstract + HTML). With GEMM shapes held fixed, changing only the input values (distribution, bit similarity, placement, sparsity) moves GPU power by up to about 40%, attributed to bit flips. Power falls as sparsity rises, and for fully sorted floating-point matrices it peaks at roughly 30-40% sparsity. Tested on A100 FP32/FP16/FP16-T/INT8, then FP16 on H100, V100 and RTX 6000. It measures no DRAM or memory-bound power, uses no LLM weights, and does not test Apple silicon. This is the closest prior work to the 'physics channel'. It already establishes that weight values alone can move power, which makes the 50% zeroing arm a replication or extension of it rather than a new effect. It does not cover memory-bound 4-bit decode on Apple GPUs, or rank-1 edits.
- Arditi et al. 2024, 'Refusal in Language Models Is Mediated by a Single Direction', arXiv:2406.11717 (opened, HTML v3). Eq. 5, W_out' <- W_out - r r^T W_out, applied to the embedding, positional embedding, attention-out and MLP-out matrices plus biases. This is the proposed folded ablation. The paper reports only a one-time compute cost and no inference energy.
- Sun, Yan, Weng 2025, 'ThinkEdit: Interpretable Weight Editing to Mitigate Overly Short Thinking in Reasoning Models', arXiv:2503.22048 (opened abstract). A linear direction controls reasoning length. The edit changes the o_proj of about 4% of heads (about 0.2% of parameters). This is a ready-made precedent for the 'verbosity direction' edit, and it reports no energy.
- Chen et al. 2025, 'SEAL: Steerable Reasoning Calibration of LLMs for Free', arXiv:2504.07986 (opened abstract). A runtime steering vector cuts reasoning tokens by 11.8-50.4%, with 'efficiency gains' but no energy or power numbers. This is prior evidence that the token channel of a direction edit is large, which the behaviour arm would only re-measure in joules.
- NOT FOUND / UNVERIFIED: the 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights' reference. The WebSearch budget for this session was exhausted before any search could run, so I could not look for it. The repo mentions 'Astra' only as a historical seat name in docs/contracts/pack_night_go_receipt.md, which is unrelated. Its existence is unconfirmed.
- LIMITATION: the session's WebSearch quota was used up before this task started. I checked prior work only by fetching arXiv IDs I already knew. I could not run searches for measured-energy studies of pruned or sparse LLM inference, energy costs of steering or SAE hooks, or data-dependent power on Apple GPUs, so the 'no prior work' conclusion is weaker than it would be after a full search.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1. The ±0.1% equivalence on the GPU rail cannot establish that the physics channel is zero. Per the KB result (KB-SLOT-FILL.md:20-23,60 on main), about 3/4 of marginal memory-traffic energy is outside the powermetrics rails. Decode on a 4-bit dense model is memory-bound, so a value-dependent effect would mostly show up in DRAM/IO toggling (data-bus activity), which the GPU rail does not see. The DC-input contrast is about 70x noisier than the GPU rail (0.0389 ± 0.0072 vs 0.00879 ± 0.00010 J/GB), so a DC-level TOST at ±0.1% needs far more than 16 pairs. Gregersen et al. measured only compute-bound GEMM, so the size of any DRAM-side data effect on Apple LPDDR is unknown. Either pre-register the DC meter as co-primary with its own (much wider) MDE, or narrow the claim to 'GPU-rail physics channel'.
2. The noise anchor does not transfer automatically. The 0.11% figure is 2.97 J pair-difference SD on about 2.7 kJ 30B-A3B SHADE episodes. A small dense 4-bit model at about 0.1-0.2 J/token needs roughly 15-25k forced-decode tokens per unit to reach kJ scale. At a few hundred joules per unit, the same roughly 2-3 J absolute noise becomes 0.5-1%, and the MDE at n=16 grows to about 0.35-0.7%, so the TOST would fail for power reasons. Size units in joules, not prompts.
3. 'Teacher-forced decode' must be token-by-token forced decode with a KV cache (a single-token step each time, with the reference token substituted for the sample). A batched teacher-forced forward pass is prefill physics (compute-bound, different kernels) and would not measure the decode physics channel.
4. The rank-1 folded ablation makes a null result close to guaranteed. It changes weight statistics negligibly, and decode kernels are value-oblivious except through toggling. A passing TOST is the expected outcome and will not persuade anyone unless the positive controls show the instrument can see a value-only effect. 50% zeroing of ONE MLP block touches only about 2% of weight bytes. Even a 20% data-dependent change in per-byte energy would give about 0.2-0.4% at the wall and much less on the GPU rail. No model makes a pre-registered 'predicted size' for it, so it is exploratory, not a positive control. Because forced decode makes behavioural collapse irrelevant, zero all MLP blocks or use a structured all-zeros/sorted variant (following Gregersen) to get a control large enough to be informative.
5. The 4-bit arms have a quantization confound. MLX affine 4-bit stores w ≈ scale·q + bias, so a zeroed weight usually does not become a q=0 code, and toggling may not drop. Folding r r^T W into 4-bit means dequantize, edit, requantize. Every touched group then gets new scales, biases and codes, and many sub-step projection changes round away, partly undoing the ablation. The null arm must be the unedited bf16 model put through the identical requantization pipeline, not the shipped 4-bit file. Otherwise the 'physics' contrast includes requantization bit-pattern changes and the behaviour contrast includes quantization drift.
6. The SAE-hook-vs-folded arm compares two different edits. Clamping an SAE latent is input-dependent, fires only when the latent is active, and leaves the error term, whereas projecting out the decoder direction acts on every token. Tokens will diverge, so only the forced-decode channel is comparable. Also, a runtime SAE adds large encoder/decoder reads: a 16k×d bf16 SAE is tens of MB per hooked layer against a roughly 2 GB model. It is therefore mainly another bytes/kernel positive control (BYTES already predicts it), not an interp contrast. SAEs exist only for a few small models (Gemma Scope, Llama Scope), so the SAE arm effectively dictates which model can be used. Check that the model 'already on the MBP' has one.
7. The bytes control is not a bytes change unless heads are physically sliced. Removing 10% of attention heads changes bytes only if q/k/v/o are sliced and reshaped. Zeroing heads leaves bytes unchanged. In 4-bit, the o_proj slice must respect group-64 boundaries, which works for head_dim 128.
8. The '≥95% via token channel' claim is close to mechanical. If the physics channel is near zero by construction and edits shift tokens by 10-70% (consistent with SEAL/ThinkEdit-scale length changes), the ratio follows directly. Per-task ratios are also ill-defined where token counts barely change and |ΔE| is about 0. Define it as an aggregate (sum |ΔE_token| / sum |ΔE|) with a CI. Note too that the ALIGN 19-58 J residual model was fit on the 30B MoE and SHADE turns. It must be refit for the new dense model on prompts that were never used for evaluation, or the attribution is circular.
9. Feasibility. A usable verbosity or looping direction, the MLX fold and requantize pipeline, an MLX SAE hook, and an unmerged-LoRA path together are more than 'about 1 desk day', more like 2-4 days. The Mac time is plausible only with joule-sized units. ABBA ordering controls drift, but arms that change kernel count (LoRA, SAE) also change thermal state, so pairs need a thermal-stable criterion. This experiment directly addresses Ed's concern that power is hard to separate from background noise, because replay with identical tokens removes the token noise. It does not remove host noise: the quiet guards and the exclusions from the pilot-10 battery top-up still apply.

**Residual novelty**

I found no prior work that splits the energy effect of an interpretability-style weight edit into a token channel and a fixed-token physics channel, or that measures any of this on Apple-silicon memory-bound decode with rails plus a DC meter. Gregersen et al. 2024 shows that weight values alone can move power by up to 40%, but only for compute-bound GEMMs on NVIDIA GPUs, with no DRAM measurement and no LLM edits. Arditi et al., ThinkEdit and SEAL show that direction edits change behaviour and token counts but measure no energy. What is genuinely new:
(a) a measured bound on the value-dependent (bit-toggle) energy of edited weights in memory-bound 4-bit decode on Apple silicon, split into GPU rail and wall power. The KB result predicts most of any such effect sits outside the rails, which makes this split the interesting part.
(b) a reusable replay-based protocol that separates "the edit changed what the model says" from "the edit changed what each token costs".

The headline TOST null for rank-1 folded ablations is close to guaranteed and has little value on its own. The publishable content is the positive controls (large zeroing in bf16 vs 4-bit affine codes, physically sliced heads, unmerged LoRA, SAE hook overhead) measured against the BYTES and KB instrument model. The prior-art search is incomplete: the web-search quota ran out before the task started, so studies of measured energy for pruning, steering or SAE inference may exist that I could not check. The 'GPT-6 Astra natively edits weights' reference was not found and is unverified.

---

## 13. Energy-capability frontiers of compute-allocation edits (registered headline) (seat 2 (Opus))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Ed's reference found. It is NOT a model editing its own weights: MIT CSAIL (Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab) had OpenAI's GPT-6 Astra edit Qwen3.5-0.8B ('weight-space programming', blog 2026-11-02, https://rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/ OPENED). Exp 8: Astra traced looping to one direction written by the MLPs of layers 15 and 19 and subtracted it from 64 output columns per layer (131,072 weights, 0.016%). Thinking on, 32,768-token cap, temp 0.6/top-p 0.95/top-k 20/presence 1.5. MATH-500 7.9% -> 48.1% (50-forward-pass budget; 10/25 passes gave only 12.3/14.1%), cap-hit 93.0% -> 33.1%; GSM8K 13.8 -> 67.1; IFEval 21.0 -> 62.3. No response-length, latency, compute or energy numbers reported. Edits released as exact diffs vs public base weights plus an apply script (CC BY 4.0 / MIT) at https://github.com/RikiyaT/lm_understand-logs (OPENED) - so the Astra edit itself CAN be replayed; the Astra-produced extraction code is in tool_calls.jsonl but intermediate activations are excluded. Secondary coverage: VentureBeat (OPENED) and The Register https://www.theregister.com/a/5307105 (OPENED), which notes no time/compute savings vs conventional methods were established.
- ThinkEdit (Sun, Yan, Weng; arXiv:2503.22048, EMNLP 2025; abs + HTML OPENED). Models: DeepSeek-R1-Distill Qwen-1.5B, Llama3-8B, Qwen-14B (32B in appendix). Edits W_o <- W_o(I - v v^T) on the top 4% 'short-reasoning' heads, 0.2% of parameters; it LENGTHENS short traces (overall mean length change about -0.27%, GSM8K lengths rise e.g. 354.5 -> 538.2 tokens on 14B) and raises accuracy (+3.34% overall). Crucially for this proposal: 'steering toward short reasoning (alpha<0) consistently degrades accuracy' (Fig. 3 only, no table). No latency, FLOPs or energy. So ThinkEdit's short-reasoning heads are a length-raising, accuracy-raising lever; using them as a short-reasoning lever is going against the paper's own frontier.
- Activation Steering for Chain-of-Thought Compression / ASC (Azizi, Baghaei Potraghloo, Pedram; arXiv:2507.04742; abs + HTML OPENED). Models R1-Distill-Qwen-7B, R1-Distill-Llama-8B, QwQ-32B. Token reductions from Table 1: 61.3% / 50.4% (Qwen-7B MATH500/GSM8K), 33.8% / 67.4% (Llama-8B), 50.7% / 45.7% (QwQ-32B) at essentially unchanged accuracy; gamma sweep (Fig. 5) shows the accuracy-vs-length frontier in TOKENS with a breakpoint beyond which accuracy falls. Steering vector is injected at inference each decoding step ('does not involve updating the model parameters'); the paper does NOT fold it into weights. Latency on an A6000, up to 2.73x; energy cited only as motivation, not measured.
- SEAL: Steerable Reasoning Calibration (Chen et al.; arXiv:2504.07986; abs OPENED): latent steering vector on R1-Distill and QwQ-32B-Preview, 11.8-50.4% fewer reasoning tokens, up to +11% accuracy; no latency or energy numbers in the abstract.
- Dauner & Socher, 'Energy costs of communicating with AI', Frontiers in Communication 2025 (OPENED). Closest measured prior: reasoning vs non-reasoning modes of the same models on a local A100 (Perun), 500 questions; reasoning modes emit 4-6x more in free-response; attributes emissions to token output qualitatively; gives an accuracy-vs-CO2 scatter (energy-per-correct-answer style) across 14 systems. Not an interp edit, not an edit-strength frontier, not Apple silicon, no late-token analysis.
- Jegham et al., 'How Hungry is AI?' (arXiv:2505.09598; abs OPENED): API-level energy/water/carbon benchmarking of 30 models; abstract does not break out reasoning tokens vs energy.
- Fernandez et al., 'Energy Considerations of LLM Inference and Efficiency Optimizations' (arXiv:2504.17674; abs OPENED): energy vs input/output token geometry on GPUs, optimizations up to 73% savings; no reasoning-model or weight-edit content in the abstract.
- Inference-cost (length-inflation) attack literature establishes that output length drives cost: Engorgio (arXiv:2412.19394, ICLR 2025, OPENED; 2-13x longer outputs, no energy numbers in abstract) and OverThink (arXiv:2502.02542, OPENED; 12-46x more reasoning tokens, cost framed in tokens). These are the inverse direction of the proposal and do not meter energy.
- Stop Overthinking survey (arXiv:2503.16419, TMLR 2025; abs OPENED): catalogs length-reduction methods; no energy measurement mentioned.
- ML.ENERGY leaderboard (https://ml.energy/leaderboard) - page is JS-rendered and returned only its title; UNVERIFIED whether it reports energy vs reasoning tokens for current reasoning models. No steering/edited variants expected there.
- JouleWise's own banked items already cover pieces of the design (canonical clone docs/research_question_bank.md): C5-W.2 'Thinking-token inflation' (natural-EOS vs fixed budget on a reasoning model; status candidate, analysis-plan-only), 'Energy-per-correct-answer vs difficulty' (affine_mod_ladder_v1, binomial >=3-correct guard, AP-5), C5-1.2 context-length energy scaling and the banked 'KV-growth decode drift' (per-token energy vs position, CHUNKED windows only: ~4 ms token cadence vs ~113 ms powermetrics sampler, no per-token joule claims), and the HORIZONS KV/attention context-slope contrast. The Block 6 draft (docs/design/block6_collection_draft.md) measured KV reads adding 2-27% to bytes/token at 2k context and Qwen3-30B-A3B reading 1.71 GB/token vs 17.2 GB resident. None of these is an interp-edit frontier.
- Search coverage caveat: the shared WebSearch budget was exhausted after one query in this session, so prior-work discovery relied on direct fetches of papers already known to me plus the repo. A dedicated search for 'steering/weight-edit x measured energy' (e.g. ML.ENERGY-style or Green-AI venues 2025-2026) was NOT possible and should be rerun before registration. I found no paper metering joules for interp weight edits, consistent with the proposal's own 3-search claim, but this is weak evidence of absence.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1) The headline effect is real and large, but mostly trivially so. Every cited lever changes tokens by 30-67% (ASC, SEAL) or changes cap-hit rate from 93% to 33% (Astra), and the repo's own E = a + b*T linearity row makes request energy near-linear in emitted tokens. A 10-70% |dE| against the stated anchors (replays repeat to ~2 J on 2-4 kJ episodes, i.e. ~0.1%) is >100x above the meter floor; detectability is not in question. The scientific risk is the opposite: the result 'fewer tokens, fewer joules' is already implied by three papers, and the registered headline must be the part that tokens do NOT predict. 2) The real noise is path variance, not the meter. The 1-2% per-task noise figure is a replay number. The design requires sampled decoding (Qwen3.5-0.8B thinking mode recommends temp 1.0, top-p 0.95, presence penalty 1.5; the Astra eval used temp 0.6 with 4 samples per MATH-500 item), and docs/specs/rq_energy_variance_design.md already documents that reasoning-path length variance ('lucky short path') dominates request energy. Per-prompt energy CV under sampling will be tens of percent, ABBA ordering within a prompt does not cancel it, and the token-model share must be estimated on sampled episodes. Sample size is then set jointly by accuracy precision and by path variance (roughly >=100 prompts x >=4 samples per arm), not by accuracy alone. 3) The unedited Qwen3.5-0.8B baseline is a cap artefact. With 93% of base answers running to the 32,768-token cap, base-arm energy is set by the experimenter's max_tokens choice, so 'dE vs base' is not a property of the model. Register the frontier as a function of cap (e.g. 8k/16k/32k) or report capped episodes as censored, and expect the base arm to cost ~100 prompts x 4 samples x ~32k tokens: at a few hundred tok/s for a 0.8B model that is 100-200 s per capped sample, i.e. a whole Mac-night for the base arm alone, before the 3-5 edit strengths. The '2-3 Mac-nights' budget is optimistic for the Astra family unless the cap is lowered. 4) 'Late tokens cost more' is architecture-dependent and is weakest exactly where the Astra edit lives. Qwen3.5-0.8B (HF card OPENED) is a hybrid: 18 Gated DeltaNet linear-attention layers and only 6 full-attention layers (2 KV heads, head dim 256), so its KV cache grows slowly and the per-token premium at long context is small; the nonlinearity you want to show is best tested on a full-attention dense model (Qwen3 dense or an R1-Distill, which ASC/ThinkEdit/SEAL already use) and on Qwen3-30B-A3B, where the repo's own bytes accounting makes the KV read (order 0.1 MB/token/layer-set, ~0.8 GB at 8k context) a large fraction of the 1.71 GB active bytes per token, i.e. a potentially 20-50% per-token premium that is pre-registrable from the Block 6 bytes model. Freeze that prediction before data; a miss is the result. 5) Resolution of the 'share explained by the token model' metric. powermetrics samples at ~113 ms against ~4 ms token cadence, so the late-token premium cannot be measured per token; it must come from episode-level regression on (T, T^2 or T x context) across arms, or from chunked windows, which the bank already restricts to CHUNKED form. That works at the planned n, but a per-token claim is barred by the repo's own rules. 6) Lever validity. (a) ThinkEdit's short-reasoning heads are a length-INCREASING, accuracy-increasing edit; the paper reports that steering toward short reasoning consistently degrades accuracy, so used as a shortening lever it will trace a bad frontier by construction. Say so in the registration, or use ThinkEdit as the 'more tokens, more correct' arm. (b) ASC is an additive inference-time vector; Qwen3 MLP/attention projections are bias-free, so 'folding into weights' means adding a bias parameter to one layer (a one-vector weight edit under mlx-lm), not a projection edit; the additive form means its per-token cost is identical to the base model, so any energy change is purely via tokens and context, which is fine but should be stated. (c) The Astra edit is replayable from released diffs; the proposal's 'own direction ablation' is a reproduction, not a new edit, and the extraction activations were not released. 7) Runtime support. mlx-lm has qwen3_5.py, qwen3_5_moe.py, qwen3_moe.py and gated_delta.py (GitHub API listing OPENED), and the repo already loaded Qwen3-30B-A3B cleanly (71.5 GB peak roster), so both models run; the repo's Qwen3.5 sanitizer strips mtp.* weights, which is harmless for decode. Applying a down_proj column edit in MLX safetensors is straightforward; the 4-bit quantization of edited weights must be done after the edit, and the edit's effect under 4-bit should be verified against the bf16 cap-hit rate before any energy night. 8) MoE-specific confound. On Qwen3-30B-A3B an edit that changes token distributions can also shift router load and expert-cache residency, so J/token may move independently of T; log per-episode routed-expert counts or treat 'per-token J at matched context' as a registered secondary. 9) Correctness denominator. J per correct answer needs the existing binomial guard (>=3 correct per cell); the base Astra arm at 7.9% MATH-500 accuracy gives ~8 correct per 100 prompts and very wide intervals, so energy-per-correct for that arm will be dominated by accuracy uncertainty. 10) Thermal and ordering: 32k-token looping episodes at sustained GPU power will heat the SoC within one arm; ABBA within prompt helps for short tasks but the capped base arm will systematically run hotter; keep the Window-class cooldown rules and record DVFS residency. 11) Ed's broader worry (power buried in machine noise) is answered by the repo's anchors for a dedicated Mac, but the honest expected outcome here is that tokens plus a context term explain >95% of dE; if so the measurement's value is to license a tokens-only frontier for the community, which should be registered as an acceptable (not null) outcome.

**Residual novelty**

No prior work meters joules (or J per correct answer) for interpretability weight edits at any strength; ASC, SEAL and ThinkEdit report tokens and (ASC) wall-clock only, Astra reports accuracy and cap-hit only, and the nearest energy study (Dauner & Socher) compares reasoning modes, not edits. The defensible novel claims are: (i) a metered energy-capability frontier across edit strength for a looping-removal edit and a concise-reasoning edit, on device, with registered floors; (ii) a quantitative test of whether the frontier departs from the tokens-only frontier through a context-dependent per-token premium, with the departure predicted in advance from the project's bytes/KV accounting (expected small on hybrid-attention Qwen3.5-0.8B, larger on full-attention dense models and on Qwen3-30B-A3B relative to its small active bytes); (iii) the MoE case, where edits may move routing and hence J/token at fixed context. The value is highest if the registration is reframed from 'edits move energy by tens of percent' (already implied) to 'how much of the energy frontier is NOT explained by tokens, and where', with 'tokens suffice' as a registered legitimate outcome. The Astra family must be run as a function of the token cap, and ThinkEdit should be positioned as the length-raising arm, not a shortening lever.

### Opus 5.5 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Weight-space programming' blog post, 2 Nov 2026, https://rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/ (opened). This is the 'GPT-6 Astra edits model weights' work Ed mentioned, and it exists. On Qwen3.5-0.8B with thinking on, Astra subtracted one 'looping' direction from 64 output columns of mlp.down_proj in layers 15 and 19 (131,072 weights, 0.016%). MATH-500 rose from 7.9% to 48.1%, and cap hits at the 32,768-token limit fell from 93.0% to 33.1%. Sampling was T=0.6, top-p 0.95, top-k 20, presence penalty 1.5, with 4 samples per problem. The post reports no energy, latency or mean token counts. The 10- and 25-pass edits barely reduced looping.
- JonathanColetti/Qwen3.5-0.8B-Astra-WeightEdit model card, https://huggingface.co/JonathanColetti/Qwen3.5-0.8B-Astra-WeightEdit (opened). The author applied the released Astra diff and matched the evaluated checkpoint hash for hash. Independent scores: base thinking-on 7.4, edited 50.2, base thinking-off 45.8 on MATH-500. The card reports no energy and calls the edit removal of a failure mode, not new capability.
- alanwest, dev.to critique 'the MATH-500 jump was a loop fix', https://dev.to/alanwest/gpt-6-astra-edited-qwen35-08bs-weights-the-math-500-jump-was-a-loop-fix-2g5a (opened). It reports no tokens, latency or energy, and finds no comparison against plain direction ablation or a repetition-penalty sweep. Its proposed fair comparison is base, edited, base with a repetition penalty, and base with an ablation.
- Sun, Yan, Weng, 'ThinkEdit', EMNLP 2025, arXiv:2503.22048 (opened). The edit touches the o_proj weights of the top 4% of heads (0.2% of parameters) on DeepSeek-R1-Distill 1.5B, 8B, 14B and 32B. It lengthens the shortest 5% of answers (for example 78.8 to 103.3 tokens) but leaves overall length 'largely stable': -0.27% net, and +2.9%, +3.5% and -5.7% by model. It reports no energy or latency.
- Azizi, Baghaei Potraghloo, Pedram, 'Activation Steering for Chain-of-Thought Compression' (ASC), arXiv:2507.04742 (opened). It adds a vector to the residual stream at inference, not as a weight edit, on R1-Distill-Qwen-7B, R1-Distill-Llama-8B and QwQ-32B. CoT is up to 67.43% shorter, with a 2.73x wall-clock speedup on an A6000. It reports no energy measurement of its own method. Its background section has an uncited A100 profile: halving a sequence from 8k to 4k tokens cuts energy by about 35%.
- Chen et al., 'SEAL: Steerable Reasoning Calibration', arXiv:2504.07986 (opened). Steering cuts reasoning tokens by 11.8-50.4% and raises accuracy by up to 11%. It reports no energy.
- Eisenstadt, Zimerman, Wolf, 'Overclocking LLM Reasoning', arXiv:2506.07240 (opened). Intervening on the progress encoding shortens thinking. It reports latency only, not energy.
- Jin, Wei, Brooks, 'The Energy Cost of Reasoning', arXiv:2505.14733 (opened). It measures the energy of test-time compute and output length. It makes no weight or steering interventions.
- Luo, Li, Wang, Chen, 'Where Does the Energy Go? Profiling LLM Agent Inference on Blackwell GPUs', arXiv:2609.29707 (opened). Thinking on vs off gives 21-75% more tokens, but per-token energy differs by less than 1%, so energy tracks output volume. This partly pre-answers the 'late tokens cost more' sub-claim, at least on datacenter GPUs.
- Wu, Han, Ruf, Grari, Detyniecki, 'Prefill, Decode, and Context: A Measured Energy Law for Agentic LLM Serving', NeurIPS 2026 workshop, https://neurips.cc/virtual/2026/170701 (opened). A three-term model (prefill, decode, KV-context re-read) fits with R^2 = 0.961 and is preferred over token-count-only baselines. So 'metered differs from tokens-only because late tokens cost more' is already-published physics. Only its application to edits is new.
- Gregersen, Patel, Choukse, 'Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs', arXiv:2409.18324 (opened). For fixed GEMM shapes, changing the data can move GPU power by almost 40%, through bit flips. This is the mechanism by which an edit could change power per token at fixed work.
- Vellaisamy et al., arXiv:2608.28044 (opened). It sweeps output length to characterize token energy on H100 and H200. It makes no interventions.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1. The token channel swamps the noise. Precision is not the problem. Qwen3.5-0.8B base hits the 32,768-token cap 93% of the time, and the edited model 33.1%. Average lengths are unreported. If a non-capped answer runs about 5-8k tokens, mean tokens per task fall roughly from 31k to 15-17k, about -45 to -50%. J per correct falls about 10x, because accuracy goes from 7.9% to 48%. Against 1-2% per-replay noise this is trivially detectable. Ed's worry about pulling power out of machine noise does not bite on the headline. The registered energy finding will almost surely be 'tokens explain it', which makes a weak headline unless the per-token channel is added.

2. The late-token premium on the headline model is small. Qwen3.5-0.8B is a hybrid: 6 of its 24 layers use full attention, the rest Gated-DeltaNet linear attention (config.json opened), with 2 KV heads of dim 256. KV is about 12 KB per token in bf16, about 0.39 GB at 32k, against about 1.6 GB of bf16 weights per decode step. The context premium is about 25% at the cap and roughly 10-12% averaged over a capped run. The metered-vs-token-model divergence on this model is therefore single-digit to about 10% of energy: resolvable, but modest. Qwen3-30B-A3B is full attention (48 layers, 4 KV heads of dim 128, my recollection of the config, not reopened). That is about 98 KB per token, about 1.6 GB at 16k, comparable to its roughly 1.7-1.9 GB of 4-bit active weights. The premium there could reach about 2x late in a run. So the frontier-divergence claim lives mostly in the MoE arm.

3. The per-token channel is probably below the floor. A rank-1 edit to 0.016% of weights changes neither FLOPs nor bytes moved. At batch 1 on bandwidth-bound unified memory, data-dependent DRAM or bus toggling from about 131k changed weights should be on the order of 1e-4 relative or less. Activation-value changes are a tiny share of bytes. With a GPU-rail resolution of about 1e-4 J/GB, a decode slope of a few hundredths of a J/GB resolves only about 0.2-0.5% relative per-token differences. Expect a bound, not a detection. Register it as a bounded null with a stated minimum detectable effect.

4. ThinkEdit is mis-cast. Overall length changes by -0.27% net (-5.7% to +3.5% by model). It will not move J per correct by tens of percent. Use it as a token-neutral, accuracy-moving control, which is useful. Its heads were found on DeepSeek-R1-Distill models, so they must be re-derived for Qwen3.5 and Qwen3.

5. ASC is not a weight edit. It is an additive residual-stream vector at inference. Qwen down_proj and o_proj have no bias, so 'folding into weights' means adding bias parameters, a structural change, and the paper's per-model gamma does not transfer. State this, or use projection-style edits throughout.

6. Sampling noise replaces meter noise. Astra's protocol is T=0.6 with 4 samples per problem. Per-task J then varies by tens of percent across samples, depending on loop vs no loop. The '1-2% per-task noise' figure applies to fixed-trajectory replays, not to sampled reruns. Pair arms with common seeds per prompt. Accuracy precision dominates n: at about 100 prompts the SE is about 4-5 pp.

7. Run time is underestimated. Base and weak-edit arms run about 30k tokens per prompt. At an optimistic 150-250 tok/s for 0.8B bf16, a capped run takes about 2-4 minutes. One 100-prompt arm is about 4-6 h, so 4-6 strengths plus base, with ABBA doubling, is about 2-4 nights for the small model alone. The 30B-A3B arms at about 5k tokens and about 60-90 tok/s, plus an agentic set, add 1-2 nights. Realistically 4-7 Mac-nights, not 2-3. Fixes:
- drop full ABBA duplication and keep repeats on a roughly 20-prompt noise subset;
- use the released diff scaled by alpha in place of own-ablation discovery;
- consider a 16k cap with a pre-registered cap-sensitivity note.

8. Quantization. Folding small column edits into a 4-bit grouped quantization (group size 64, edits on 64 columns) can round much of the edit away. Apply edits before quantization, pass every arm through an identical pipeline, and prefer 8-bit or bf16 for the 30B-A3B on the 128 GB Mac.

9. The Astra fidelity risk is real: 10- and 25-pass edits barely reduced looping. Use the released diff, whose checkpoint hash was independently matched, as the anchor arm. Treat own direction ablation as a second arm, not a substitute.

10. Confounds to pre-register:
- loop rate depends on presence penalty, so include a base-plus-penalty arm per the dev.to critique;
- base thinking-off already scores about 46% on MATH-500, so include thinking-off as a frontier point;
- thermal state and background load matter only for the per-token channel.

**Residual novelty**

No source I opened reports measured energy (J per task or J per correct answer) for any interp weight edit or steering intervention on reasoning length. Astra, ThinkEdit, ASC, SEAL and Overclocking report tokens or latency at most. ASC has only an uncited A100 profile of sequence halving, not of its own method. So a metered energy-capability frontier for the Astra loop edit, a folded concise direction and ThinkEdit heads is open. It would also be the first energy number for the widely discussed Astra result.

Coverage caveat: the session's WebSearch budget was exhausted before this check began. I did no new searches and instead fetched known papers directly, plus URLs found in earlier agents' logs. A concurrent 2026 paper could therefore have been missed.

The novelty is narrower than the claim states:
(a) The headline energy effect is almost entirely a token-count effect, predictable before any measurement.
(b) 'Metered differs from tokens-only because late tokens cost more' is already published as a context-term energy law (Wu et al., NeurIPS 2026 workshop). Luo et al. 2026 found less than 1% per-token difference between thinking modes on Blackwell. The registered comparator must be the token+context model, not tokens-only, or the headline is a strawman.

What remains genuinely new and interp-relevant is a decomposition into two channels:
(1) The token channel. Edits change how many tokens are generated, at what context. This is large and predictable.
(2) The per-token channel. Does a surgical edit change joules per token at identical work, through data-dependent switching (Gregersen et al.) or MoE routing and cache locality?

Channel 2 is the actual intersection Ed asked about. The proposal as written does not isolate it. Adding a forced-trajectory replay would. Teacher-force the same token sequence, decoded token by token, through edited and unedited weights, and compare J at matched tokens and context. This gives either a tight bound or a detection. Prior work has a GPU GEMM micro-benchmark but no per-token-energy measurement of edited LLM weights, so this is publishable either way.

Also new: a dose-response in edit strength from scaling the released Astra diff by alpha, which avoids having to rediscover the direction.

---

## 14. Toggle model: joules per bit transition on Apple silicon (seat 2 (Opus))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Taneja et al., 'Hot Pixels: Frequency, Power, and Temperature Attacks on GPUs and Arm SoCs' (USENIX Security 2023; arXiv 2305.12784, opened via ar5iv). Closest prior work. On Apple M1/M2 (MacBook Air/Pro, Mac mini) they read power/frequency/temperature from IOReport (same counters powermetrics exposes; 10 ms sampling) and showed power is data-dependent: M1 CPU `add` with operand 0 vs 1 = 11.7 vs 12.0 W (about 2.5%); CPU and M1 iGPU power correlate with the Hamming distance between consecutive operands at |r| about 0.97, and iGPU power with operand Hamming weight at r about -0.96; CPU Hamming weight showed no usable correlation (Appendix A: 'unable to conclusively model'). Each point needed 400-800 s of averaging. So 'Apple-silicon software power counters resolve a Hamming-distance toggle model' is already demonstrated, for ALU operands in synthetic loops, not for the LLM weight stream and not for weight edits.
- Lipp et al., PLATYPUS (platypusattack.com, opened): Intel RAPL software power counters resolve instruction type and operand Hamming weight of loads/ALU operands; needed single-instruction repetition (SGX-Step) to beat the 20 kHz averaged counter. Establishes the 'software power meter sees per-bit data dependence' premise on x86.
- Wang et al., Hertzbleed (hertzbleed.com, opened): data-dependent power -> DVFS frequency on Intel 8th-11th gen and AMD Zen 2/3; Arm 'may be affected', Apple not tested. Relevant as a confound: on Apple silicon a data-dependent power change can surface partly as a frequency change, so fixed-token ΔE must be read with per-arm GPU frequency printed (the BYTES rule already does this).
- Shumailov et al., 'Sponge Examples' (arXiv 2006.03463, opened via ar5iv): zero/sparsity-dependent energy on real GPUs (GTX 1080 Ti via NVML) was 'too small to observe reliably'; only 1-3% on a sparsity-exploiting ASIC simulator. Directly bears on the 'energy per zero operand' parameter: without hardware zero-skipping, the zero-operand term is at or below GPU noise floors.
- Yang, Chen, Sze, 'Designing Energy-Efficient CNNs using Energy-Aware Pruning' (CVPR 2017, arXiv 1611.05128, abstract opened only): predicts network energy from software-computable data-movement/sparsity statistics with coefficients 'extrapolated from actual hardware measurements' (Eyeriss). Precedent for 'predict energy of a weight change from stream statistics before measuring', but on a sparsity-aware accelerator, not a GPU/LPDDR system.
- Pekhimenko et al., 'Toggle-Aware Bandwidth Compression for GPUs' (HPCA 2016) — UNVERIFIED (PDF downloaded from the CMU author page but no local text extractor; IEEE page returned empty). From memory: defines bit toggles between consecutive flits on GPU DRAM/on-chip buses and shows compression raises toggle count and bus energy; the canonical 'energy per bus transition' treatment for GPU weight/data streams.
- Brier, Clavier, Olivier, 'Correlation Power Analysis with a Leakage Model' (CHES 2004) — UNVERIFIED (Springer paywall redirect). The W = a·HD(consecutive bus states) + b model is the standard side-channel leakage model; the proposed 'two-parameter toggle model' is this model plus a Hamming-weight/zero term.
- JouleWise in-repo: BYTES known-bytes stream test (docs/process_traces/2026-10-31-magistrate-5cbfbcfc/KB-SLOT-FILL.md; reading rule docs/registrations/bytes_known_bytes_reading_rule_DRAFT.md). Rotate-minus-repeat contrasts: GPU rail 0.00879 ± 0.00010 J/GB, rails 0.0092 ± 0.0021, DC 0.0389 ± 0.0072, rest 0.0296 ± 0.0084 J/GB in one hour (M3 Max, mx.sum on a uint8 payload). This measures memory-traffic energy per logical byte, independent of payload content; the toggle idea is the payload-content derivative of exactly this probe. No toggle/Hamming/zero-fraction question exists in docs/research_question_bank.md or the registry (closest is C5-1.12 quantization benefit decomposition).
- Hardware facts used (Wikipedia Apple M3 / Apple M4 / LPDDR pages, opened): M3 Max LPDDR5-6400, 32 x 16-bit channels (512-bit), 409.6 GB/s; M4 LPDDR5X-7500, 8 x 16-bit channels (128-bit), 120 GB/s; LPDDR4 has per-byte DMI Data Bus Inversion enable-able for reads and writes. LPDDR5/5X DBI (RDBI/WDBI, optional mode-register feature) — UNVERIFIED (JEDEC page 403; Wikipedia silent).
- Ed's reference 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights': could not be located or verified. Web search budget for this session was exhausted before the check; no primary source was opened. Treat as an unverified pointer, not a citation.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1) Magnitude vs. the floors. Use the repo's own anchors as the energy-per-bit scale: incremental traffic cost is 1.1 pJ/bit on the GPU rail (0.00879 J/GB) and about 4.9 pJ/bit at DC input (0.039 J/GB). The data-dependent part is dynamic ½CV² switching on the LPDDR DQ lines (0.5 V class I/O, on-package traces, order 0.1-0.3 pJ per transferred bit for random data) plus on-die fabric/L2 wire toggling (order 0.1-0.3 pJ/bit). So the maximum synthetic contrast (all-zero vs max-entropy stream) is roughly 0.3-0.6 pJ/bit = 0.002-0.005 J/GB total, of which perhaps 0.001-0.002 J/GB sits on the GPU rail. Against the GPU-rail half-width of 0.0001 J/GB that is a 10-20 sigma effect in a one-hour BYTES-style ladder: the synthetic ladder IS feasible. Against the 'rest' half-width of 0.0084 J/GB (where the DRAM-package share lands) it is below 1 sigma per hour; the LPDDR-chip toggle term needs 10-20 hours per rung or a DRAM rail that powermetrics does not expose (rails = cpu+gpu+ane only). 2) Real edits are undetectable by construction. A 4-bit affine stream (MLX: code = round((w-β)/s), codes 0-15, group 64) is already near max-entropy; a surgical edit (rank-one update, a few thousand to a few million weights) changes the stream's toggle density by well under 1e-4 relative. Even at the maximal 3% per-token sensitivity (16 GB/token x 0.004 J/GB over a ~2 J token), the predicted fixed-token ΔE for a real edit is about 1e-6 relative, i.e. ~0.01 J on a 2-4 kJ episode against a 2 J replay floor: 100-1000x below the floor. 'Validated on held-out real edits within its interval' is therefore vacuous: prediction and measurement are both 0 ± floor, and any model predicting ~0 also 'validates'. The positive control of 0.5% can only come from the synthetic ladder, not from edits; the honest deliverable is an upper bound on J/transition and J/zero plus an impossibility statement for surgical edits. 3) The 'zero operand' parameter is ill-posed for quantized weights. In MLX qmv_fast (quantized.h, opened) weights are never dequantized: y = scale·Σ(x·q) + Σx·bias, and there is no zero-skipping; a code 0 is the group minimum β, not 0, and a true zero weight has a per-group code round(-β/s). 'Zero fraction' of the stored nibbles and of the mathematical weights are different statistics with no fixed relation; the design must pick one, and Sponge Examples found the sparsity term unobservable on a GPU without zero-skipping hardware. 4) 'Hamming distance between consecutive words in the kernel's streaming order' is the wrong statistic. Physical transitions occur per bus lane between consecutive beats: on LPDDR5 each 16-bit channel delivers a 64 B burst as 16-32 beats, so the DQ-lane HD is between words 2 B apart within a line in address order, while on-die 64-128 B wide fabrics the HD is between words 64-128 B apart; 32 channels interleave independently; 32 simdgroup lanes load 8 B each per step in parallel, so 'consecutive in kernel order' is neither. Compute HD at several strides (2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128 B) and let the ladder identify the stride, or the regression coefficient has no physical meaning. 5) Data Bus Inversion. LPDDR4 has per-byte DBI (verified); LPDDR5 RDBI/WDBI is optional and whether Apple's controller enables it is unobservable (UNVERIFIED). DBI caps lane transitions (or ones) at half the lanes per beat and makes a 0x00/0xFF alternating pattern cost about as little as all-zeros, so the per-transition coefficient is a platform+firmware constant that will differ between M3 Max (LPDDR5) and M4 (LPDDR5X, 128-bit, 120 GB/s, different compute/memory balance). Cross-machine 'replication' should expect different coefficients, not the same ones; the design should say so up front. 6) Confounds in the synthetic ladder: all-zero or low-entropy payloads may also change ALU toggling in the reduction/multiply datapath (Hot Pixels shows ALU-operand HD effects of about 2.5% on M1), DVFS frequency (print per-arm GPU frequency and residency, as the BYTES rule does), and possibly any transparent compression paths; the two-parameter fit cannot separate interconnect toggles from ALU toggles without a stride-selective pattern set. 7) Integration time: Hot Pixels needed 400-800 s per data point to see ~0.3 W effects with 10 ms sensor samples; the repo's hour-scale A-B-B-A geometry is about right for the synthetic rungs but a 'desk-only, 1 day' cost is only true if S1/pilot arms are re-used; a proper stride/DBI-diagnostic ladder is one additional measurement night on the MBP.

**Residual novelty**

Not previously done anywhere found: a calibrated energy-per-transition and energy-per-zero coefficient for the LLM weight stream on Apple silicon, read on powermetrics rails and a DC meter, with the physically correct stride-resolved Hamming statistics. Reframe the experiment to keep what is novel and drop what is vacuous: (a) Synthetic ladder on the existing BYTES probe geometry (payload size fixed at the 32 MiB resolved rung, only content varied: all-zero, random, 0x55/0xAA alternating per byte, 0x00/0xFF alternating at 2 B / 64 B / 128 B strides). This gives per-boundary J/transition coefficients, a DBI on/off diagnostic for free (the 0x00/0xFF-at-2 B rung collapsing to the all-zero cost means DBI-AC is on), and an on-die vs. off-die split by stride. GPU-rail detectability is comfortable (10-20 sigma per hour); 'rest' is an upper bound only. (b) Replace 'held-out real edits' with output-preserving re-encodings as the positive control: with MLX affine quantization, q -> 15-q with s -> -s, β -> β+15s reproduces every weight bit-exactly, so logits and tokens are identical while every nibble's Hamming weight flips (4-HW) and consecutive-word HD is unchanged. Nibble/group permutations that the kernel tolerates give the complementary case (HD changed, HW unchanged). These isolate the two parameters with zero compute or token confound, which no real edit can do. (c) Publish the impossibility bound: convert the fitted coefficients into the minimum number of changed nibbles (or minimum change in stream entropy) that a weight edit must cause to move fixed-token energy by one replay floor (2 J on 2-4 kJ). The expected answer (edits must touch a sizable fraction of all weights, i.e. pruning/re-quantization, not surgical edits) is itself a useful negative result for Ed's interp question and directly answers 'is power a usable signal for surgical weight alteration' with a number. (d) Cross-machine: present M3 Max vs M4 as two independent calibrations with different expected coefficients (LPDDR5 vs LPDDR5X, 512- vs 128-bit bus), not as a replication of one value.

### Opus 5.5 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Gregersen, Patel, Choukse, 'Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs', arXiv:2409.18324 (SC24 poster), opened: on an A100, GEMM power changes by up to about 38-40% with value distribution, bit similarity, placement and sparsity alone. They hypothesize that bit flips cause it, but fit no regression and give no energy-per-flip figure. Sampling was DCGM at 100 ms. Not Apple, and it does not separate memory from compute.
- Horace He, 'Strangely, Matrix Multiplications on GPUs Run Faster When Given Predictable Data!' (thonking.ai), opened: on an A100 with a power cap, zeros or predictable data run faster than randn (295 vs 257 TFLOPS). The model is qualitative (power is about clock times flips per clock) and flips are inferred, not counted.
- Taneja, Kim, Xu, van Schaik, Genkin, Yarom, 'Hot Pixels: Frequency, Power, and Temperature Attacks on GPUs and ARM SoCs', arXiv:2305.12784 (USENIX Sec 2023), opened via ar5iv: on M1/M2 MacBook Air, Mac mini and MBP iGPUs, IOReport GPU power and frequency correlate with operand Hamming weight (r about -0.96) and Hamming distance (r about -0.97). No absolute watt deltas for HW/HD are given. This is the closest Apple-silicon evidence that GPU-rail telemetry sees operand bit statistics. Partly mediated by DVFS on fanless machines.
- Chawla et al., 'The Power of Telemetry: Uncovering Software-Based Side-Channel Attacks on Apple M1/M2 Systems', arXiv:2306.16391, opened: the SMC key PHPC leaks the Hamming weight of AES state, needing about 1M traces at about 1 s refresh. The IOReport PCPU energy channel showed NO data dependence, which the authors attribute to it being modeled or too coarse. This is a direct warning about powermetrics observability.
- Kogler et al., 'Collide+Power', USENIX Security 2023 (collidepower.com, opened): CPU power rises with the number of bits changed when one value overwrites another. This is the Hamming-distance leakage model on Intel, AMD and ARM, not Apple.
- Background, not opened (UNVERIFIED as cited): the classic Hamming-distance/Hamming-weight CMOS leakage models from the DPA/CPA literature (e.g., Brier-Clavier-Olivier CHES 2004), and Pekhimenko et al., 'Toggle-Aware Bandwidth Compression for GPUs' (HPCA 2016), which treats energy per bit toggle on GPU interconnect and DRAM. Both URLs I tried returned 404.
- In-repo anchor (docs/process_traces/2026-10-31-magistrate-5cbfbcfc/KB-SLOT-FILL.md, RECORD.md): the KB DRAM-versus-cache contrast (rotate minus repeat) was GPU rail 0.00879 ± 0.00010 J/GB, rails 0.0092 ± 0.0021, DC 0.0389 ± 0.0072, and rest (outside the rails) 0.0296 ± 0.0084 J/GB. Most of the energy of a DRAM byte lands outside the powermetrics rails.
- Ed's reference 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights': NOT FOUND. My web-search budget for the session ran out before I could search for it, so I can neither confirm nor deny that it exists. Treat it as unverified.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1. Validating on real surgical edits can't fail as designed. ROME/MEMIT-style edits touch roughly one matrix in one layer, under about 0.5% of weight bytes, and leave quantized codes looking random, so the toggle rate inside the edited block barely moves. Rough scale: changed-byte fraction (about 5e-3) × change in toggle rate (about 1e-2 or less) × toggle share of DRAM and fabric energy (about 0.1-0.3) × DRAM share of decode energy (about 0.2) puts the real-edit ΔE at about 1e-6 to 1e-5 of episode energy. The replay floor is about 2 J on 2-4 kJ, i.e. 5e-4 to 1e-3, 50 to 1000 times larger. The model will predict about 0 and measure about 0 within its interval, which proves nothing. Fix: make the held-out criterion falsifiable, with a measured-on-predicted slope whose CI excludes 0 across edits that span the predicted range. Get that range from large real edits (function-preserving neuron or head permutations, outlier clipping, heavy magnitude pruning, re-encoding), and report surgical edits as a bound, not a validation.
2. Where the energy lands versus what can resolve it. In the KB contrast, only 0.0088 of 0.039 J/GB of DRAM-access energy appeared on the GPU rail; about 0.030 J/GB was off-rail, visible only at DC with an hourly half-width of about ±0.007-0.008 J/GB. Toggles on the LPDDR bus and in the DRAM core therefore fall mainly where resolution is worst. A full-swing synthetic ladder (all-constant against random weight bytes) might move about 1-10 mJ/GB of DRAM-path energy, which is only about 1σ per DC hour. On-chip toggles (SLC, fabric, register file, ALU after dequantization) land on the GPU rail with ±0.0001 J/GB, which is ample. The 0.5% positive control is plausible for synthetic extremes: Gregersen saw up to 40% on a compute-bound A100, but decode is memory-bound, so expect a few percent, not tens. It is not plausible for realistic edits.
3. Observability of powermetrics. Chawla et al. found the IOReport PCPU energy channel blind to data, possibly because it is modeled. Hot Pixels found IOReport GPU power data-dependent on M1/M2, partly through DVFS on fanless Airs. On the M3 Max and M4 the GPU channel might be partly counter-based. Run a crude go/no-go first (all-zero against random-byte buffers streamed by the same kernel) and check that GPU-rail and DC deltas agree in sign before building any model.
4. 'Streaming order' is ill-defined for wires. The Hamming distance that matters depends on which words follow each other on each physical line: LPDDR5/5X burst beats within a cache line, channel and bank interleave, fabric arbitration across many SIMD groups (close to random in time), and register-file lane mapping. Apple's memory controller may also scramble data (Intel's does) or use DBI-DC, either of which would erase or fold bus Hamming statistics. Both are unknown and UNVERIFIED for Apple. A software statistic computed in 'kernel streaming order' may be a weak proxy. Compute several candidate orderings (intra-line beat, intra-lane k-loop, random) and let the ladder decide. That adds parameters.
5. The two parameters are confounded. Zero fraction, Hamming weight and Hamming distance are strongly collinear in naive ladders. The calibration needs an orthogonal design: alternating 0x55/0xAA (no zeros, maximum Hamming distance, mid Hamming weight), sorted against shuffled versions of the same multiset (Hamming distance moves, Hamming weight and zero fraction fixed), and constant nonzero (zero toggles, no zeros). In affine 4-bit and 8-bit formats a zero weight is not a zero code (it encodes as about -bias/scale), so 'zero operand' means the dequantized fp16 value at the ALU, while transitions happen on the packed-code bus. Those are two different physical sites, and the per-group scale and bias words are part of the stream too.
6. Confounds from activations and trajectories. Edits change activations downstream, and therefore datapath toggles, whatever the weight statistics. Fixed-token energy needs a teacher-forced, identical token sequence, plus a function-preserving control arm to isolate weight-stream effects. Data-dependent power can also move GPU clocks (Hot Pixels mechanism), which changes time per token. Pin High Power mode and report frequency and residency per arm.
7. Transfer between machines. The M3 Max and M4 differ in LPDDR generation, interleave and SLC size, so 'replicate' really means recalibrate, and the parameters should not be expected to carry over. Also, a model under about 100 MB, or edited blocks that fit in the SLC, will skip DRAM entirely.
8. The cost is understated. The ladders are new measurement arms, not desk work, unless S1 already contains orthogonal synthetic arms. Modelling the stream orderings for the MLX quantized-GEMV layout is about 2-3 days on its own. Proportionate plan: one go/no-go hour (point 3), then a 4-6 arm orthogonal ladder, then permutation edits as the held-out test, with surgical edits reported as an upper bound.

**Residual novelty**

Not refuted. The pieces are all established: operand-dependent power on GPUs, Hamming-weight and Hamming-distance leakage, and HW/HD correlation in Apple iGPU IOReport power. No source I opened calibrates a quantitative energy per bit transition or per zero operand on Apple unified memory for an LLM decode weight stream, or uses such a model to predict, before measuring, the fixed-token energy change of real weight edits. What would be new is that calibrated number per platform (M3 Max, M4) and per format, split into the on-chip (GPU rail) and off-rail (DC minus rails, mostly DRAM) parts, plus a predictive check on held-out edits. For Ed's interpretability question, the most valuable and most likely result is a calibrated upper bound: surgical edits change weight-stream bit statistics far too little to show up in power, so any measured power change after an edit comes from changed trajectories or token counts, not from the weights' bits. That bound is publishable and directly answers whether surgical edits change power. The strongest version of the test uses edits that preserve the function, so they cannot change behaviour. Permuting MLP hidden units or attention heads (sorted to minimize consecutive-word Hamming distance) leaves outputs identical and leaves zero fraction and Hamming weight unchanged while moving Hamming distance. Permutation as a toggle-energy lever for LLM weights on Apple silicon appears unexplored in what I could check, though my searching was cut short.

---

## 15. Blinded tamper-detection challenge (safety null with positive controls) (seat 2 (Opus))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Michscan (Paul & Zuzak, arXiv:2501.10174, opened abstract + HTML): black-box runtime integrity check of TinyML models on an STM32F303RC via a 10 Ω shunt sampled at 96 MS/s; detects BadNets retrain, last-layer poisoning, and a 4-parameter bit-flip edit with 100% detection / 0 false positives from 5 inferences (P<1e-5). Detection uses a 225 kHz band-passed trace correlated against a golden template (Mann-Whitney U), NOT total energy; explicitly cannot tell architecture changes from parameter changes. Shows that small weight edits ARE power-detectable when you have a single-task MCU, synchronised high-bandwidth traces and a per-device golden template; it does not speak to 1 Hz rail energy on a multitasking SoC.
- HAMLOCK (Amgain, Lobo, Chatterjee, Bhunia, Suya, arXiv:2510.19145, USENIX Security 2026; abstract opened; power-detection details from Help Net Security 2026-06-15, opened, secondary): a few-neuron weight tweak plus hardware Trojan; area/power overheads 'as low as 0.01%' and per the article about 1% on the VGG-16 accelerator and ~0 on two other designs, 'disappear into the normal swings of chip manufacturing'. Closest published analogue to the predicted null: tiny weight-level changes are not power-separable from unit-to-unit variation. ASIC accelerator setting, not LLM decode on Apple silicon.
- Energon (arXiv:2508.01768, ICCAD 2025, abstract opened): GPU power + thermal side channels recover transformer layer/head counts and model family (>89%, hyperparameters 100%) at user privilege. Supports the half of the claim that energy witnesses structure; says nothing about weight values or fine-tune variants of one architecture.
- Kraken (arXiv:2603.02891, SaTML 2026, abstract opened; full text not read): CPA with an architecture-specific energy model and near-field EM on Tensor Cores extracts weights, with exploratory far-field (100 cm through glass) LLM weight/hyperparameter leakage. Establishes that data-dependent switching energy of weights exists and is exploitable with many aligned high-bandwidth traces; the proposed design integrates that signal away to a scalar per episode, so Kraken is the reason the null is a measurement bound, not a physical impossibility.
- Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs (Gregersen, Patel, Choukse, arXiv:2409.18324, abstract opened; A100/CUTLASS/DCGM details from the search summary, UNVERIFIED): GEMM power varies by almost 40% with value distribution, bit similarity, placement and sparsity at fixed shapes, attributed to bit-flip counts; sparse inputs generally lower power. Directly relevant: the 'zeros' and 'outlier' classes are value-distribution changes, and this paper is the only quantitative anchor for how large such effects can be on a GPU (NVIDIA, compute-bound; no Apple-silicon or decode-regime data exists that I found).
- Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab, 'Weight-Space Programming' blog (rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/, 2026-11-02, opened; The Register 2026-11-03 opened): GPT-6 Astra edited 131,072 weights (0.016%) of Qwen3.5-0.8B by subtracting one direction from 64 output columns of the MLPs in layers 15 and 19; MATH-500 7.9%→48.1%, GSM8K 13.8%→67.1%, IFEval 21.0%→62.3%, loop-to-limit 93%→33%; authors call it 'very early' and a Gemma 3 270M control showed no gain ('could remove an obstacle that hid an ability, but it could not add an ability the model lacked'). Ed's reference exists. Note the edit is a direction-subtraction, physically the same class as refusal-direction orthogonalization: same shapes, same bytes, values nudged in two matrices. It is the archetype of the surgical class and makes a 'fixed-token' null uninteresting on its own, because the whole point of the edit is that it changes WHICH tokens are generated (fewer loops), i.e. the work; the energy change under free decode is large and trivially explained by token count.
- JouleWise internal (origin/desk/2026-11-01-align-pilot-v0 docs/align/budget_pilot.md l.5 and runner.md l.4): 'Power counts work; it cannot reveal what one forward pass computes.' Council files (docs/align/council-2026-11-05/*, council-2026-11-06-v4-review/opus-stats.md) give the replay floor the idea leans on: |ΔE_GPU| ≤ 3 × max(2.97 J, 0.0011 × E) for identical-work replays, K-session σ_w 4.7-5.1 J on ~2.7 kJ (0.18%). KB3 RECORD (docs/process_traces/2026-10-31-magistrate-5cbfbcfc/RECORD.md) gives the lock-in anchor: GPU rail Δ 0.00879 ± 0.00010 J/GB over a 3,559 s hour. No JouleWise question-bank entry covers tamper detection or weight edits (grep of research_question_bank.md / registry for tamper|fingerprint|weight edit|backdoor: none).
- Web search budget was exhausted after four searches; a planned search for LLM-scale runtime integrity via power/performance counters was not run. Not opened (UNVERIFIED): thonking.ai 'matmuls run faster on predictable data', the secondary Astra coverage (YourStory, FourWeekMBA, emergent.sh, zeli), Energon and Kraken full texts.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

Not refuted: nobody has measured whether weight-value-only edits move LLM inference energy on Apple silicon at rail/DC resolution, and the two closest results point opposite ways (Michscan detects 4-parameter edits with trace-shape CPA on an MCU; HAMLOCK's ≤1% hardware-level tweaks vanish in device variation). But the proposal as written has several physics and design problems.

1. The surgical null is a measurement bound, not a theorem. Fixed-token (teacher-forced) replay holds FLOPs, bytes streamed and kernel schedule constant, so the only remaining channel is data-dependent switching energy (bit toggles in the MAC datapath, register files, fabric and LPDDR bus). Kraken and Michscan show that channel carries weight information; Gregersen et al. show it can be ~40% of GEMM power between extreme value distributions on an A100. A rank-one or direction-subtraction edit on two matrices changes the toggle statistics of ~1% of the weight stream by a random-looking perturbation, so an effect of ~1e-6 to 1e-5 relative is the right order of magnitude; orthogonalization touches every residual-writing matrix but again leaves codes random-like, so ~1e-5 is plausible. Downstream activations also change (that is the point of the edit), but activation bit statistics stay pseudo-random. Prediction of ≤1e-5 is reasonable; what the experiment can actually show is 'below ~4e-4 to 1e-3 per copy' (2-3 J on 2-4 kJ), i.e. a one-to-two-decade gap between the predicted effect and the demonstrable bound. State the result as an equivalence bound, not as 'energy is not a fingerprint'.

2. The predicted floor is tighter than the kit's own. Replay repeatability is 2.97 J on the GPU rail (0.11% of 2.7 kJ) and K-session σ_w 4.7-5.1 J (0.18%); with 3 copies per class a 90% equivalence CI half-width is ~2.9 × 0.1-0.18% / √3 ≈ 0.17-0.3%, above the 0.04-0.1% margin quoted. Either use ≥8 replays per copy or run the lock-in variant (both copies resident in unified memory, alternate ABAB on identical tokens within one process; a 17 GB 4-bit 30B pair fits trivially, an 80B-A3B pair at 90 GB fits tightly). The KB3 lock-in resolved 0.0001 J/GB on a 0.009 J/GB contrast in one hour, so a lock-in design could push the bound to ~1e-4 relative, but only if the alternation is between resident copies, not reloads.

3. 'Positive controls expected at ≥1%' is unsupported for two of the three. (a) 2% outlier injection in fp16 changes neither bytes, structure nor zeros; its only channel is exponent/mantissa toggle statistics, and there is no Apple-silicon data saying that is 1%. In 4-bit group-quantized weights, outliers blow the group scale and collapse neighbours to zero codes, so it becomes a 'zeros' class by accident. (b) 10% head removal implemented by zeroing is also a pure data-dependent-power question (Apple GPU zero-operand gating is uncharacterised); implemented by actually pruning (shape change, config change, GQA-group constraints) it is a bytes+FLOPs change and will show at several percent. Decide which it is; a design needs at least one guaranteed positive control (layer drop, 8-bit vs 4-bit copy, or a changed token budget) and should treat zeros and outliers as hypotheses to be measured, which is where the real novelty lies.

4. Artifact-format leakage would make the surgical classes trivially detectable for the wrong reason. Rank-one edits and LoRA fusion on quantized models require dequantize → edit → requantize; if the blinder leaves the edited matrices in fp16, bytes quadruple for those tensors and the kernel switches from quantized to fp16 matmul. Pre-register that every copy has byte-identical shapes, dtypes, quantization config and total size, with only hashes differing; otherwise the test is of MLX kernel selection, not of surgery.

5. MoE routing (Qwen3-30B-A3B) is a hidden structural channel: an edit that moves router logits changes which experts are fetched; bytes per token are unchanged in count but the fetched tensors differ, and with the episode_prefix cache the prefill replay path is identical anyway. Expected ~0 but worth a logged router-agreement rate so an unexpected positive can be attributed.

6. Blinding and ordering: classes must be interleaved and randomised across the night (not blocked), copies loaded from the same SSD with page-cache state controlled or load excluded from the owned window, thermal start gate applied per copy. With 18 copies the ALIGN member law (516 s + 1.135 × episode) makes 18 × 8 × ~630 s ≈ 25 h, so 'about 0.5 night' needs a leaner replay harness (no 300 s idle per member), or fewer copies. 'Rides on Stage 3' is ambiguous: ALIGN's Stage 3 is a build stage (sidecar, gates, kit v4), not a measurement night; BYTES stages are packs with their own blinding. Also the edit preparation itself is not free on the Mac: ROME-style covariance statistics and a LoRA backdoor on a 30B need hours of forward/training passes before the night; refusal orthogonalization is cheap.

7. The fixed-token framing answers a question nobody is asking about Astra-style edits. The Astra edit's observable effect is fewer loop tokens (93%→33% hitting the limit), so under free decode its energy change is enormous and purely 'work'. The interesting interp-adjacent question for Ed's intersection (surgical edits vs power AND performance) is the free-decode decomposition: ΔE = ΔE_tokens (counted) + ΔE_per-token (this experiment's residual). The design should report both arms so the null on the second term is read next to the large first term, which is the 'power counts work' statement made quantitative.

**Residual novelty**

Genuinely new if run as an equivalence experiment: (a) the first blinded, pre-registered bound (target ~1e-3 by replay, ~1e-4 by resident-copy lock-in) on how much value-only weight surgery (direction subtraction/orthogonalization, rank-one edit, fused LoRA) moves fixed-token LLM inference energy on Apple silicon, where no data exist; (b) the first measurement of data-dependent decode power on an M-series GPU for LLM weights (zeros fraction and outlier distribution at fixed shapes), the Apple-side counterpart to Gregersen et al.'s 40% A100 GEMM range and the quantity that determines whether 'zeros' is ever an energy witness; (c) a clean decomposition of Astra-class edits into token-count energy versus per-token energy, which is the concrete form of 'power counts work; it is blind to what a token says'. Reframe: drop 'energy is not a fingerprint of tampering' as the headline (HAMLOCK already argues it, and the design can only bound, not prove it), keep the positive-control classes as measured hypotheses rather than assumed positives, and bank the hash-is-the-right-tool point as a one-line conclusion rather than a finding.

### Opus 5.5 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- OPENED: Gregersen, Patel, Choukse, 'Input-Dependent Power Usage in GPUs', arXiv:2409.18324 (2024). Keeping GEMM shapes fixed and changing only the operand values changes GPU power by up to about 38-40% on A100, and less on Quadro RTX 6000. The effect comes from value distribution, bit similarity, placement/sorting and sparsity, and the authors suggest bit flips as the cause. They changed both operands, not weights alone, and list weight-only changes as future work. This is the main reason the surgical null cannot be assumed on physics grounds alone. It is also the main reason the 'zeros' and 'outlier' classes might show up.
- OPENED: Cina, Demontis, Biggio, Roli, Pelillo, 'Energy-Latency Attacks via Sponge Poisoning', arXiv:2203.08147, Information Sciences 702 (2025). Poisoned weights raise energy and latency for every input while accuracy stays intact. The mechanism is lower activation sparsity on sparsity-exploiting accelerators. Weight tampering can therefore change energy, but only on hardware that skips zeros. Dense MLX kernels and SiLU/GELU LLMs do not use activation sparsity, so this channel mostly does not apply on the Mac.
- OPENED: Batina, Bhasin, Jap, Picek, 'CSI NN: Reverse Engineering of Neural Network Architectures Through Electromagnetic Side Channel', USENIX Security 2019. Recovers layer count, neuron count, activations and weights from timing and EM traces on an ARM Cortex-M3. Structure being visible through physical side channels is already known. That needs high-bandwidth per-operation traces on a simple device, not integrated ms-scale rail energy on an SoC.
- OPENED: Takehi, Teoh, Zhang, Khattab (MIT CSAIL), 'Weight-Space Programming' blog post, 2026-11-02, rikiyat.github.io/blog/weight-space-programming/. This is the source of Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights' reference, and it exists. On Qwen3.5-0.8B, Astra removed one direction written by the MLPs of layers 15 and 19 (64 output columns each, 131,072 weights, 0.016% of the model). MATH-500 went from 7.9% to 48.1%, and the share of answers hitting the token cap fell from 93% to 33.1%. The post reports no energy or power measurements. Secondary coverage opened: zeli.app/story/50341174. A WebSearch result also listed theregister, yourstory, fourweekmba, letsdatascience, emergent.sh and buildmvpfast; buildmvpfast returned 403 and none of the others was opened.
- UNVERIFIED (not opened; the session's search budget ran out): Arditi et al. 2024, refusal-direction orthogonalization; Meng et al. 2022, ROME rank-one editing; Shumailov et al. 2021, 'Sponge Examples'; power-side-channel DNN architecture recovery on FPGAs/GPUs ('Open DNN Box by Power Side-Channel Attack', DeepSniffer, Hermes). None was checked for an energy-based tamper-detection result. I could not search for prior work on 'fingerprinting LLM weight edits from GPU energy'. The absence of such work is therefore not established, only not found.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**

1) The surgical-class null is close to predictable from first principles. Rank-one edits, refusal orthogonalization and merged LoRA keep every shape, kernel, FLOP and byte the same. Under a fixed-token replay, only bit-toggle activity changes. The edits are small dense low-rank perturbations, so the weight bit statistics barely move, and the 1e-5-or-below prediction is plausible against a 4e-4 to 1e-3 floor (2 J on 2-4 kJ). A blinded six-class study spends most of its effort confirming something a cheaper check can bound.

2) Gregersen et al. show that GPU power is not data-oblivious (up to about 38% on A100 from operand values alone), and Apple-GPU sensitivity is unmeasured. The missing number is the Apple GPU's toggle sensitivity to weight-value statistics. Measure that first with a GEMM or decode microbenchmark at fixed shapes: random weights vs sorted vs 50% zeroed vs outlier-laden vs low-entropy. The GPU rail's resolution (about 1e-4 J/GB) suits this. If even drastic value changes give under 0.1%, the surgical null follows a fortiori and the blinded study is overkill (proportionate-care). If they give several percent, the interesting deliverable becomes a dose-response curve, not a binary null.

3) The positive controls are confounded or trivial as written. '10% head removal' is a structural change (smaller shapes, about 10% of attention params, so roughly 2-4% of decode bytes) only if heads are actually pruned. If they are zeroed in place, it is a zeros class and may be null on a dense Apple GPU, which does not skip zeros. 'Bytes' controls (for example a different quantization) are trivially detectable and say nothing about the detection threshold. Use graded controls (for example zero 0.1/1/10/50% of weights at fixed shapes; prune 1/5/10% of heads) to locate a minimum detectable tamper.

4) Quantization trap. If the Mac runs MLX 4-bit group-quantized weights, edits must go in before requantization. '2% outlier injection' then reshapes group scales: with group size 64, about 1-0.98^64, or roughly 73%, of groups contain an outlier, so most codes in those groups collapse toward zero. That is a massive zeroing, not a subtle edit. Even a rank-one edit rewrites many 4-bit codes across the whole matrix. State whether the model is bf16 or quantized.

5) Statistics. With 3 copies per class (18 copies plus a reference), 'classifies at chance' cannot be told apart from weak above-chance detection. A null must be an equivalence bound: per-class paired ΔE/E with a 95% CI inside a pre-registered margin (TOST), not classifier accuracy. Use randomized interleaved ABAB order against the reference, because model-load and page-cache warmth plus thermal drift can easily exceed 1e-5 and create false positives.

6) Scope of the claim. Fixed-token design deliberately removes the channel through which edits actually move energy: output length. Astra's edit cut token-cap hits from 93% to 33.1%, which would change per-task energy by tens of percent. 'Energy is not a fingerprint of tampering' must be limited to per-token energy at fixed tokens. Under free generation, behavior-changing edits are plainly visible in energy, through token counts rather than watts. That intersection (an edit changes performance and energy together, via length) is closer to Ed's stated interest than the tamper null.

7) Threat model. An auditor with powermetrics rail access on the host can almost always hash the weights. The only coherent no-weights case is a sealed appliance with an external DC-input meter, so the floor that matters is the meter's, not the rails'. The design should report both.

8) Logistics. 18 copies of an 8B model at about 4.5 GB each come to about 80 GB of disk (ENOSPC risk per the disk-guard memory). Have the blinder apply edits at load time from sealed seeds or diffs instead of storing copies. 0.5 night is feasible only with short replays and a few repeats per copy.

**Residual novelty**

Not refuted: no opened source tests blinded classification of surgical LLM weight edits from fixed-token energy on Apple silicon, or on any platform. A literature search was not possible: the search budget ran out after one query, so this rests on four opened sources and several unverified ones. Three questions are adjacent but already answered. Operand values move GPU power (Gregersen et al.). Weight poisoning can raise energy on sparsity-exploiting hardware (sponge poisoning). Physical side channels reveal architecture on simple devices (CSI NN).

The new part is narrow. First, a measured upper bound on how sensitive Apple-silicon per-token energy is to weight-value statistics at fixed shapes; Gregersen-style data dependence has not been characterized on M-series GPUs or for weight-only changes, which that paper lists as future work. Second, the resulting minimum detectable tamper.

The surgical-class null itself has low novelty because physics nearly predicts it. Recommended reshaping: (a) a cheap rail-level weight-statistics microbenchmark (sorted, zeroed, outlier, low-entropy vs random at fixed shapes) as the gate; (b) a graded dose-response for zeros and pruning, reported as equivalence bounds instead of chance-level classifier accuracy; (c) a free-generation arm on an Astra-style direction-removal edit, showing that edits move energy mainly through token count. Arm (c) speaks directly to Ed's edits-change-power-and-performance interest and is likely the more publishable result. Bank the blinded six-class challenge unless (a) shows a non-trivial toggle sensitivity.

Ed's 'GPT-6 Astra' reference was found and opened: MIT CSAIL, 'Weight-Space Programming', 2026-11-02. It reports no energy data.

---

## 16. Unlogged self-modification leaves an energy signature (ALIGN extension) (seat 2 (Opus))

### Fable 5.1 checker: not refuted

**Prior work**

- Zweiger, Pari, Guo, Akyürek, Kim, Agrawal, 'Self-Adapting Language Models' (SEAL), arXiv:2506.10943, 2025 — OPENED (abs + HTML v2). Self-edits applied by LoRA SFT: Qwen2.5-7B (rank 64/alpha 128, batch 10, 2 epochs, lr 3e-4; per-edit search over 1-20 epochs) and Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct (rank 16, up to 375 training steps). No energy or power measurement anywhere. Relevant as the dose recipe, not as prior detection work.
- Meng, Bau, Andonian, Belinkov, 'Locating and Editing Factual Associations in GPT' (ROME), arXiv:2202.05262, NeurIPS 2022 — OPENED (abs + rome.baulab.info). Rank-one closed-form edit to one mid-layer MLP on GPT-2 XL / GPT-J; compute per edit is tiny (the v* optimisation step count was not on the opened pages: UNVERIFIED). Relevant as the cheapest-edit adversary the proposal does not cover.
- Clark, Ransford, Rahmati, Guineau, Sorber, Xu, Fu, 'WattsUpDoc: Power Side Channels to Nonintrusively Discover Untargeted Malware on Embedded Medical Devices', USENIX HealthTech 2013 — OPENED. Power draw as a run-time monitor for unauthorised computation (94% known / 85% unknown malware). Establishes the genre 'power as a tamper-evident record of what a machine ran'; nothing on LLMs, agents or training.
- Wei, Luo, Li, Liu, Xu, 'I Know What You See: Power Side-Channel Attack on CNN Accelerators', arXiv:1803.05847 (ACSAC 2018) — OPENED. Recovers CNN inputs from FPGA power traces. Shows power carries fine workload structure, not just totals.
- Shavit, 'What does it take to catch a Chinchilla? Verifying Rules on Large-Scale NN Training via Compute Monitoring', arXiv:2303.11341, 2023 — OPENED. Detecting unlogged training via firmware weight snapshots, run logs and chip supply-chain tracking; power/energy not used as the signal.
- Wasil, Reed, Miller, Barnett, 'Verification methods for international AI agreements', arXiv:2408.16074, 2024 — OPENED (HTML v2). 'Energy monitoring' is method 3 of 10: convert facility energy to FLOPs; explicitly 'coarse-grained ... only large-scale violations', 'plausible in theory ... unproven in practice'. Closest conceptual precedent for 'unlogged training leaves an energy signature', at facility scale; nothing at device/episode scale.
- Jia et al., 'Proof-of-Learning: Definitions and Practice', arXiv:2103.05633, IEEE S&P 2021 — OPENED. Verifies that parameters came from expended training compute via SGD trajectory evidence; no energy.
- Luccioni, Jernite, Strubell, 'Power Hungry Processing', arXiv:2311.16863, FAccT 2024 — OPENED (abs only). Inference energy per 1,000 queries; no per-token training-vs-inference figures on the abstract page.
- UNVERIFIED (not opened; web search budget exhausted this session): Batina et al. 'CSI NN' (USENIX Security 2019, EM side channel recovering NN architecture); 'Hidden in Plain Sight? On the Efficacy of Power Side Channel-Based Control Flow Monitoring' (USENIX Security 2024); Kaplan et al. 2020 for the C≈6ND training-FLOP rule used in my estimates; the full-text ROME v* step count; any mlx-lm LoRA energy measurement on Apple silicon.
- Ed's reference 'GPT-6 Astra can natively edit model weights': NOT FOUND. openai.com returned 403, web search was unavailable, and the only repo occurrences of 'Astra' are the model id `gpt-6-astra` used as a council seat (docs/decision_log.md D-184). Treat the premise as unverified.
- In-repo: no ALIGN, Block-6 or research-audit document on any branch discusses weight edits, fine-tuning or training compute as a hidden-work class (git grep over docs/align, docs/research*, docs/design/block6*). E2 (research-program.md:249) already plans real injected unlogged decode compute at 0/256/512/1,024 tokens; this proposal is E2 with a different load generator.

**Physics or feasibility concerns**
